# Project: the creation and Eden companions → Hebrew

Read `../../translation-guidelines.md` first, then `../corpus.md`, `../renderings.md` (corpus, binding; it inherits the flood corpus's shared renderings), `renderings.md` (this project), then this file.

## Scope
Seven short texts that, with Enūma eliš (project 1), make up the Mesopotamian evidence for **Genesis 2–3** (and Gen 1:26–28): man from clay or from the ground, the "not yet" chain, the work of the garden, the food of life refused, Dilmun, the forbidden plants and the curse, the rib, primeval humans without bread or clothing. From item 2 of `../../curated-translation-candidates.md`; relevance in `../../curation-surveys/relevance-survey--2026-10-16.md` (§A 2–7, 13); availability in `../../curation-surveys/availability-survey--2026-10-16.md`.

**One project, one pair of files per text.** Each text has its own `annotated-<N>-<Name>.rtl.md` and `translation-<N>-<Name>.rtl.md`, and its own section below (scope, sources, decisions, open decisions, progress log, parallels index, proposals). `translation.rtl.md` (all seven) is **generated** by `build_translation.py`; `verify.py` checks every text.

| N | Text | Language | Base text (rank 1) | Lines | Files |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Adapa** (and the South Wind): MB (Amarna) + SB (Nineveh) | Akkadian | eBL `L/1/7` (Mitto; transl. Foster): `../../sources/eBL/Adapa-MB/`, `Adapa-SB/` | 78 + 97 | `annotated-1-Adapa.rtl.md` |
| 2 | **Enki and Ninḫursaĝa** (the Dilmun myth), with the Ur version | Sumerian | ETCSL 1.1.1 (`../../sources/ETCSL/1.1.1--Enki-and-Ninhursaga/`); ORACC Q000332 + P346086 (Ur) for lemmas and morph | 305 | `annotated-2-Enki-Ninhursag.rtl.md` |
| 3 | **Enki and Ninmaḫ** | Sumerian | ETCSL 1.1.2; ORACC Q000333 | 142 | `annotated-3-Enki-Ninmah.rtl.md` |
| 4 | **The Song of the Hoe** | Sumerian | ETCSL 5.5.4; ORACC Q000778 | 110 | `annotated-4-Hoe.rtl.md` |
| 5 | **Grain and Sheep** (the debate; cosmogonic prologue 1–42) | Sumerian | ETCSL 5.3.2 (ETCSL's TEI lemmatisation; ORACC has no text) | 193 | `annotated-5-Grain-Sheep.rtl.md` |
| 6 | **The bilingual "Creation of the World by Marduk"** ("Founding of Eridu", CT 13 35–38 = BM 93014) | Sumerian–Akkadian | eBL fragmentarium transliteration of BM 93014 (`../../sources/eBL-fragmentarium/atf/BM.93014.atf`); King 1902 I 130–139 (rank 5) | obv. 40 + rev. 27′ | `annotated-6-Founding-of-Eridu.rtl.md` |
| 7 | **KAR 4** ("When heaven had been separated from earth": man from the Alla gods' blood) | Sumerian–Akkadian | SEAL 1600 (`../../sources/SEAL/KAR-4/`), checked against eBL's VAT 9307 and K 4175 transliterations | ≈ 75 composite lines | `annotated-7-KAR-4.rtl.md` |

## Line numbering
Each text keeps its base edition's numbering:
- Adapa: eBL, with the recension as prefix: `MB 1'` … `MB 78'` (eBL primes the Amarna tablet's lines), `SB a+1` … `SB f+10` (eBL numbers the six NA fragment groups a–f by their first preserved line: a+1–24, b+1–13, c+1–19, d+1–9, e+1–22, f+1–10). The older literature cites the Amarna tablet as fragment B, lines 1–71 (Izre'el 2001), and the Nineveh pieces as fragments A, C, D: the apparatus gives the old number where a parallel depends on it.
- Sumerian texts: ETCSL composite numbers (with ETCSL's letters, e.g. `9A`). The Ur manuscript of Enki and Ninḫursaĝa (ORACC P346086) by its column and line, with the concordance to the composite.
- Founding of Eridu: tablet lines `obv. 1` … `obv. 40`, `rev. 1'` … (eBL ATF = King's numbering for the obverse).
- KAR 4: SEAL's line numbers (= Pettinato / Lambert 2013 numbering of the composite; each line has a Sumerian and an Akkadian half).

## Parallels with Genesis to watch for (preliminary; checked in each text's index)
The relevant commentary files: `../../../../פירוש/1-בראשית/1020-בראשית-ב_ד-ב_יז-גן_עדן.rtl.md` (Gen 2:4–17: `שִׂיחַ הַשָּׂדֶה`, `עֵשֶׂב הַשָּׂדֶה`, `וְאָדָם אַיִן לַעֲבֹד אֶת הָאֲדָמָה`, `וְאֵד יַעֲלֶה`, `עָפָר מִן הָאֲדָמָה`, `נִשְׁמַת חַיִּים`, `גַּן בְּעֵדֶן מִקֶּדֶם`, the four rivers, `עֵץ הַחַיִּים`, `עֵץ הַדַּעַת`, `לְעָבְדָהּ וּלְשָׁמְרָהּ`, `מוֹת תָּמוּת`), `1030-בראשית-ב_יח-ג_כד-אדם_ואשה.rtl.md` (the woman from the rib, the naming of the animals, the snake, the eating, the garments, the curses, `בְּזֵעַת אַפֶּיךָ תֹּאכַל לֶחֶם`, `עָפָר אַתָּה`, `אֵם כָּל חָי`, `כָּתְנוֹת עוֹר`, `פֶּן יִשְׁלַח יָדוֹ … וָחַי לְעֹלָם`), and for Enki and Ninmaḫ `../../../../פירוש/2-שמות/2040-שמות-ב_כג-ד_יז-משה_והסנה.rtl.md` (Ex 4:10–11) and `../../../../פירוש/3-ויקרא/3480-ויקרא-כא_טז-כא_כד-פסילת_כהן_בעל_מום.rtl.md` (Lev 21:16–24).
- **Adapa:** the sage given wisdom but not eternal life; the bread and water "of death" that are of life, refused on a god's warning ↔ 2:17, 3:3–5, 3:22–24; the garment and the oil ↔ 3:21; Anu's laughter; the gatekeepers Dumuzi and Gizzida ↔ the cherubim (3:24)?; the breaking of the South Wind's wing by a word (the conjurer).
- **Enki and Ninḫursaĝa:** Dilmun pure, without predators, illness or old age ↔ 2:8–17 (and Isa 11:6–9, 65:25); fresh water brought up from the earth ↔ 2:6, 2:10; the eight plants eaten and named ↔ 2:16–17, 3:6, 2:19–20; the curse "until he dies I shall not look at him with the eye of life" ↔ 3:14–19; the eight healing deities, Nin-ti "lady of the rib / of life" ↔ 2:21–22, 3:20 (debated); birth in nine days ↔ 3:16 (weak).
- **Enki and Ninmaḫ:** the gods' toil and complaint; man formed from the clay of the Abzu to bear the basket ↔ 2:5, 2:7, 2:15; Namma as mother; the six defective humans and the fates/livelihoods decreed for them ↔ **Ex 4:11** (`מִי שָׂם פֶּה לָאָדָם אוֹ מִי יָשׂוּם אִלֵּם אוֹ חֵרֵשׁ אוֹ פִקֵּחַ אוֹ עִוֵּר`) and **Lev 21:17–23** (the priest with a blemish, who may eat but not approach); the barren woman ↔ 11:30 etc.; Umul.
- **Song of the Hoe:** heaven separated from earth (↔ 1:6–8, *never* `הִבְדִּיל`); humans breaking through the ground like plants at Uzumua ↔ 2:7, 3:19, 3:23; the hoe and the brick mould.
- **Grain and Sheep:** "not yet" chain ↔ 2:5; primeval humans without bread or clothes, eating grass like sheep, drinking ditch water ↔ 1:29–30, 2:16, 2:25, 3:7, 3:18–19, 3:21, 3:23, 4:2 (Abel and Cain), 9:3; grain and sheep created by the gods and sent down.
- **Founding of Eridu:** the chain of "not yet" ↔ 2:4b–5; "all the lands were sea" ↔ 1:2, 1:9; the reed raft on the face of the waters and the dust poured beside it ↔ 1:2 (`עַל פְּנֵי הַמָּיִם`), 2:7 (`עָפָר`); mankind created with Aruru, then cattle, Tigris and Euphrates, grass, reeds ↔ 2:7–14, 2:19; cities ↔ 4:17, 10:10, 11.
- **KAR 4:** heaven separated from earth; the gods' council "what shall we make?" ↔ 1:26 `נַעֲשֶׂה`; humans from the blood of slain Alla gods ↔ Enūma eliš VI, Atra-ḫasīs I; the first humans named (Ullegarra and Annegarra) and set to work the fields "for ever" ↔ 2:5, 2:15, 3:23.

## Decisions (coordinator, before drafting)
1. **Corpus renderings** (`../renderings.md`, which inherits `../../סיפורי-המבול-המסופוטמיים/renderings.md`) bind every text; the Sumerian conventions of the flood corpus's project 3 (`../../סיפורי-המבול-המסופוטמיים/3-סיפור-המבול-השומרי--זיוסודרה/`) bind the four Sumerian texts. This project's `renderings.md` adds the terms of Gen 2–3 that recur across the seven texts, fixed **before** drafting, and every translator extends it.
2. **Sequential drafting, one translator at a time**, in the order 1 → 7; each later translator receives the Hebrew of the cross-text terms fixed by the earlier ones (`renderings.md`), and the coordinator reviews each text before the next starts.
3. **Section headings:** follow the edition's sections where it has them (eBL `isBeginningOfSection`; ETCSL translation paragraphs), and add descriptive Hebrew headings (`## 1–28: …`) in both files.
4. **Sumerian aspect** (flood project 3, Decisions 1): the ORACC `morph` field decides ḫamṭu / marû; narrative ḫamṭu → Hebrew past; narrative marû → Hebrew participle by default (noted); *ga-* → cohortative; *ḫe₂-* → jussive.
5. **Bilinguals (6, 7):** translate the **Akkadian** line where it survives; the Sumerian goes into the apparatus, and where it says something different, the Hebrew line notes it (`{שומרית: …}` is not used; differences are in the apparatus, and the clean translation gives a footnote only when the difference matters for the comparison). Where only the Sumerian survives, translate the Sumerian and say so in the line's apparatus.
6. **Adapa:** MB (the Amarna tablet, the fullest version of the story) and SB (the Nineveh fragments) are translated as two sections of one file, each line from its own manuscript(s); where they overlap, identical Akkadian gets identical Hebrew.

## Open decisions for the user
The per-text lists are in each section below; the coordinator's summary of the most important ones is in "Coordinator: status, harmonization and open decisions" at the end of the file.

## Working rules for the text translators
- Follow guidelines §5 step 5 for **every line**: read the edition (eBL `reading-edition.md`; ETCSL `transliteration.md` and the TEI XML; ORACC `*--lemmatised-morph.md`; SEAL `.md`), parse every word (Akkadian: lemma, stem, tense, ventive, subjunctive, *-ma*; Sumerian: lemma, the verbal chain from the ORACC `morph` field, ḫamṭu/marû, case markers); form your own rendering; **only then** read the rank-3 translation (eBL Foster; ETCSL; SEAL) and record disagreements; run the echo check (§6.4) with the tags.
- Format exactly as `../1-אנומה-אליש/annotated-VI.rtl.md` and guidelines §7.2: a Hebrew header block (base text, numbering, signs, comparison translations, tags, reserved words), `## <range>: <Hebrew heading>`, then per line `### <label>`, the original in backticks, `> ` Hebrew line, `- **פירוק:**`, `- **תרגומים:**`, `- **הקבלה:**` (when relevant), `- **הערות:**` (when relevant). The apparatus is in Hebrew. Labels exactly as in "Line numbering" above.
- Draft unvocalized, plene; vocalize names at first occurrence in the text and ambiguous words (guidelines §5, §6.2).
- `translation-<N>-<Name>.rtl.md`: `# <Hebrew title>`, a short Hebrew introduction (what the text is, date, manuscripts, edition, how to read the signs), `##` section headings, numbered lines `**<label>** <Hebrew>` followed by two spaces, footnotes `[^n]` only for the major cruxes and key parallels. Its numbered lines must equal the annotated `>` lines (`python3 -I verify.py <N>`).
- Run `python3 -I ../../tools/check_bible_quotes.py <files>` and fix every mismatch. Quote the Bible with the vocalization of `תנך-מנוקד/` (the repository's verse division can differ: check with `--show`).
- Record in this file, under the text's section: scope, sources consulted, decisions, open decisions for the user, progress log (including the three review passes), the **parallels index** (table with exactly these columns: `| Line | Original | Hebrew rendering | Bible | Tag | Comment |`), proposals for the shared renderings, lessons for the guidelines.
- Do not edit `../renderings.md`, `../parallels-index.md`, `../corpus.md` or the guidelines: proposals go into this file; the coordinator merges them. You may extend this project's `renderings.md` (add rows; do not change rows fixed by an earlier text without a note in its Changelog).

## Text 1: Adapa

### Scope
- **Text:** *Adapa (and the South Wind)*, Akkadian, both recensions in eBL `L/1/7`: **MB** (the Amarna tablet VAT 348 = EA 356, 14th cent. BCE; 78 lines `MB 1'`–`MB 78'`, the fullest version of the story; beginning lost) and **SB** (five Neo-Assyrian pieces, 7th cent.: MLC 1296, K 15072, K 8743, K 8214, K 9994; 97 lines in six groups `SB a+1` … `SB f+10`). **175 entries, all translated** (lines of which only traces survive have `…`/`[…]` entries).
- **Files:** `annotated-1-Adapa.rtl.md` (Hebrew header; per line: eBL normalization, Hebrew, parsing, Foster + our departures, parallels with tags, notes with the old line number), `translation-1-Adapa.rtl.md` (introduction, 15 sections, 8 footnotes). Both were generated from line-by-line data files by a small builder script (session scratchpad; not kept in the repo), which copies eBL's normalization and Foster's translation verbatim and computes the old line numbers from eBL's ATF.
- **Sections:** MB follows eBL's section breaks (`---` before 7', 15', 39', 53', 62', 69', 78'); SB has none in eBL, so its sections (a–f) follow eBL's fragment groups, with plot headings.
- **Old numbering (concordance):** the older literature cites the Amarna tablet as "fragment B", lines 1–71 = the tablet's own lines (obverse 1–36, reverse 37–71), which is also SEAL's numbering; eBL's notes cite it that way too (e.g. the note on MB 58' cites Izre'el "ad 51'" = tablet r 15). Every MB entry gives it: the warning = B 29–32, the bread of life = B 60–65, Anu's laughter = B 66, "you shall not live" = B 68, "return him to his earth" = B 70. For SB, every entry names the manuscript and tablet line; MLC 1296 = fragment A (a+N = A N; "wisdom he gave him, eternal life he did not" = A 4), K 8743 = C, K 8214 = D (Anu's laughter = D 4′). eBL's `oldLineNumbers` field is empty for this text.

### Sources consulted
- **Rank 1:** eBL `L/1/7` (Mitto, rev. Földi, Heinrich, Jiménez): `../../sources/eBL/Adapa-MB/`, `Adapa-SB/` (`reading-edition.md`, `glossary.md`, `manuscripts.md`, `bibliography.md`, `_raw/text.json` intro). Second opinion on the MB transliteration: SEAL 1557 (`../../sources/SEAL/Adapa-MB-EA-356/`), which differs from eBL at MB 15' (*i-de* = *idû* "to know", eBL *idī* "arms") and MB 5' (*lu-ú-še-bi-ir* as one form).
- **Rank 2:** the eBL dictionary glosses in `glossary.md` (stems checked there for every verb); the eBL API once (`/api/texts/L/2/2`, to identify the parallel cited at SB c+5 as *Ludlul bēl nēmeqi*).
- **Rank 3, after drafting:** Foster's translation (eBL T lines). Earlier editors only through eBL's notes: Izre'el 2001, Dietrich 1991, Kienast 1978, von Soden 1976, Picchioni 1981, Kämmerer 1998, Edzard 2002, Dalley 1989, Foster 2005, Lambert (folios; review 2003), Peterson 2017 (MB Sumerian, Nippur), Cavigneaux 2014 (Sumerian, Meturan).
- **Not available (paid / not owned), not used:** Izre'el 2001 (*Adapa and the South Wind*, MC 10, the standard edition), Cavigneaux 2014, Peterson 2017, Picchioni 1981, Dietrich, Kämmerer, Hecker (TUAT), Foster's *Before the Muses*, Dalley. No pirate copies were searched for.

### Decisions
1. **Two recensions, one file; each line from its own manuscripts.** Identical Akkadian gets identical Hebrew, brackets excepted (MB 16' = SB c+12; MB 20' = SB c+17; MB 18' ≈ SB c+15; SB c+4 = c+10; within MB 21'/44', 23'/46', 24'/47', 27'/50', 11'/40'/55'); slight differences get equally slight differences (25' "בארצנו" / 48' "בארץ"; 26' / 49'; 30' *damiqta* / 63' *banīta*; SB e+1–3 "אמר לו" for *iqbīšum* against MB "לקחו לו" for *ilqûniššum*).
2. **Brackets** follow eBL's N and the ATF per word, by the creation-corpus rule: a word is in `[ ]` when its root or most of it is broken; one broken sign of a certain word is not bracketed (the parsing says so); partial brackets where part of the word survives. Scribal omissions (SB a+11 ⟨עושה⟩, a+17 ⟨המלך⟩?) are `⟨ ⟩`.
3. **Key verbs by distribution.** The whole text was checked for *akālu*, *šatû*, *balāṭu*, *mâtu/mūtu*, *labāšu*, *pašāšu*, *nadānu*, *leqû*, *târu*, *ṣiāḫu*, *šebēru*: *leqû* = `לָקַח` frames the story (MB 14' "let them take him here", 68'–72' "they took (fetched) for him" ×4, 77' "take him and return him"); *akālu*/*šatû* only in the warning (33'–34'), the offer (68'–70'), the question (74') and the excuse (76'); *labāšu* G/Gt/N = `לָבַשׁ`, Š (17', restored) = `הִלְבִּישׁ`; *târu* (77', partly restored) = `הֵשִׁיב`, cognate of `שׁוּב` (so `שָׁב` is used for nothing else: *saḫāru* = `חָזַר`, SB e+18).
4. **Terms (cross-text renderings as used):** *akalu* = `לֶחֶם`; *akal balāṭi* / *akala ša mūti* = `לֶחֶם הַחַיִּים` / `לֶחֶם הַמָּוֶת` (both construct in Hebrew; the Akkadian difference *ša*/construct is in the note); *mê balāṭi* / *mê mūti* = `מֵי הַחַיִּים` / `מֵי הַמָּוֶת`; *napištu dārītu* = `חַיֵּי נֶצַח`; *balāṭu* G stative = `חָיָה` (`לֹא תִחְיֶה`); *nēmequ* `חָכְמָה`, *uznu* `בִּינָה`, *ḫasīsu* `תְּבוּנָה` (*atra ḫasīsa* = `רַב־תְּבוּנָה`), *tašīmtu* `הַבְחָנָה` (new); *lubāru* = `בֶּגֶד`, *ṣubātu* = `שִׂמְלָה` (new), *karru* = `מַעֲטֵה אֵבֶל` (new); *šamnu* = `שֶׁמֶן`, *pašāšu* = `סָךְ`; *ṣiāḫu* = `צָחַק` (Gtn by doubling the verb: `וְיִצְחֲקוּ וְיִצְחֲקוּ`); *apkallu* = `חָכָם`; *banû* (verb) = `בָּנָה`.
5. **"Good" words kept apart** so that `טוֹב` (↔ `טוֹב וָרָע`) appears only where the Akkadian is its cognate *ṭābu* (SB e+20): *damqu* = `נָאֶה` (MB 30'), *banû* (adj.) = `יָפֶה` (MB 31' `פָּנִים יָפוֹת`, 63', 65' `שֶׁאֵינָהּ יָפָה`).
6. **The South Wind:** *šūtu* (a single word, "the South") = `רוּחַ הַדָּרוֹם` as the acting, winged figure; `רוּחַ` is the Hebrew idiom, not *šāru* (cf. `תֵימָן` alone as the south wind, Song 4:16, Ps 78:26). Enūma eliš IV 43 renders the same word `דָּרוֹם` in the list of directions; both are kept, by function. The construction *ša šūti kappaša* = `כְּנָפָהּ שֶׁל רוּחַ הַדָּרוֹם` (anticipatory genitive), construct *kappi šūti* (SB e+13) = `כְּנַף רוּחַ הַדָּרוֹם`.
7. **Echo control, choices against a ready Genesis word:** *nazāru* = `גִּדֵּף` (not `אָרַר`/`קִלֵּל`); *qaqqaru* = `קַרְקַע` (not `אֲדָמָה`, which would manufacture the 3:19 echo and the `אָדָם`/`אֲדָמָה` pun); *lapātu* = `לָפַת` (not `נָגַע`, cf. 3:3); *qabû* "order (something for someone)" = `אָמַר לוֹ` (not `צִוָּה`, cf. 2:16); *mār šipri* = `שָׁלִיחַ` (not `מַלְאָךְ`); *karru* not `שַׂק` (Akk. *saqqu* is its cognate); *arkât ūmī* = `לְאֹרֶךְ יָמִים` (cognate *ark-*; not `לְעוֹלָם`, not `בְּאַחֲרִית הַיָּמִים`); *ina ūmīšu* / *inūmīšu* = `בָּעֵת הַהִיא` (corpus; not `בַּיָּמִים הָהֵם`). The cognates that do carry the comparison are rendered by them and tagged: *leqû* `לָקַח`, *akālu* `אָכַל`, *lā* `לֹא` (apodictic prohibition, = `לֹא תֹאכַל`), *mūtu* `מָוֶת`, *labāšu* `לָבַשׁ`, *ṣiāḫu* `צָחַק`, *târu* `הֵשִׁיב`, *šebēru* `שָׁבַר`, *šuklulu* `שִׁכְלֵל` (the Shafel itself), *zēru* `זֶרַע`, *kussû* `כִּסֵּא`, *ṭebû* `הִטְבִּיעַ`, *qerēbu* `קָרַב`.
8. **Deviation from Enūma eliš (project 1):** there *ṭeḫû* = `קָרַב`; here *qerēbu* (its cognate) occurs too (MB 53'), so *ṭeḫû* = `נִגַּשׁ` and `קָרַב` is kept for *qerēbu*. Not a change of the corpus file (`קרב` is not in its §0–3), only of the project-1 glossary's usage. Also *dagālu* = `צָפָה` (Gilg. XI 93 uses `הִסְתַּכֵּל` for *palāsu* Gt; EE uses `הִבִּיט` for *palāsu* N, kept here).
9. **Readings followed (eBL against older editors), each marked and discussed in the apparatus:** MB 5' precative stative *lū šebir* "let it be broken" (not *lušebbir* "I will break"); MB 15' *idī* "arms" (von Soden; majority and SEAL: *īdi* "he knew"); MB 58' "halfway, crossing" (Peterson; older and CDA: "the sea became smooth"), italic; MB 60' *ultamṣil* "plunged?" (Izre'el), italic; MB 65' *lā banīta*; MB 75' statement "לא תחיה!" (Foster: question); SB a+6 *šeddi* (alt. *riddi*); SB a+17 *<šar>-ru*.
10. **Names** (vocalized at first occurrence in each recension): אֲדָפָּה, אֵאָה, אָנוּ, אִילַבְּרַת (*Ilabrat*, vizier; *šukkallu* = `וְזִיר`, as in Enūma eliš), דּוּמוּזִי (note: = `תַּמּוּז`, יחזקאל ח:יד; not assimilated in the text), גִּיזִּידָה (the text's *Gizzida*; note: = Ningišzida `נִינְגִּשְׁזִידָה`), אֶרִידוּ, אֲנוּנָּכִּי, נִינְכַּרַּךְ. The old Adapa/`אָדָם` name comparison is mentioned only to be rejected (MB 11').

### Open decisions for the user
1. **MB 15'**: eBL's "Ea, (he) of heaven, touched his arms" (followed, italic) or the majority reading "Ea, who knows (the ways of) heaven, touched him" (SEAL too). The second strengthens the Ea / snake comparison (`כִּי יֹדֵעַ אֱלֹהִים`, 3:5).
2. **MB 58'**: "as I crossed the sea, in its middle" (eBL/Peterson, followed) or the older "the sea was smooth (like a mirror)" (CDA).
3. **MB 75' *lā balṭāta***: statement "לא תחיה!" (followed; most translators) or Foster's question "Won't you live?".
4. **karru** = `מַעֲטֵה אֵבֶל` (Isa 61:3 `מַעֲטֵה … תַּחַת … אֵבֶל`) or the more common `בִּגְדֵי אֵבֶל` (2 Sam 14:2, where it stands with "do not anoint with oil" – the Adapa pair). `בגד` was avoided because it renders *lubāru*.
5. **qaqqaru** (MB 77') = `קַרְקָעוֹ` (followed, echo control) or the more natural `אַדְמָתוֹ`, which would put `אֲדָמָה` (3:19, 3:23) into the text.
6. **šūtu** = `רוּחַ הַדָּרוֹם` (followed) or `הַדָּרוֹם` alone, like `תֵימָן` in Song 4:16 (closer to the single Akkadian word, but harder to read).
7. **šēdu** (SB a+6) = `רוּחַ־מָגֵן` (followed) – `שֵׁד` is the cognate but means "demon" in Hebrew (false friend).
8. **pašīšu** (SB a+9) = `מָשׁוּחַ` (the Hebrew priestly root `משח`) or a form of `סוך`, to keep the link with the oil of MB 36'/72'.
9. **Dumuzi** = `דּוּמוּזִי` in the text (followed, as for all deities) or the biblical `תַּמּוּז` (Ezek 8:14).
10. **Old sigla of the SB fragments:** A = MLC 1296, C = K 8743, D = K 8214 follow the conventional labels; Izre'el's sigla for K 15072 and K 9994 could not be checked (his edition is paid). **Buying Izre'el 2001** would also resolve the restorations eBL cites from it (MB 16', 17', 60').
11. **Print stub and combined file:** `../../הדפסה/סיפורי-הבריאה-המסופוטמיים/2-טקסטי-בריאה-ועדן.rtl.md` and the project's generated `translation.rtl.md` are the coordinator's (outside this text's remit).

### Progress log
- **Drafting** (2026-10-16): all 175 lines, MB then SB, from eBL N/L/notes and `glossary.md`; parsing per word; Foster read after each line's draft; echo check per line, and the key-verb distribution over the whole text (Decision 3).
- **Review (a) fidelity** (full read-through of the clean translation against N and the ATF): bracket placement corrected at MB 14' (`[וְיִ]קְחוּהוּ`: *qu* survives), MB 20' (`לַשָּׁ[מַ]יִם`, two signs broken), SB e+1–e+2 (`אמר` unbracketed: one sign broken), e+9 (`שָׂם` unbracketed); SB e+5 *ina ilī* `מכל האלים` → `מבין האלים` (no "all" in the Akkadian); SB a+8 `משל` → `של` (*ša anunnakī*); SB a+12 *ša eridu* `לארידו` → `של ארידו`.
- **Review (b) consistency** (repeated lines compared as strings; lemma-level check against the corpus and project renderings and against the `>` lines of Enūma eliš and Gilgameš XI): *dagālu* `הסתכל` → `צפה` (collision with Gilg. XI 93 *palāsu* Gt); *wapû* Š made one form (`לְהוֹפִיעַ`, e+11, f+7); duplicated Foster quotations removed from the apparatus; the MB/SB pairs listed in Decision 1 verified identical.
- **Review (c) Hebrew** (read aloud): SB e+11 word order and infinitive smoothed; MB 26'/49', 31', 63' kept literal but readable; the doubled Gtn verbs (22', 29', 52') kept as the deliberate rendering of the iterative.
- **Checks:** `python3 -I verify.py 1` → OK (175 entries = eBL's line list; translation = annotated `>` lines). `tools/check_bible_quotes.py` on both files and this file → 0 problems (106 checked quotations in the annotated file). The `>` lines contain none of the reserved words of `renderings.md` §3 (`טובה` once, SB e+20, for *ṭābu*, its cognate).

| MB 77' | *[te]rrāšu ana qaqqarīšu* | [השי]בוהו אל קרקעו | `וַיְשַׁלְּחֵהוּ יְהוָה אֱלֹהִים מִגַּן עֵדֶן לַעֲבֹד אֶת הָאֲדָמָה אֲשֶׁר לֻקַּח מִשָּׁם` (בראשית ג:כג) | [divergence] | Return to earth after the failed chance of immortality; but no expulsion as punishment, and in SB a privileged status follows (e+9–e+11). Restored verb. |
| MB 21', 44' | *ina bābu āni dumuzi gizzida izzazzū* | בשער אנו דומוזי וגיזידה עומדים | `וַיַּשְׁכֵּן מִקֶּדֶם לְגַן עֵדֶן אֶת הַכְּרֻבִים וְאֵת לַהַט הַחֶרֶב הַמִּתְהַפֶּכֶת` (בראשית ג:כד) | [divergence] | Guardians at the entrance of the divine realm; but Dumuzi and Gizzida are vanished gods, not winged beings, and they let Adapa in and plead for him; the cherubim keep man out. No `כְּרוּבִים` in the text. |

| SB e+9 | *maṣṣarta iškun* | משמרת שם | `לִשְׁמֹר אֶת דֶּרֶךְ עֵץ הַחַיִּים` (בראשית ג:כד) | [equivalent] | *maṣṣartu* "guard / duty" laid on Adapa (Foster: "duty"); thematic and uncertain. *maṣṣartu* is from *naṣāru* (נצר), not שמר. |

Partly restored. |

| MB 73' | *iṣṣīḫ* (Anu) | צחק (אנו) | `יוֹשֵׁב בַּשָּׁמַיִם יִשְׂחָק` (תהלים ב:ד) | [equivalent] | The god enthroned in heaven laughs at mortals. |

| MB 17', 24', 35'–36', 71'–72' | *karru*; *lubāru*; *šamnu*; *pašāšu* | מעטה אבל; בגד; שמן; סך | `וְלִבְשִׁי נָא בִגְדֵי אֵבֶל וְאַל תָּסוּכִי שֶׁמֶן` (שמואל ב יד:ב) | [equivalent] | Mourning = mourning dress and no oil; Adapa arrives in mourning dress and receives garment and oil. |
| MB 71'–72' | *ittalbaš*; *ittapšiš* | ולבש; וסך | `וַיִּרְחַץ וַיָּסֶךְ וַיְחַלֵּף שִׂמְלֹתָו` (שמואל ב יב:כ) | [equivalent] | Anointing and changing clothes end mourning (David, followed by eating bread). |

| MB 17' | *karru* | מעטה אבל | `מַעֲטֵה תְהִלָּה תַּחַת רוּחַ כֵּהָה` (ישעיהו סא:ג) | [equivalent] | Source of the Hebrew `מַעֲטֶה`; in the same verse `שֶׁמֶן שָׂשׂוֹן תַּחַת אֵבֶל`. |
| MB 16'; SB c+12 | *malâ ušteššīšu* | את השער סבך לו | `וְרֹאשׁוֹ יִהְיֶה פָרוּעַ` (ויקרא יג:מה) | [equivalent] | Dishevelled hair as a sign of mourning / impurity. |
| MB 42' | *ana šamê īteli* | ולשמים עלה | `מִי עָלָה שָׁמַיִם וַיֵּרַד` (משלי ל:ד) | [equivalent] | A man's ascent to heaven (and descent). |
| MB 42' | *ana šamê īteli* | ולשמים עלה | `וַיַּעַל אֵלִיָּהוּ בַּסְעָרָה הַשָּׁמָיִם` (מלכים ב ב:יא) | [divergence] | Elijah, like Enoch, does not return. |
| MB 64' | *issakut* | שתק | `הַסְכֵּת וּשְׁמַע יִשְׂרָאֵל` (דברים כז:ט) | [cognate] | *sakātu* = סכת "be silent". Not rendered `הִסְכִּית` (Modern Hebrew "listen"). |
| MB 59' | *uṭṭebbânni* | הטביעה (אותי) | `טֻבְּעוּ בְיַם סוּף` (שמות טו:ד) | [cognate] | *ṭebû* = טבע. |
| MB 2' | *šūtu* | רוח הדרום | `עוּרִי צָפוֹן וּבוֹאִי תֵימָן` (שיר השירים ד:טז) | [equivalent] | A wind addressed by its direction name alone, feminine imperative – as Adapa addresses *šūtu*. |
| SB a+9 | *ella qātī* | טהור ידיים | `נְקִי כַפַּיִם וּבַר לֵבָב` (תהלים כד:ד) | [equivalent] | Purity of hands for temple service. |
| SB a+13 | *paššura irakkas* | את השולחן עורך | `תַּעֲרֹךְ לְפָנַי שֻׁלְחָן` (תהלים כג:ה) | [equivalent] | The idiom. |
| SB c+4 | *mūdû libbi ilī* | היודע את לב האלים | `אֲנִי יְהוָה חֹקֵר לֵב בֹּחֵן כְּלָיוֹת` (ירמיהו יז:י) | [equivalent] | Knowing / examining hearts (SB c+5 *ibarrû* "examines"). |
| SB e+6 | *qibīssu … mannu uʾattar* | אמירתו … מי יוסיף עליה? | `עָלָיו אֵין לְהוֹסִיף וּמִמֶּנּוּ אֵין לִגְרֹעַ` (קהלת ג:יד) | [equivalent] | A divine word that cannot be surpassed. |
| — | the name *Adapa* | אֲדָפָּה | `וַיִּקְרָא אֶת שְׁמָם אָדָם` (בראשית ה:ב) | [absent] | The 19th-century Adapa/Adam comparison has no linguistic basis (the name is probably Sumerian, not from *adm*); the translation does not suggest it. |
| — | (no woman, no tree, no snake in Adapa) | — | `וַתִּקַּח מִפִּרְיוֹ וַתֹּאכַל` (בראשית ג:ו) | [absent] | Adapa is alone; there is no woman, no tree and no tempter; the food is offered by the supreme god himself. |

### Proposals for the shared renderings
For the corpus `renderings.md` (and for later texts of this project, which `renderings.md` §4 already binds):
1. **šūtu**: `רוּחַ הַדָּרוֹם` when it is an acting wind (a figure), `דָּרוֹם` in lists of directions (Enūma eliš IV 43); first-occurrence note that the Akkadian has the single word.
2. **leqû** = `לָקַח` [cognate] (incl. "fetch for", *leqû* + ventive + dative = `לָקַח לוֹ`); **târu** G/D = `שָׁב` / `הֵשִׁיב` [cognate of שוב] – then keep `שוב` for *târu* only (*saḫāru* = `חָזַר`).
3. **Clothing:** *karru* = `מַעֲטֵה אֵבֶל`; *ṣubātu* = `שִׂמְלָה` (beside *lubāru* `בֶּגֶד`, *lubuštu* `לְבוּשׁ`).
4. **kullu** D "hold out, proffer" = `הוֹשִׁיט`; **nazāru** "revile, curse" = `גִּדֵּף` (so that `אָרַר` = *arāru* and `קִלֵּל` = Sum. *aš bal* stay apart).
5. **"Good":** *ṭābu* = `טוֹב` only; *damqu* = `נָאֶה`; *banû* (adj.) = `יָפֶה` (*pānū banûtu* = `פָּנִים יָפוֹת`; *lā banû* = `שֶׁאֵינוֹ יָפֶה`).
6. **Knowledge words:** add *tašīmtu* = `הַבְחָנָה`; *šuklulu* = `שִׁכְלֵל` [cognate, the Shafel] (↔ `כְּלִיל`, Ezek 28:12); *rapša uzni* (Ea's epithet) = `רְחַב הַבִּינָה`; *mūdû* = `הַיּוֹדֵעַ` [cognate].
7. **Approach verbs:** *qerēbu* = `קָרַב` [cognate]; *ṭeḫû* = `נִגַּשׁ`; *kašādu* = `הִגִּיעַ`; *dagālu* = `צָפָה`; *palāsu* N = `הִבִּיט` (as in Enūma eliš). The corpus should decide whether Enūma eliš's *ṭeḫû* = `קָרַב` is changed to `נִגַּשׁ` (it would then collide with *âru* = `נִגַּשׁ` there; alternative for *ṭeḫû*: `קָרַב` everywhere and *qerēbu* = `הִתְקָרֵב`).
8. **Court words:** *šukkallu* = `וְזִיר` (as Enūma eliš); *mār šipri* = `שָׁלִיחַ` (not `מַלְאָךְ`); *maṣṣartu* = `מִשְׁמֶרֶת` (as Enūma eliš); *šubarrû* = `חֵרוּת` (not `דְּרוֹר`, which is *andurāru*; not `חָפְשִׁי`, false friend of *ḫupšu*); *arkât ūmī* = `לְאֹרֶךְ יָמִים` [cognate]; *bābu* (of a god's house, heaven) = `שַׁעַר`; *ḫarrānu* = `דֶּרֶךְ`; *qaqqaru* = `קַרְקַע` (not `אֲדָמָה`).
9. **Names:** Ilabrat = `אִילַבְּרַת`; Dumuzi = `דּוּמוּזִי` (note `תַּמּוּז`); Gizzida = `גִּיזִּידָה`, Ningišzida = `נִינְגִּשְׁזִידָה`; Ninkarrak = `נִינְכַּרַּךְ`; Adapa's epithet *atra ḫasīsa* = `רַב־תְּבוּנָה` (= the name Atra-ḫasīs, corpus).
10. **False friends** (for guidelines §6.7): *šēdu* (protective spirit) ~ `שֵׁד` (demon) – render `רוּחַ־מָגֵן`; *sakātu* "be silent" ~ `הִסְכִּית` (Modern "listen"; biblical `הַסְכֵּת` "be silent") – render `שָׁתַק`; *Adapa* ~ `אָדָם` (no relation).

### Lessons for the guidelines
- **Generate both files from one line-by-line data file** when the base edition is eBL: a small builder that copies eBL's N and T verbatim, computes the old line numbers from the ATF and writes the annotated and the clean file together removes every N-typo and every annotated/clean mismatch; `verify.py` then only confirms.
- **The old "fragment B" numbering of the Amarna Adapa tablet is the tablet's own line count** (obverse 1–36, reverse 37–71), recoverable from eBL's ATF line labels (`o N'` → N, `r N` → 36 + N); eBL's own notes cite earlier editors in that numbering. Check this for any eBL text whose `oldLineNumbers` is empty.
- **When a text has several "good" adjectives** (*damqu*, *banû*, *ṭābu*), give each its own Hebrew word, and reserve `טוֹב` for the cognate *ṭābu*: otherwise the translation itself manufactures echoes of `טוֹב וָרָע` and `לֹא טוֹב`.
- **Prefer a cognate verb pair over a cognate noun:** the strongest find of this text is verbal – *leqû … akālu* (MB 68') = `וְלָקַח … וְאָכַל` (Gen 3:22), and *leqû/târu* (77') = `לֻקָּחְתָּ … שׁוּבְךָ` (3:19) – invisible in the English translations ("bring him … let him eat"; "take him back").
- **Read the later recension against the earlier one for the "who deceived whom" question:** the SB line "Anu laughed at what Ea had done" (e+4) settles a crux that the MB alone leaves open; translate every recension even when fragmentary (guidelines §5, "Translate the minor recensions too").

## Text 2: Enki and Ninḫursaĝa

### Scope
- **Text:** *Enki and Ninḫursaĝa* ("the Dilmun myth"), Sumerian, Old Babylonian copies (19th–18th cent. BCE); ETCSL 1.1.1 = ORACC Q000332. The whole composite: **305 entries** (ETCSL lines 1–36, 40–49, 49A–49V, 50–126, 126A–126CC, 127–132, 142–159, 164–187, 190–230, 235–241, 245–281), all translated; lines of which only traces survive have `…`/`[…]` entries. Lines lost without a number (37–39, 133–141, 160–163, 188–189, 231–234, 242–244) are marked between the entries.
- **Manuscripts** (ETCSL, after Attinger 1984): **N** = CBS 4561 (Nippur, PBS 10/1 1; the main six-column tablet); **Ur** = UET 6/1 1 (U 7754; ORACC P346086); **L** = AO 6724 (TCL 16 62; Louvre, unknown provenance). Recension tags in the Hebrew lines: `{נוסח אור}` for 49A–49V and 253 (Ur only); `{כ״י הלובר}` for 126A–126CC (ETCSL: "a ms. of unknown origin" – the only such manuscript is AO 6724; an inference, not stated by ETCSL); `{לא בכ״י ניפור}` for 177 (ETCSL "1 line not in the ms. from Nibru"; Ur does not preserve this section, so the witness is presumably L).
- **Files:** `annotated-2-Enki-Ninhursag.rtl.md` (Hebrew header; 43 sections following ETCSL's translation paragraphs; per line: ETCSL transliteration, Hebrew, parsing with the ORACC morph, ETCSL + our departures, parallels with tags, notes with the Ur comparison); `translation-2-Enki-Ninhursag.rtl.md` (introduction, the same sections, 23 footnotes). The clean file is generated from the annotated `>` lines by a scratch script (session scratchpad, not kept), so the two are identical by construction; `python3 -I verify.py 2` → OK.
- **Content:** Dilmun pure, without predators, illness, old age or mourning – and without fresh water (1–28); Ninsikila's complaint and Enki's water brought up from the earth (29–62), with the Ur version's blessing of the foreign lands and their goods (49A–49V); Enki's unions over three (L: four) generations of goddesses, with nine-day pregnancies (63–185); Uttu's demand for fruit, Enki watering the gardener's land and coming disguised as the gardener (147–185); Ninḫursaĝa removes his semen and grows eight plants, which Enki looks at, has his vizier pick, eats, "decides the fate of" and "knows the heart of" (186–219); Ninḫursaĝa's oath "with the eye of life, until he dies, I shall not look at him" (220–221); the fox brings her back (222–246); she seats Enki at her vulva and gives birth to eight healing deities from his eight aching body parts, among them Nin-ti from the rib (247–271); their destinies (272–280); doxology (281).

### Sources consulted
- **Rank 1:** ETCSL 1.1.1 (`../../sources/ETCSL/1.1.1--Enki-and-Ninhursaga/`: `transliteration.md`, `c.1.1.1.xml` with ETCSL's lemmas).
- **Rank 2:** ORACC Q000332 (`../../sources/ORACC/epsd2-literary--Q000332--…/Q000332--lemmatised-morph.md`): citation form and `{morph;base}` for every word – used for ḫamṭu/marû and the prefix chains in every line; ORACC P346086 (the Ur manuscript, `../../sources/ORACC/epsd2-literary--P346086--…/`), compared line by line (concordance below); the ePSD2 glossary (`gloss-sux.json` in `../../sources/ORACC/_bulk/epsd2-literary.zip`, read in place); ETCSL bulk concordance searches (`../../sources/ETCSL/_bulk/etcsl-unzipped/etcsl/transliterations/`) for *ur₅ ḫe₂-na-nam-ma* (1.4.1 89; 1.8.2.2 216), *igi nam-til₃-la* (1.6.2 389; 2.5.4.01 103; 2.5.4.02 41; 2.5.4.15 7; 2.5.5.5 13), *nam-erim₂ … kud* (1.8.1.4 20; 1.8.1.5.1 137; 2.5.4.01 380), *a … dug₄* "to water" (1.7.4 A 10; 2.4.2.14 24; 2.4.4.3 1–2; 4.08.05), *giri₁₇-zal*, *dirig ga-ri*, *ambar-ra im-da-la₂*, *NI-buluĝ₃* (only this text).
- **Rank 3:** ETCSL's English translation (`translation.md`). **Note (honesty):** the ETCSL translation was in view from the start (it was read together with the transliteration when the sources were opened), so the rule "own reading first" (guidelines §3) was kept per line only in the sense that every line was parsed from the morph before its ETCSL rendering was compared; every departure is recorded in the line's `תרגומים`.
- **Not available, not used:** Attinger 1984 (*ZA* 74, the edition), Jacobsen 1987 (*The Harps that Once…*), Katz 2007, Kramer 1945 (BASOR SS 1, the *ti*/rib proposal; cited from general knowledge only), Römer 1993 (TUAT), Bottéro & Kramer 1989. No pirate copies were searched for.

### Decisions
1. **Aspect** (project Decisions 4): narrative marû → Hebrew participle (`אֵינוֹ מַשְׁמִיעַ`, `מֵצִיץ`… – e.g. 11–28, 55–57, 65–66, 93, 174–176, 181, 203–218); ḫamṭu → past (`יָלְדָה`, `קִבְּלָה`, `גָּזַר`); *ga-* → cohortative (`אֲנַוְּטָה`, `אִיעָצֵךְ`, `אֹמְרָה`, `אֶתְּנָה`, `אֶטְּעָה`); *ḫe₂-* → jussive (`יַעֲלוּ`, `תִּשְׁתֶּה`, `יִהְיֶה`, `תִּנָּשֵׂא`); *bara-* (221) → emphatic negative vow (`לֹא אַבִּיט`); *na-* affirmative + *me* (`na-nam`) → `אָכֵן`.
2. **Formulas** fixed at the first occurrence and verified by script (identical Hebrew wherever the Sumerian is identical; differences only where the Sumerian differs: 102–104 without *-a-ni*; 114–115 imperative vs 91/94 question; 126M *u₃-*; 126Q/185 *-kam*; 164–166 ḫamṭu "brought" vs 174–176 marû "gives"). Table: `renderings.md` §4, Text 2.
3. **Ur and Louvre material**: lines found only in one manuscript keep their ETCSL number and get a recension tag at the start of the Hebrew line (guidelines §6.8). For lines that Ur shares with the composite (1–23, 251–268) the Ur wording is given in the apparatus (`נוסח אור: …`), translated when the sense differs (5, 12, 15, 19, 20–22).
4. **Sexual vocabulary**: the Sumerian nouns are plain anatomical words, so the Hebrew uses sober anatomical nouns, neither coarse nor euphemistic: *ĝiš₃* = `זַכְרוּת`, *gal₄-la* = `עֶרְוָה`, *ur₂* = `חֵיק`, *ḫaš₂* = `יָרֵךְ`; the verbs: *nu₂* = `שָׁכַב`, *ĝiš₃ du₃/dug₄* = `בָּעַל`, *ne su-ub* = `נָשַׁק`, *a šag₄ ri* = `יָצַק מַיִם בָּרֶחֶם`; *a* "water/semen" stays `מַיִם` (the Sumerian image), the parsing gives the sense.
5. **Water words**: *a dug₄* = `הִשְׁקָה` (72; precedent flood project 3 A 10); *a si* = `מִלֵּא מַיִם`; *ed₃* (of water) = `עָלָה`; *a dug₃* = `מַיִם מְתוּקִים` vs *a sis* = `מַיִם מָרִים`; *ki-ta* = `מִן הָאָרֶץ`. Each is the natural Hebrew word; the Gen 2:6 overlaps are tagged, not created.
6. **"Good" words**: *sag₉* = `נָאֶה` (as Akk. *damqu*), *sig₅* = `מְעֻלֶּה`, *dug₃* = `טוֹב` (general; = Akk. *ṭābu*) / `מָתוֹק` (water). `טוֹב` occurs only in 49Q–49R.
7. **The oath (220)**: *mu X nam-erim₂ kud* = `עַל שֵׁם X שְׁבוּעָה נִשְׁבְּעָה` (ETCSL corpus: *nam-erim₂ kud* is "to swear an oath"); ETCSL's "cursed" is recorded; no `ארר`/`קלל`. **"The eye of life"** (221) = `עֵין הַחַיִּים` (a known Sumerian idiom of the life-giving divine look).
8. **Nin-ti (269)**: the name is transliterated (`נִין־תִּי`), with both senses of TI (*ti* "rib", *ti(l)* "to live") in the note and footnote; `צֵלָע` is used only for *ti* in 268. The note states that the Hebrew has no corresponding pun.
9. **Deviations from `renderings.md` §2, by meaning** (Changelog there): *ki* "earth (below)" = `הָאָרֶץ` without *an* (43, 54); *zi* as the body part = `נֶפֶשׁ` (264; elsewhere `חַיִּים`, 70, 239); *du₃* of trees = `נָטַע?` (227; elsewhere `בָּנָה`).
10. **Echo control, choices against a ready biblical word**: *dar* = `חֻגְלָה` (not `קוֹרֵא`); *niĝ₂ al di* = `דְּבַר־חֵפֶץ` (not `נֶחְמָד`, ב:ט; ג:ו); *ul … ti* = `הִפְרִיחַ?` (not `עֶדְנָה`, root of `עֵדֶן`); *1-a* "first" = `תְּחִלָּה` (not `בָּרִאשׁוֹנָה`, letters of `בָּרָא`); *ḫe₂-am₃* "consent" = `'הֵן'` (not `יְהִי`); *tag* = `מִשֵּׁשׁ` (not `נָגַע`); *mu pad₃* = `יִנָּקֵב` (not `יִקָּרֵא`); *ur₅ ḫe₂-na-nam-ma* = `כָּךְ אָכֵן הָיָה` (not `וַיְהִי כֵן`); *gu₃ de₂* = `פּוֹנֶה` / `צָעַק` (never `קָרָא`); *u₂-sar-ra* = `צֶמַח־הָעֲרוּגָה` (not `יֶרֶק`). The reserved words of §3 do not occur in the Hebrew lines except `צֵלָע` (268, *ti*), `הִשְׁקָה` (72, *a dug₄*), `גַּן`/`גַּנָּן` (156–171, *kiri₆*), `עָפָר` (156, 222, *saḫar*), `יָדַע` (219, *zu*), `טוֹב` (49Q–R, *dug₃*) – each its word's meaning, each tagged.
11. **Names**: Uttu = `אוּטּוּ` (with ט, vocalized at every occurrence: never confusable with Utu `אוּתוּ`, also always vocalized, nor with `אוֹתוֹ`); Magan = `מָגָן` always vocalized; Ningiriutud hyphenated `נִין־גִּירִי־אוּ־תּוּד` so that the sequence "אותו" never appears; Sumer = `שׁוּמֶר` (Kengir; not `שִׁנְעָר`); Elam, Ur, Uruk in biblical forms (place names, guidelines §6.4.6).
12. **ORACC vs ETCSL**: ETCSL's composite is translated; ORACC's different lemmatisations are noted where they change the sense (30 *nam* "fate"; 73 Ninḫursaĝa ergative; 96 *dirig* "exceed"; 187 *a* = "cry"; 265 Nazi = Nanše?).

### Open decisions for the user
1. **73**: ETCSL (followed, marked `?`): "(Enki) poured water into the womb of Ninḫursaĝa"; the Sumerian puts Ninḫursaĝa first with *-ke₄*, which ORACC parses as ergative: "Ninḫursaĝa let the water be poured (took the water) into the womb".
2. **219** *šag₄-ba ba-ni-in-zu*: "he knew their heart" (followed: Enki learns the plants' nature by eating them; [equivalent] ↔ `וַיֵּדְעוּ`, Gen 3:7) or ETCSL's "had them know it in their hearts" (Enki makes the plants know their destiny) – the second weakens the Gen 3:7 parallel.
3. **220** *nam-erim₂ kud*: "swore an oath" (followed) or "cursed" (ETCSL and most translations) – matters for the Gen 3:14–17 row.
4. **264** *zi* = `נַפְשִׁי` ("my throat/breath/life", followed, with a footnote) or the plain `גְּרוֹנִי` (clearer, but loses the "life" sense and the Gen 2:7 link).
5. **67–68**: "the august one" (*maḫ*) = Nintur/Ninmaḫ, who pushes Enki away and cries "no man shall take me in the marsh" (followed, `?`), or Enki himself.
6. **Uttu's epithet** *munus NI-buluĝ₃* = `הָאִשָּׁה הַמְטֻפַּחַת?` (from *buluĝ₃* "to rear") or ETCSL's "the exalted (?) woman".
7. **The Ur ms. 22** writes "um-ma me-en mu-ni-ib₂-be₂" without *nu-*: a scribal slip or a positive statement ("the old woman says 'I am old'")? Only a collation or Attinger can tell.
8. **Buy / borrow Attinger 1984** (ZA 74, the edition with the score of all three manuscripts) – it would settle the Louvre attribution of 126A–126CC and 177, the readings of 49A–49V (ETCSL vs ORACC), and the cruxes 19, 24, 25, 41, 150, 178.
9. **Print stub and the combined `translation.rtl.md`** are the coordinator's (not created here).

### Progress log
- **Drafting** (2026-10-16/17): all 305 entries in order, from the ETCSL transliteration and the ORACC lemma+morph of every word (merged per line by a scratch script), with ETCSL concordance searches for the formulas and rare idioms; the Hebrew of each formula fixed at its first occurrence; ETCSL's translation compared after each line's parse; echo check per line; the Ur manuscript compared at every line it preserves. Drafted unvocalized and plene except names (first occurrence) and ambiguous words (the first 28 lines were drafted with full niqqud and then rewritten unvocalized by hand, not by stripping).
- **Review (a) fidelity** (full read of every `>` line against the transliteration, brackets by root): 49H `עֹ[צֶם]` → `עֹצֶם` (*usu* is preserved; only an optional sign after it is restored); 124 `הַשּׂ[בַע]` → unbracketed (only *-na-gin₇* restored); 157 `כָּרַךְ […]` → `כָּרַךְ` (only the suffix *-e-de₃* is restored; noted in the parsing); 98/118/126M `העמידה` → `העמידהּ` (ḫamṭu with agent: he set **it**, the foot; unvocalized it read "she stood"); 227 `ייקב` → `יִנָּקֵב`. The bracket placement of 33, 36, 40–44, 81–87, 105–111, 126–126CC, 147–149, 164, 190–218, 235–238, 268–281 was re-checked and kept.
- **Review (b) consistency** (repeated lines compared as strings by script, brackets/vocalization ignored; 29 groups): all identical or differing only where the Sumerian differs or by first-occurrence vocalization. Name and term checks against `renderings.md` §1–3 and against the `>` lines of Adapa and the flood corpus's Sumerian project: Utu unvocalized in 50, 51, 62, 237 → `אוּתוּ` everywhere; Uttu `אוטו` (32 occurrences, lines and apparatus) → `אוּטּוּ`; Magan `מגן` → `מָגָן` (7); *1-a/1-am₃* `בראשונה`/`ראשונה` → `תחילה` in 97, 117, 126L, 228–230 (the letters ב־ר־א; guidelines §5 working rules); *sukkal* = `וְזִיר` and *nam tar* = `גָּזַר גּוֹרָל` confirmed against Adapa/§2.
- **Review (c) Hebrew** (read aloud): 171 nested quotation marks → `'הֵן'`; the word order of the long formula lines (72, 73, 126P, 184) kept close to the Sumerian but readable; 24 and 178 left with italic (uncertain) verbs rather than smoothed.
- **Checks**: `python3 -I verify.py 2` → OK (305 entries = ETCSL's line list; translation = annotated `>` lines). `tools/check_bible_quotes.py` on the annotated file, the translation, this file and `renderings.md` → 0 problems (after fixing 6 quotations in the draft: Isa 59:11, Ezek 27:15 ketiv/qere, Song 6:2 defective spelling, Gen 15:18, Num 1:17, Gen 2:25/3:10). Grep of the `>` lines for the reserved words: only the tagged occurrences listed in Decision 10.

### Concordance: the Ur manuscript
UET 6/1 1 = ORACC P346086 (70 lines in ORACC): obverse col. i (o i 1–23), col. ii (o ii 1–21), reverse col. i′ (r i′ 1′–23′), col. ii′ (r ii′ 1′–3′, a colophon). The obverse col. i ends at 23; lines 24–49 (≈26 lines) are lost from it, so col. i held about 49 lines and **49A–49V stood at the top of col. ii, right after 49**. The reverse col. i′ is the end of the text (251–268); 269–281 are lost in Ur.

| Ur (P346086) | Composite (ETCSL) | Difference that matters |
|---|---|---|
| o i 1–2 | 1–2 | none (`kug-kug-ga` without the copula). |
| o i 3–4 | 3–4 | 3 *šen-na* "clean" for *sikil-am₃*; 4 `ki sikil-la … kug-ga` ("Dilmun, a pure place; Dilmun the pure") for *sikil-am₃ … dadag-ga-am₃*. |
| o i 5, 8 | 5, 8 | *dili-ĝu₁₀-ne* ("I/we alone"?) for *dili-ni-ne*; verb *u₃-bi₂-nud* without the agent *-n-*: "when (they) lay down", not "when he laid (her) down". |
| o i 6, 9 | 6, 9 | Enki in the ergative; *dam-a-ni-ta* (orthography). |
| o i 7, 10 | 7, 10 | no copula. |
| o i 11 | 11 | none (KA-KA = *gu₃-gu₃*). |
| o i 12 | 12 | the francolin "does not utter speckled (varied) words" (*inim gun₃-gun₃*) for "the francolin's cry". |
| o i 13 | 13 | the lion "does not strike the neck" (*gu₂ … rah₂-rah₂*) for *saĝ ĝiš ra* "kill": same sense. |
| o i 14 | 14 | none. |
| o i 15 | 15 | the dog "does not know to curl/bend the **tail**" (*kun gurum-gurum*) for "to make the **kids** crouch": the predator image is gone. |
| o i 16–18 | 16–18 | none. |
| o i 19 | 19 | *šub* "to let fall": "the dove does not droop its head" – the reading followed for the composite's unclear RU. |
| o i 20–21 | 20–21 | first person: "the sick of eye does not say '**my** eye is sick'", "the sick of head … 'my head is sick'" – closer to `וּבַל יֹאמַר שָׁכֵן חָלִיתִי` (ישעיהו לג:כד). |
| o i 22 | 22 | "… 'I am an old woman' (she) says" **without** *nu-* (break before it): slip or positive? (open decision 7). |
| o i 23 | 23 | only the end of the verb. |
| — | 24–49 | lost in Ur. |
| o ii 1–21 | 49A–49V | **Ur only.** ETCSL and ORACC read the same tablet differently in places: 49B *sag₁₀-ga* (ORACC) for *X-ga*; 49I *na₄ kalag* "strong stone" for *{na₄}esi*; 49L *gukkal* "fat-tailed sheep" for *siki gun₃(!)* "multicoloured wool"; 49N *aga* "crown" for *barag* "dais"; 49O *{tug₂}šutur* for *tug₂ maḫ*. These are reading differences, not variants. |
| — | 50–250 | lost in Ur (the water's arrival, the unions, the plants, the oath, the fox): **no Ur wording survives for the water (50–62), the plants (190–219) or the curse/oath (220–221).** |
| r i′ 1′–2′ | 251–252 | traces only. |
| r i′ 3′ | 253 | **Ur only** among the manuscripts ETCSL used ("1 line not in the ms. from Nibru"). |
| r i′ 4′–9′ | 254–259 | 256 the god's name *{d}ab-ba₆* ("Abba") for *ab-u₂*. |
| r i′ 10′–15′ | 260–263 | the composite's single lines 260 and 262 (question + answer) are two lines each in Ur; 261 *nin-kiri₃-e-tu* (= *nin-giri₁₇-u₃-tud*, "nose"). |
| r i′ 16′–18′ | 266–267 | **order**: in Ur the arm and Azimua (266–267) come **before** the throat and Nazi (264–265); *a₂-zid-mu₂-a*. |
| r i′ 19′–21′ | 264–265 | see above. |
| r i′ 22′–23′ | 268 | "my **rib** hurts me" (*ti-ĝu₁₀ ma-gig*): the rib line is preserved in Ur too; Nin-ti (269) is lost. |
| r ii′ 1′–3′ | (colophon) | month Udduru, day 24 (?); a year name "the year (DN) gave him the strong weapon of Enlil (and) Uruk was destroyed" – probably of Rīm-Sîn I of Larsa (conquest of Uruk), not checked. |

### Parallels index (Enki and Ninḫursaĝa)

| Line | Original | Hebrew rendering | Bible | Tag | Comment |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 62 (50) | *i₃-ne-eš₂ {d}utu ud ne-a ur₅ ḫe₂-na-nam-ma* | עתה – אוּתוּ, ביום הזה – כך אכן היה | `וַיְהִי כֵן` (בראשית א:ז) | [equivalent] | Fulfilment formula: what was promised (40–49) comes about as said (50–61). Not rendered `וַיְהִי כֵן`. |
| 13–16 | *ur-gu-la saĝ ĝiš nu-ub-ra-ra*; *ur-bar-ra sila₄ nu-ub-kar-re* | האריה אינו הורג; הזאב אינו חוטף טלה | `וּלְכָל חַיַּת הָאָרֶץ … אֶת כָּל יֶרֶק עֵשֶׂב לְאָכְלָה` (בראשית א:ל) | [equivalent] | No predation in the first land; in Genesis the animals' food is plants. |
| 11–28 | *nu-* + marû (×17) | אינו משמיע / אינו הורג / אינה אומרת… | `וְכֹל שִׂיחַ הַשָּׂדֶה טֶרֶם יִהְיֶה בָאָרֶץ` (בראשית ב:ה) | [divergence] | The primeval land described by negations; the Sumerian has a present negation, **not** "not yet" (`טֶרֶם`). ETCSL's "not yet" (11, 18) imports the Genesis idea. |
| 33, 36 | *iri kar id₂-da nu-un-tuku-a*; *iri a-šag₄ a-gar₃ ab-sin₂ nu-un-tuku-a* | עיר שרציף נהר אין לה; עיר ששדה, שדמה ותלם אין לה | `כִּי לֹא הִמְטִיר יְהוָה אֱלֹהִים עַל הָאָרֶץ וְאָדָם אַיִן לַעֲבֹד אֶת הָאֲדָמָה` (בראשית ב:ה) | [divergence] | The first land lacks water and tilled field; in Genesis rain and a man; here river water. 36 is mostly restored. |
| 59 | *a-šag₄ a-gar₃ ab-sin₂-a-ni še mu-na-ab-X-šum₂?* | שדה, שדמה ותלמיה – שעורה נתנו? לה | `וְכָל עֵשֶׂב הַשָּׂדֶה טֶרֶם יִצְמָח` (בראשית ב:ה) | [divergence] | After the water the fields yield grain. Verb partly unread. |
| 43, 54 | *a dug₃ ki-ta (mu-na-ra-DU)* | מים מתוקים מן הארץ (יצאו לה?) | `וְאֵד יַעֲלֶה מִן הָאָרֶץ וְהִשְׁקָה אֶת כָּל פְּנֵי הָאֲדָמָה` (בראשית ב:ו) | [equivalent] | **Fresh water from the earth**, not from the sky (from the Abzu, Enki's realm). No *edû* (the reserved `אֵד`); the overlap is the image and `מִן הָאָרֶץ` (*ki-ta*). 43 is wholly restored from 54. |

### Proposals for the shared renderings
1. **Sumerian purity words** (for all Sumerian texts): *kug* = `טָהוֹר` (already flood project 3), *sikil* = `זַךְ`, *dadag* = `בָּהִיר`, *šen* = `נָקִי`.
2. **Water**: *a dug₄* = `הִשְׁקָה` (confirm the flood project-3 precedent as a corpus rule, with the [equivalent] tag to Gen 2:6); *a si* = `מִלֵּא מַיִם`; *ed₃* (of water) = `עָלָה`; *a dug₃* = `מַיִם מְתוּקִים`, *a sis* = `מַיִם מָרִים`; *ḫe₂-ĝal₂* = `שֶׁפַע`, *ḫe-nun* = `שֹׂבַע`.
3. **"Good"** across languages: Sum. *dug₃* = `טוֹב` (as Akk. *ṭābu*), *sag₉* = `נָאֶה` (as *damqu*), *sig₅* = `מְעֻלֶּה`.
4. **Oath / curse**: *nam-erim₂ kud* = `נִשְׁבַּע שְׁבוּעָה` (= *māmītu*); keep *aš bal* = `קִלֵּל`, *arāru* = `אָרַר`; specify this in §2.
5. **Sexual vocabulary** (Enki and Ninmaḫ, Song of the Hoe may need it): *ĝiš₃* = `זַכְרוּת`, *gal₄-la* = `עֶרְוָה`, *ĝiš₃ du₃* = `בָּעַל`, *ne su-ub* = `נָשַׁק`, *ur₂* = `חֵיק`, *nu₂* = `שָׁכַב`.
6. **Body parts** (Enki and Ninmaḫ has the defective bodies): *ti* = `צֵלָע`, *zag* = `צַד`, *ugu-dili* = `קָדְקֹד`, *giri₁₇*/*kiri₃* = `אַף`, *ka* = `פֶּה`, *a₂* = `זְרוֹעַ`, *zi* (body part) = `נֶפֶשׁ` (note "throat"), *ḫaš₂* = `יָרֵךְ`, *igi* = `עַיִן`.
7. **Names**: Uttu = `אוּטּוּ` (always vocalized; add to §1 next to Utu); Isimud = `אִיסִימוּד`; Damgalnuna = `דַּמְגַּלְנוּנָּה`; Sumer (*ki-en-gi*) = `שׁוּמֶר`; Magan = `מָגָן` (always vocalized); Elam = `עֵילָם`, Urim = `אוּר`, Larsam = `לַרְסָה`, Nibru = `נִיפּוּר`.
8. **Formulas** likely to recur in Sumerian texts: *X Y-ra gu₃ mu-na-de₂-e* = `X אֶל Y פּוֹנֶה:`; *X mu-na-ni-ib-gi₄-gi₄* = `X מֵשִׁיב לוֹ:`; *na ga-e-de₅ … inim ga-ra-ab-dug₄* = `אִיעָצֵךְ עֵצָה … אֹמְרָה לָךְ דָּבָר` (with flood project 3); *ur₅ ḫe₂-na-nam-ma* = `כָּךְ אָכֵן הָיָה`; *igi nam-til₃-la* = `עֵין הַחַיִּים`; *za₃-mi₂* = `תְּהִלָּה`; *nam tar* + *zu* in the plant scene.
9. **Guidelines §6.7 (false friends / traps)**: `בָּרִאשׁוֹנָה` contains the letters of `בָּרָא` / `בְּרֵאשִׁית` – use `תְּחִלָּה` for "first(ly)"; `עֵין הַחַיִּים` (*igi nam-til₃*) sounds like `עֵץ הַחַיִּים` – keep the idiom but say in the note that the assonance is Hebrew only; unvocalized `אוטו` reads "car" and `אותו` "him": vocalize Uttu and Utu always.

### Lessons for the guidelines
- **A single-witness passage has no variants, only readings.** For 49A–49V (Ur only), the differences between ETCSL and ORACC are two editors' readings of the same signs (*esi*/*na₄ kalag*, *siki gun₃(!)*/*gukkal*, *barag*/*aga*); say so, and do not present them as "the Ur version". Before building a "version" argument, check which manuscripts a composite line rests on.
- **Map a manuscript to the composite by its columns.** P346086's 70 lines fall into three blocks (1–23, 49A–49V, 251–268): the column structure (col. i ≈ 1–49, so 49A–V at the top of col. ii) shows where the Ur-only lines stood, and the reverse block shows a **different order** of the healing deities (266–267 before 264–265) and lines split in two (260, 262) – details invisible in the composite.
- **Check the rank-3 translation's tense against the morph**: ETCSL's "not yet" (11, 18) and "had not been taught" (15) create the Gen 2:5 `טֶרֶם` echo out of a plain present negation; ETCSL's "cursed" (220) creates the Gen 3:14 echo out of the ordinary Sumerian for "to swear an oath". Both are now recorded in the parallels index as rank-3 imports.
- **An ETCSL concordance search settles idioms quickly**: *nam-erim₂ kud* (oath), *igi nam-til₃-la* (life-giving look), *a dug₄* (to water), *ur₅ ḫe₂-na-nam-ma* (so it was). The throw-away scripts used here (`data.py`: ETCSL transliteration + ETCSL lemmas + ORACC morph per line; `conc.py`: regex over the ETCSL bulk forms with the translation paragraph; `gl.py`: ePSD2 glossary lookup) are worth a `tools/sumerian_workbench.py` (not written here: the brief forbids writing outside the project folder).
- **Generate the clean translation from the annotated `>` lines** (with a header file and a footnote table) – as for Adapa – so that `verify.py` only confirms.
- **Puns on names need a two-level treatment**: transliterate the name in the line, give the pun in the note and a footnote, and test the claimed biblical "pun parallel" in Hebrew: for Nin-ti the Hebrew `צֵלָע` / `חַוָּה` has no pun, which is itself the finding.

## Text 3: Enki and Ninmaḫ

### Scope
- **Text:** *Enki and Ninmaḫ*, Sumerian, Old Babylonian copies (19th–18th cent. BCE); ETCSL 1.1.2 = ORACC Q000333. The whole composite: **142 entries** (ETCSL 1–141 and 87A), all translated; lines of which only traces survive (109–121, 135–139) have `…`/`[…]` entries.
- **Manuscripts** (ETCSL header): AO 7936 (TCL 16 71), BM 12845 (CT 42 28), CBS 2168+2202+11327+12738+13368 (PBS 1/1 4 etc.), N 1889, N 2571, ?N 6385 – mostly Nippur. ETCSL gives "1 ms. has instead" variants at 66, 67, 68, 74, 79, 85, 87, 124, 126, 127 and one extra line (87A); all are translated in the apparatus (`נוסח חילוף`). The **Neo-Assyrian bilingual** from Nineveh (known from the literature) is **not** in the ETCSL XML and not in our sources: not translated, recorded as a gap.
- **Files:** `annotated-3-Enki-Ninmah.rtl.md` (Hebrew header; 20 sections following ETCSL's translation paragraphs, split where a paragraph covers a fragmentary block; per line: ETCSL transliteration, Hebrew, parsing with the ORACC morph, ETCSL + departures, parallels with tags, notes, variants); `translation-3-Enki-Ninmah.rtl.md` (introduction, the same sections, 22 footnotes). The clean file is generated from the annotated `>` lines (scratch builder, session scratchpad; not kept); `python3 -I verify.py 3` → OK.
- **Content:** the gods' toil and complaint (1–11); Enki asleep in the Engur, wakened by his mother Namma (12–23); Enki's instructions: knead the clay above the Abzu, the birth goddesses nip it off, Namma brings the form into being, Ninmaḫ and seven goddesses assist, the basket is bound on man (24–37); birth (38–43, broken); the feast and the praise of Enki (44–51); the beer and Ninmaḫ's boast "good or bad as my heart inclines" (52–55); Enki's counter-boast (56–57); **the six creatures** and their fates (58–78); Enki's challenge (79–82); Enki makes **Umul** through a woman's womb (83–91); Ninmaḫ cannot sustain him (92–101); Enki recites the six fates (102–108); Ninmaḫ's lament over her ruined city and house (109–128, partly broken); Enki's reply (129–139, broken); "Ninmaḫ did not equal the great lord Enki" (140–141).

### Sources consulted
- **Rank 1:** ETCSL 1.1.2 (`../../sources/ETCSL/1.1.2--Enki-and-Ninmah/`: `transliteration.md`, `c.1.1.2.xml` with ETCSL's lemmas and the variant notes).
- **Rank 2:** ORACC Q000333 (`../../sources/ORACC/epsd2-literary--Q000333--Enki-and-Ninmah/Q000333--lemmatised-morph.md`): citation form and `{morph;base}` for every word – used for ḫamṭu/marû and the prefix chains in every line (ORACC merges ETCSL's variant lines into unnumbered `None` lines; they were separated by the ETCSL XML). ePSD2 glossary (`gloss-sux.json` in `../../sources/ORACC/_bulk/epsd2-literary.zip`, read in place) for *ĝešnu*, *u₆*, *umul*, *lil*, *tiru*, *kiĝ-sig*, *dupsik*, *me-dim₂*, *gurum*, *šuš*, *an-ta*, *sur*, *ḫum*, *sig*, *sa₂*, *lul*, *lib*, *geleĝ*, *ul*, *te*, *ara*, *niĝin*. ETCSL bulk concordance (`../../sources/ETCSL/_bulk/etcsl-unzipped/etcsl/transliterations/`) for *ĝiš-nu₁₁*, *ĝiš-nu₁₁ gi₄*, *lu₂ u₆-e*, *igi nu-du₈* (the ordinary Sumerian for "blind", 6.1.01 36 etc. – absent here), *u₄-mu-ul* (this text only), *{lu₂}lil*, *tiru* (also 1.8.1.4 268), *nam-nar* (1.3.1 33; 2.4.2.02 155–162).
- **Comparison:** Atra-ḫasīs I (`../../סיפורי-המבול-המסופוטמיים/1-אתרחסיס/translation-I.rtl.md`) and the corpus renderings (`סַל`, `עָמָל`, `טִיט`); Enūma eliš project renderings (*ušumgallu* = `דְּרָקוֹן־עֲנָק`); texts 1–2 of this project (formulas, *sukkal*, the "good" words, body parts, *a šag₄ ri*).
- **Rank 3, after each line's own parse:** ETCSL's English translation (`translation.md`). As in text 2, the ETCSL translation was opened together with the transliteration at the start (to get the paragraph structure); every line was nevertheless parsed from the morph before its ETCSL rendering was compared, and every departure is recorded under `תרגומים`.
- **Not available, not used:** Benito 1969 (the edition), Klein 1997 (COS 1.159), Jacobsen 1987, Römer 1993, Bottéro & Kramer 1989, Ceccarelli 2016 (*Enki und Ninmaḫ*, the new edition with the NA bilingual), Kilmer 1976 ("Speculations on Umul, the first baby"; cited from general knowledge only, in the note to 88). No pirate copies were searched for.

### Decisions
1. **Aspect** (project Decisions 4): narrative marû → participle (9–11, 13–15, 27, 46–48, 52, 96–97); ḫamṭu → past; marû in speech → future (32); *ga-* → cohortative (57, 82); *ḫe₂-*/*ḫa-* → jussive (23, 33, 36, 37, 94, 132–134, 136, 139); *u₃-* → `אַחֲרֵי שֶׁ…` (22–23, 28, 31–32, 37).
2. **Formulas of text 2 followed** (renderings §4, Text 2): *X Y-ra gu₃ mu-na-de₂-e* = `X אֶל Y פּוֹנֶה:` (29, 53, 80, 84, 92, 112; with `(הוּא)` where X is implicit); *X Y mu-na-ni-ib-gi₄-gi₄* = `X מֵשִׁיב לְY:` (56, 102, 129; 100 with *inim* = `דָּבָר … מְשִׁיבָה`); *nam tar* = `גָּזַר גּוֹרָל` throughout (3, 37, 51, 55, 57, 61, 64, 74, 78, 81–82, 93–94, 103–108).
3. **The six creatures** (59–78, 102–108) in fixed formulas, verified by script (identical Hebrew wherever the Sumerian is identical; brackets aside): ordinal – description – `עִצְּבָה`; `אֶנְקִי, כִּרְאוֹתוֹ X –`; `אֶת גּוֹרָלוֹ גָּזַר: …`; in Enki's recital `ל־X – גּוֹרָל גָּזַרְתִּי, לֶחֶם נָתַתִּי;`. Table: `renderings.md` §4, Text 3.
4. **The disabilities are translated as the Sumerian describes them**, never by the biblical term: "משיב את האור?, איש בוהה" (not `עִוֵּר`), "שתי הרגליים שבורות, הרגליים תפוסות" (not `פִּסֵּחַ`), "נוטף מים", "אישה לא יולדת" (not `עֲקָרָה`), "איש אשר בגופו לא הושמה זכרות, לא הושמה ערווה" (not `סָרִיס`), Umul "לדבר אינו יודע" (not `אִלֵּם`). Reason: Sumerian has the plain words (*igi nu-du₈* "blind" exists in ETCSL and is not used here); the text describes. The biblical words are in the notes and footnotes, tagged [equivalent].
5. **ĝar = `שָׂם` only and always** (11, 30, 45, 75–76, 79, 90, 108, 132; passive `הוּשְׂמָה`), so that every `שָׂם` in the Hebrew is a real echo of `מִי שָׂם פֶּה לָאָדָם` (שמות ד:יא) and is tagged. Other "place" verbs got other Hebrew: *ri* = `הֵשִׁית` / `יָצַק`, *sig₉* = `הֵטִיל`, *ĝal₂* = `קַיָּם` / `תְּהִי` / (with an agent) `הֵבִיא לִכְלַל הֲוָיָה`, *gub* (causative) = `הֶעֱמִיד`. `שָׁם` "there" and `שֵׁם` "name" are vocalized.
6. **Creation vocabulary:** *dim₂* = `עִצֵּב` (never `יָצַר`/`בָּרָא`); *mud* = `חוֹלֵל` (verb; the noun "creature" in 30 `הַמְחוֹלָל`); *me-dim₂* = `מִבְנֶה` (= Akk. *binûtu*, corpus `מִבְנֶה`), never `צֶלֶם`/`דְּמוּת`/`תַּבְנִית`; *ĝal₂* with agent (32) = `הֵבִיא לִכְלַל הֲוָיָה` (the corpus rendering of *šubšû*, its Akkadian equivalent; [equivalent] ↔ `וַיִּבְרָא`, Gen 1:27); *kir₃* = `קָרַץ` (= Akk. *karāṣu*, cognate of `קרץ`, Job 33:6); *šar₂* (clay) = `לָשׁ`; *im* = `טִיט`; *saḫar* = `עָפָר`; *du₃* = `בָּנָה` (38, 74, 124–125, 139); *tud* = `יָלַד`.
7. **Toil words as Atra-ḫasīs and the corpus:** *kiĝ₂* = `עֲבוֹדָה`, *dulum* = `עָמָל`, *zub-sig₃* = `סַל`, *keše₂* (a basket on someone) = `קָשַׁר`, *il₂* = `נָשָׂא`; *diĝir šar₂-šar₂* / *diĝir tur-tur* = `הָאֵלִים הָרַבִּים` / `הָאֵלִים הַקְּטַנִּים`.
8. ***lu₂* = `אִישׁ`** for each creature (as `אִישׁ עִוֵּר`, Lev 21:18), not `אָדָם`; *nam-lu₂-ulu₃* = `הָאֱנוֹשׁוּת`. The ordinals (*gi*, *gi₄-bi*, *peš-bi*, *peš-gi*, *peš-peš-gi*, *peš-bal-gi*) = `אֶחָד`, `שֵׁנִי לוֹ`, `שְׁלִישִׁי לוֹ`, `רְבִיעִי`, `חֲמִישִׁית`, `שִׁשִּׁי` – `אֶחָד`, not `רִאשׁוֹן` (no "first man").
9. **85 split by aspect:** the first half (no finite verb) is Enki's instruction, the second half (*mu-un-u₃-tud*, ḫamṭu) narrative past ("ילדה האישה ההיא"); ETCSL has the whole line as speech in the future. Departure recorded in the line.
10. **igi du₈** = `רָאָה`, in the formula `כִּרְאוֹתוֹ` (not `בִּרְאוֹתוֹ`, which contains the letter sequence ב־ר־א; cf. text 2's `תְּחִלָּה` for `בָּרִאשׁוֹנָה`); *igi bar* stays `הִבִּיט` (text 2). Likewise `לֵישֵׁב` (99), not `לָשֶׁבֶת`, whose unvocalized form is the string of the reserved `שָׁבַת`.
11. **Echo control, other choices against a ready biblical word:** *an-ta* = `חֲבֵרָה` (33; not `עֵזֶר`); *te* = `נִגַּשׁ`/`הִגִּישׁ` (58, 96; = Akk. *ṭeḫû*, text 1), not `קָרַב`; *šu gid₂* = `פָּשַׁט יָד` (97; not `שָׁלַח יָד`, Gen 3:22, and not `הוֹשִׁיט`, reserved for *kullu* in Adapa); *tiru* = `*אִישׁ־הֶחָצֵר*` (77; not `סָרִיס`); *sag₉* = `נָאֶה` (54, 57; `טוֹב` only for *dug₃*, as text 2); *sig₁₀* (55) = `מְתַקֶּנֶת?`; *du₇* = `שָׁלֵם` (132; not `תָּמִים`); *palil* = `קְדוּמָה` (17; not `רִאשׁוֹנָה`/`קַדְמוֹנָה`); *u₃* "sleep" = `שֵׁנָה` (never `תַּרְדֵּמָה`); *a-nir* = `נְהִי` (`קִינָה` is *i-lu*, text 2); *ka tar sil* = `שֶׁבַח הִשְׁמִיעַ`, *me-teš₂ i* = `פֹּאַר`, *za₃-mi₂* = `תְּהִלָּה`; *za₃-mi₂ … dug₃* = `תְּהִלָּתְךָ מְתוּקָה` (141; *dug₃* of taste/praise = `מָתוֹק`, as text 2 for water).
12. **Names:** נַמָּה, נִינְמַח (renderings §1); the assisting goddesses as ETCSL reads them: נִין־אִימָּה (as text 2), שׁוּ־זִי־אַנָּה, נִין־מָדָה, נִין־בָּרַג (ETCSL *nin-barag*; the readings Ninšar/Ninniĝina of the literature cannot be checked), נִין־מוּג, שַׁר־שַׁר־גַּבָּה? (*{d}ŠAR.ŠAR.GABA*, "Mumudu"?), נִין־גּוּנָּה; SIG₇-EN SIG₇-ḪI = `אֵלוֹת־הַלֵּדָה?` (26, 32, 46); נַמְתָּר (flood corpus); אוּמוּל (meaning "my day is far" in the notes); אֶנְכּוּם וְנִינְכּוּם; חַל־אַן־כּוּג; חַרָלִי (text 2); הָאֶנְגּוּר; הָאַפְּסוּ; נִיפּוּר; הָאֵכּוּר; *me* = `הַ־מֶה` (vocalized: unvocalized `המה` reads "they").
13. **Emesal** (the women's/goddesses' register: *dim₃-me₂-er*, *du₅-mu*, *na-aĝ₂-*, *ka-na-aĝ₂*, *mu* = *ĝiš*) is noted in the parsing, not marked in the Hebrew.
14. **Deviations from `renderings.md` §2**, by meaning (Changelog there): *inim ĝar* "to complain" = `דָּבָר שָׂם` (11; *inim* = `דָּבָר` kept, the idiom in the note); *zi* as the dying breath (89) = `נֶפֶשׁ` (as text 2, 264); *tuku* by sense: `נִלְקַח` (marriage, 5), `נִקְנָה?` (133), *ĝiš tuku* = `שָׁמַע` (124, 139).

### Open decisions for the user
1. **The disability words (Decision 4):** keep the Sumerian descriptions in the lines (followed), or add the biblical term in parentheses, e.g. `מֵשִׁיב אֶת הָאוֹר?, אִישׁ בּוֹהֶה (= עִוֵּר)`? The second makes the Ex 4:11 / Lev 21 comparison visible in the clean text, but puts biblical words into the translation that the Sumerian does not have.
2. **77 *tiru***: `*אִישׁ־הֶחָצֵר*` (followed; the dictionary sense "courtier") or ETCSL's "eunuch (?)" = `סָרִיס`, which would put Isa 56:3–5 / Esth 2:3 into the line.
3. **74 *du₃***: `לְבֵית הַנָּשִׁים בְּנָאָהּ?` (followed; literal, and it keeps the Gen 16:2 / 30:3 `אִבָּנֶה` overlap visible) or ETCSL's "made her belong to the queen's household" (`שִׁיְּכָהּ`).
4. **85**: the split into instruction + narrative (Decision 9) or ETCSL's single speech in the future.
5. **61 *saĝ lugal-la-ke₄***: `לְרֹאשׁ הַמֶּלֶךְ` (followed; literal, with 1 Sam 28:2 `שֹׁמֵר לְרֹאשִׁי`) or ETCSL's "servant of the king" (`לִמְשָׁרֵת הַמֶּלֶךְ`). The note links *saĝ lugal* to Akk. *ša rēš šarri* → `סָרִיס` (our hypothesis; it would connect creature 1 with creature 6).
6. **133 *tuku***: `נִקְנָה?` (followed, "acquired") – which creates a Hebrew-only overlap with `קָנִיתִי אִישׁ` (Gen 4:1), flagged as weak – or a neutral `אֲשֶׁר יֶשְׁנוֹ`.
7. **mud = `חוֹלֵל`**: the flood corpus uses `חוֹלֵל` for *šakānu* + *abūbu* ("to bring about a flood"). Acceptable (different language, different collocation), or choose another verb for *mud* (`הוֹלִיד` would collide with *tud* = `יָלַד`)?
8. **Buy / borrow Ceccarelli 2016** (the current edition, with the NA bilingual and the manuscripts) – it would settle 8, 22, 23 (*kiĝ₂-sig₁₀* "substitute" vs "meal"), 41, 43 (*na de₅*), 46, 59 (*šu šu₂-šu₂ sa₂-sa₂*), 77 (*tiru*), 87–88, 98 (*{ĝiš}gur₈-u₆*), 113–118 and the end (122–139). Klein 1997 (COS) would give a second modern translation.
9. **Print stub and the combined `translation.rtl.md`** are the coordinator's (not created here).

### Progress log
- **Drafting** (2026-10-17): all 142 entries in order, from the ETCSL transliteration, the ETCSL lemmas and the ORACC lemma+morph of every word; ETCSL concordance searches and ePSD2 lookups for the rare words; the Hebrew of each formula fixed at its first occurrence (29, 56, 59–61) and of the six-creature formulas before drafting 62–108; ETCSL compared after each line's parse; echo check per line, with the distribution of *ĝar*, *dim₂*, *tud*, *nam tar*, *ninda šum₂*, *igi du₈*, *gub*, *te* over the whole text.
- **Review (a) fidelity** (every `>` line against the transliteration, word by word; brackets by root): **the whole draft had been written with full niqqud** against the working rule – all 142 lines were rewritten by hand in plene spelling, unvocalized, keeping niqqud only on names at first occurrence and on ambiguous forms (`שָׂם`/`שָׁם`/`שֵׁם`, `יָדְךָ`/`יָדֵךְ`, `אַתְּ`, `הֶעֱמִידוֹ`, `רְחָצוֹ`, `בְּנָאָהּ`, `כְּאָב`, `שֵׁכָר`, `כָּלָה`, `לִדְבַר`/`לְדַבֵּר`, `לְהָרִים`, `הֵרִים` …); 64 `לוֹ` → `(לו)` (no dative in the Sumerian); 136 *ud* was untranslated → `ביום?`; 41 `שלָּך` → `שֶׁלָּךְ`; brackets re-checked at 1–3, 20, 63, 65–67, 90 (`[ראשו]`: *saĝ* broken), 95, 99, 106–108, 112, 126; 127 left unbracketed (*lu₂* restored, the root *kar* preserved).
- **Review (b) consistency** (repeated phrases compared as strings by script, niqqud ignored: 56 = 102 = 129; 1 ≈ 2; 59/60, 62/63/104, 66/67/105, 69/70/106, 72/73/107, 75/76/108; 21/81/93/101; 31/58; 54/57; 103–105 – all identical except brackets and where the Sumerian differs: 81 vs 93 (*-še₃*, *nam-bi* vs *nam*), 103 *šu sig* vs 59 *šu šu₂-šu₂*). Against texts 1–2 and the renderings: `בִּרְאוֹתוֹ` → `כִּרְאוֹתוֹ` (14 occurrences, letters ב־ר־א), `לָשֶׁבֶת` → `לֵישֵׁב` (string of `שבת`), forms of `עִצֵּב` written plene `עיצבה` (vs `עצבה` "sorrow"), the gu₃ de₂ / gi₄-gi₄ formulas identical to text 2, *ba* = `חָלַק` (text 2) and therefore *ḫal* = `פִּזֵּר`, *ušumgal* = `דְּרָקוֹן־עֲנָק` (project 1). Noted, not changed: *la₂* = `שָׁקַל` here, Adapa's *wabālu* Št = `שָׁקַל` (different languages, different senses).
- **Review (c) Hebrew** (read aloud): the long formula lines kept close to the Sumerian order; 11 `דבר שָׂמִים` kept (literal; the idiom `שָׂם דָּבָר בְּ־`, 1 Sam 22:15, is in the note); 22, 41, 98 left literal with `?` rather than smoothed; 77 `שֵׁם` vocalized so that it is not read `שָׂם`.
- **Checks:** `python3 -I verify.py 3` → OK (142 entries = ETCSL's line list; translation = annotated `>` lines). `tools/check_bible_quotes.py` on the annotated file, the translation, this file and `renderings.md` → 0 problems (after fixing 7 quotations in the draft: Gen 1:2 `תְהוֹם` without article, Gen 1:26, 2 Sam 9:13 `פִסֵּחַ`, Lev 15:13 `בְּמַיִם חַיִּים`, Dan 1:3, Lev 21:23, and one paraphrase taken for a quotation). Reserved words (renderings §3) in the `>` lines: none; `עָפָר` (10, *saḫar*), `בָּנָה` (38, 74, 124–125, *du₃*), `שֵׁם קָרָא` (77, *mu sa₄*), `נִלְקְחוּ` (5, *tuku*), `שָׂם` (the *ĝar* lines) – each its word's meaning, each tagged.

| 42 | *ĝiš-nu₁₁ mi-ni-in-il₂* | אור הרים | `יְהִי אוֹר` (בראשית א:ג) | [divergence] | "Light" next to "mankind" in a broken line; perhaps the newborn raised to the light. Weak. |

| 32 | *me-dim₂ u₃-mu-e-ni-ĝal₂* | אחרי שתביאי לכלל הוויה את המבנה | `וַיִּבְרָא אֱלֹהִים אֶת הָאָדָם` (בראשית א:כז) | [equivalent] | *ĝal₂* with an agent = Akk. *bašû* Š (*šubšû*), the corpus's closest match to `בָּרָא`; here said of man's form, by the mother goddess. |

| 1 | *ud re-a … an ki* | בימים ההם, בימים אשר שמיים וארץ [עוצבו] | `בְּיוֹם עֲשׂוֹת יְהוָה אֱלֹהִים אֶרֶץ וְשָׁמָיִם` (בראשית ב:ד) | [equivalent] | The creation-time "day(s)" opening; *dim₂* "fashion" (restored), not a creation verb of Genesis. |

Bread = livelihood and a place in the divine order, in both. |
