### Proposals for the shared renderings
For the corpus `renderings.md` (and for texts 4–7, which `renderings.md` §4 already binds):
1. **Sum. *ĝar* = `שָׂם`, reserved** in all Sumerian texts (and Akk. *šakānu* = `שָׂם`, as Adapa already has it), so that `שׂים` in any Hebrew line is a real echo of `מִי שָׂם פֶּה לָאָדָם` (Ex 4:11), `וַיָּשֶׂם שָׁם אֶת הָאָדָם` (Gen 2:8) etc., and can be tagged. Other "put" verbs: *ri* = `הֵשִׁית`/`יָצַק`, *sig₉* = `הֵטִיל`, *ĝal₂* = `קַיָּם`/`הָיָה`.
2. **Sumerian creation vocabulary** (add to §2): *mud* = `חוֹלֵל` (noun `הַמְחוֹלָל`); *me-dim₂* = `מִבְנֶה` (= Akk. *binûtu*); *ĝal₂* with an agent = `הֵבִיא לִכְלַל הֲוָיָה` (= *šubšû*); *kir₃* (clay) = `קָרַץ` (= *karāṣu*); *šar₂* (clay) = `לָשׁ`; *nam-kug-zu* = `חָכְמָה` (= *nēmequ*); *ĝeštug₂ daĝal* = `רְחַב הַבִּינָה` (= *rapša uzni*).
3. **Toil** (complete §2, *kin*/*dulum*/*dub-sig*): *zub-sig₃* is the same word as *dub-sig* (= `סַל`); *keše₂* (basket on someone) = `קָשַׁר`; *il₂* (toil) = `נָשָׂא`; *diĝir šar₂-šar₂* / *diĝir tur-tur* = `הָאֵלִים הָרַבִּים` / `הָאֵלִים הַקְּטַנִּים`.
4. **Verbs of seeing and approaching:** *igi du₈* = `רָאָה` (formula `כִּרְאוֹתוֹ`), *igi bar* = `הִבִּיט` (text 2), *u₆ e* = `הִתְבּוֹנֵן` (participle `בּוֹהֶה` for a blind stare); *te* = `נִגַּשׁ` / `הִגִּישׁ` (= *ṭeḫû*); *šu gid₂* = `פָּשַׁט יָד`.
5. **Court and fate:** *igi lugal gub* = `עָמַד לִפְנֵי הַמֶּלֶךְ` (↔ 1 Sam 16:21); *saĝ lugal* = `רֹאשׁ הַמֶּלֶךְ`; *tiru* = `אִישׁ־חָצֵר`; *e₂-mi₂* = `בֵּית הַנָּשִׁים`; *ninda šum₂* = `נָתַן לֶחֶם`; *nam-nar* = `נְגִינָה`, *nar* = `מְנַגֵּן`.
6. **Disabilities and the body:** translate the Sumerian description, never the biblical term (`עִוֵּר`, `פִּסֵּחַ`, `אִלֵּם`, `חֵרֵשׁ`, `עֲקָרָה`, `סָרִיס` are not used unless the original has the plain word – e.g. *igi nu-du₈* "blind" would be `עִוֵּר`, tagged). Add: *a-za-ad* = `גֻּלְגֹּלֶת`, *mur* = `רֵאָה`, *lipiš* = `מֵעַיִם`, *murgu₂* = `גַּב`, *zag-še* = `כָּתֵף`, *gig* = `חוֹלֶה`; *ĝiš₃*/*gal₄-la* as text 2.
7. **Names** (§1): נִין־אִימָּה, שׁוּ־זִי־אַנָּה, נִין־מָדָה, נִין־בָּרַג, נִין־מוּג, נִין־גּוּנָּה, אוּמוּל, אֶנְכּוּם וְנִינְכּוּם, חַל־אַן־כּוּג, הָאֵכּוּר; *me* = `הַ־מֶה` (always vocalized); *{d}ama-{d}inana* = `הָאֵלוֹת` (vocalized at first occurrence: `אָלוֹת` "curses").
8. **Speech words:** *ka tar sil* = `שֶׁבַח הִשְׁמִיעַ`; *me-teš₂ i* = `פֵּאֵר`; *za₃-mi₂ dug₃* = `תְּהִלָּה מְתוּקָה`; *a-nir* = `נְהִי`; *inim ĝar* = `שָׂם דָּבָר` (= complain).
9. **Traps (guidelines §6.7):** string collisions with reserved words in unvocalized Hebrew – `בראותו` (ב־ר־א; use `כראותו`), `לשבת` "to sit" (= `שבת`; use `לֵישֵׁב`), `עצבה` (sorrow) vs `עיצבה` (fashioned): write plene; `לשׁ` vs `לֵשׁ`. And *ĝiš* in Emesal is *mu*: *ĝiš tuku* "to hear" can appear as *mu tuku*.

### Lessons for the guidelines
- **Draft unvocalized from the first line.** This session drafted all 142 lines with full niqqud and had to rewrite every line by hand (stripping would have given defective spellings). Writing the Hebrew line plene and unvocalized *first*, and adding niqqud only to names and ambiguous forms, is faster and safer.
- **Grep the unvocalized `>` lines for reserved letter sequences, not only for reserved words:** `ברא` inside `בראותו`, `שבת` inside `לשבת`. A reader who searches the translation for `ברא` finds them. Add a check to `verify.py`-type scripts.
- **Track one verb of the biblical comparison verse through the whole original.** Here *ĝar* = `שׂים` (Ex 4:11 `שָׂם`, `יָשׂוּם`): reserving `שָׂם` for *ĝar* showed eight occurrences, among them "mouth … not put" (90), "organs not put" (75) and "who will put?" (132) – invisible in the English translations ("put into its mouth", "with neither penis nor vagina", "who can oppose").
- **Describe defects as the original describes them.** Sumerian has the plain word for "blind" (*igi nu-du₈*), and the text does not use it; an English or Hebrew translation that writes "blind" hides the fact that the text describes (and creates the Lev 21 / Ex 4 echo by itself). Tag the biblical term in the apparatus.
- **ORACC merges ETCSL's "1 ms. has instead" variants into unnumbered `None` lines**; separate them with the ETCSL XML before parsing.
- **When a rank-3 translation puts a whole line into a speech, check the aspect of each verb**: a ḫamṭu verb at the end of an instruction may be the narrative of its execution (85).

## Text 4: The Song of the Hoe

### Scope
- **Text:** *The Song of the Hoe* (*The Creation of the Hoe*), Sumerian, Old Babylonian school text (19th–18th cent. BCE); ETCSL 5.5.4 = ORACC Q000778. The whole composite: **110 entries** (ETCSL 1–109 and 18A), all translated. 18A is an alternative line of one manuscript (in place of 18), tagged `{כ״י אחד}`; the other "1 ms. has instead" variants (3, 6, 7, 15, 18, 30, 38, 50, 53, 64, 66, 78, 80, 95) are translated in the apparatus.
- **Manuscripts:** 88 OB tablets and fragments (ETCSL header), mostly from Nippur (CBS, N numbers), with Ur, Louvre (AO 7087, AO 8898) and British Museum (BM 80170) pieces – the best-attested text of this project; one damaged line (95 var.).
- **Files:** `annotated-4-Hoe.rtl.md` (Hebrew header; 15 sections following ETCSL's translation paragraphs; per line: ETCSL transliteration (verified by script to equal `transliteration.md`), Hebrew, parsing with the ORACC morph and a `**משחק מלים:**` note for every *al* pun, ETCSL + departures, parallels with tags, notes); `translation-4-Hoe.rtl.md` (introduction incl. a paragraph on the puns, the same 15 sections, 16 footnotes). The clean file is generated from the annotated `>` lines by a scratch script (session scratchpad, not kept); `python3 -I verify.py 4` → OK.
- **Content:** Enlil, the lord whose decrees cannot be changed, hastens to separate heaven from earth so that "the seed of the Land" may come out of the earth and "heads" may grow at Uzuea / Uzumua; he hangs the *bulug* ("pin, axis"?) in Duranki (1–7). With the hoe he makes daylight come out, fixes the work quota and the wages of hoe and basket, praises and crowns the gold-and-lapis hoe (8–17). At Uzuea he drives the hoe into the ground, puts "the head of mankind" into the brick mould, and his Land breaks through the earth towards him (18–20). He looks favourably on the black-headed; the Anuna ask him for them; Ninmena sets births going; Nunamnir calls them by name and assigns them to provide the gods' rations; Enki praises the hoe, Nisaba is set over the decisions, and the hoe is in people's hands (21–34). The hoe builds the temples: Ekur and Tummal in Nippur (35–42), the Abzu of Enki (43–45), Keš of Ninḫursaĝa (46–48), Eana of Inana, Zabalam, Utu (49–55), Nisaba's Eḫamun (56–58); Ninurta brings hoe-work into the rebel lands; Šara receives weapons and the hoe; Gibil refines it in fire (59–70); the drumsticks of Ĝeštinana; the hoe buries the dead and brings the dead up; Gilgameš (71–82); a list of things whose names contain *al* in heaven, on earth and in the mountains (83–93); the hoe's works: brick mould, strength of youth, city and house building, fields subdued, weeds uprooted (94–106); praise of the hoe and of Nisaba (107–109).

### Sources consulted
- **Rank 1:** ETCSL 5.5.4 (`../../sources/ETCSL/5.5.4--Song-of-the-Hoe/`: `transliteration.md`, `c.5.5.4.xml` with ETCSL's lemmas and variant notes, README).
- **Rank 2:** ORACC Q000778 (`../../sources/ORACC/epsd2-literary--Q000778--Song-of-the-Hoe/Q000778--lemmatised-morph.md`): citation form and `{morph;base}` of every word, used for ḫamṭu/marû and the prefix chains in every line (ORACC's unnumbered `None` lines separated with the ETCSL XML). ePSD2 glossary (`gloss-sux.json` in `../../sources/ORACC/_bulk/epsd2-literary.zip`, read in place) for *al*, *al-tar*, *ušub*, *kindu*/*kindar*, *bulug*, *bad*, *dar*, *ḫuĝ*, *kal*, *sa₄* (*še*), *zid*, *immal*, *altirigu*, *argibil*, *alĝarsur*, *šudum*, *iginim*, *irigal*, *adda*, *šenallu*, *salḫub*, *maltum*, *mayaltum*, *nesaĝ*, *šukur*, *massu*, *gud-si-AŠ*, *bisaĝ*, *dag*, *agrun*, *ḫulĝal*, *usag*, *numun*/*numun₂*, *ḫirin*, *dub₂*, *zir*. ETCSL bulk concordance (`../../sources/ETCSL/_bulk/etcsl-unzipped/etcsl/transliterations/`) for *uzu-e₃-a* / *uzu-mu₂-a* (this text only), ***ki … dar*** (a plant verb: 2.4.2.16 10, 4.13.04 1–2, 5.6.3 64, 6.1.03 81; of people "like grass": **1.1.4 3**, Enki's Journey), *saĝ šum₂* "hasten" (1.6.2 162 etc.), *an ki-ta bad* (1.8.1.4 8; 1.8.2.1 1), *giri₁₇ šu ĝal₂* (prayer gesture: 1.6.1 106; 1.8.2.3 250), *u₃-šub* (1.1.3 335–338, Kulla; 2.1.7, Gudea), *igi piriĝ* (1.3.1 11, 23), *a₂ si sa₂*, *bulug la₂*, *ki-in-du*, *ki-in-dar*, *kindagal*.
- **Comparison:** SEAL KAR 4 (`../../sources/SEAL/KAR-4/`) line 24 "In Uzumua, the bond of heaven and earth" (for text 7); texts 1–3 of this project (formulas, *ĝar*, *ušumgal*, *sukkal*, *za₃-mi₂*, *igi bar*, *ki*); corpus and flood renderings; the commentary on Gen 1:6–8 (`../../../../פירוש/1-בראשית/1010-…`, the "day 2" section on `רָקִיעַ` / `וַיַּבְדֵּל`), Gen 2:4–17 (1020) and 2:18–3:24 (1030).
- **Rank 3, after each line's own parse:** ETCSL's English translation (`translation.md`). As in texts 2–3, ETCSL's paragraph ranges were read at the start (for the sections); every line was parsed from the morph before its ETCSL rendering was compared, and every departure is recorded under `תרגומים`.
- **Not available, not used:** Farber 1997 (COS 1.157, the standard translation), Farber 2001 (electronic composite and translation, listed in ETCSL's bibliography), Farber 1999, Civil 1994 (on 79 and 85), Pettinato 1971, Wilcke 1972, Kramer 1944, Edzard 2000. Their readings (e.g. *ki-in-dar* in 17) are mentioned only as "readings in the literature, not checked". No pirate copies were searched for.

### Decisions
1. **Aspect** (project Decisions 4): narrative marû → participle (10, 16, 20, 22, 24–25, 27, 29, 30–31, 36, 40, 42, 45, 53, 61–63, 65, 99, 103–105); ḫamṭu → past; nonfinite *-ed-e* → `כדי ש…` / infinitive (3–6, 26, 53); nonfinite *-a* → relative clause or participle. **29** *mu-ni-ib-sa₄-sa₄* is taken as marû (the *-b-* before the stem; no suffix written) against ETCSL's past – noted.
2. **The puns on *al* are not imitated.** Every line is rendered by its meaning; every pun is named in the parsing (`**משחק מלים:**`, about 45 lines) and described in the introduction of both files. Names and objects that exist for their *al* (83–93) are given as class word + transliterated name (`גרזן־הדורלוב`, `רשת־האלוחב`, `קערת־המלתום` …), as text 2 did with the plant names, so that the *al* is visible. The one natural Hebrew sound-play, `אַלּוֹן` for *allanum* (91, [cognate] with Akk. *allānu*), is used because it is the meaning; none is forced elsewhere.
3. ***{ĝiš}al* = `מַעְדֵּר`** (Isa 7:25 `בַּמַּעְדֵּר יֵעָדֵרוּן`), every occurrence. ***u₃-šub* = `מַלְבֵּן`** in its biblical sense (2 Sam 12:31 qere; Jer 43:9; Nah 3:14), not `תַּבְנִית` (reserved for *tamšīlu*); ***dusu* = `סַל`** (renderings §2); ***pisaĝ* = `טֶנֶא`** (38), to keep `סַל` for the work basket.
4. **Separation of heaven and earth (4–5):** *an ki-ta bad* = `להפריד את השמיים מן הארץ` (§2), *saĝ šum₂* = `מיהר`; `הִבְדִּיל` and `רָקִיעַ` occur nowhere in the Hebrew lines (grep).
5. ***ki* = `הָאָרֶץ`** for earth, ground, soil (3, 20, 63, 74–75, 84; text 2 Decision 9), ***kalam* = `הָאָרֶץ`** (3, 20); the apparatus says which word stands behind `הָאָרֶץ`. **`אֲדָמָה` is never used**, also not in 18–20 where the hoe has dug the ground (the renderings allow it for tilled ground): here it would manufacture the `אָדָם` / `אֲדָמָה` link of Gen 2:7 at the very line that is compared with it. The cost – `ארצו את הארץ בוקעת` (20), `זרע הארץ מן הארץ` (3) – is accepted and explained.
6. **Uzuea / Uzumua:** ETCSL's composite reads *uzu-e₃-a* "(where) flesh came forth" in 6 and 18 (Uzumua is the variant of 2 mss. and 1 ms., and 18A); we follow the edition: `אוּזוּאֵאָה`, with Uzumua `אוּזוּמוּאָה` in the variants and 18A, and both meanings in the notes. Written unhyphenated so that it is not read as "Uzu" + the god Ea (`אֵאָה`).
7. **Verbs of coming out and growing:** *e₃* = `יָצָא` / `הוֹצִיא` (3, 8, 15, 30, 53), *ed₃* = `עָלָה` (variants of 3 and 30), *mu₂* = `צָמַח` (6, 36, intransitive), *ki dar* = `בָּקַע (את הארץ)` (20), *pa e₃* = `הוֹפִיעַ` (1, = Akk. *šūpû*, project 1). Each overlap with Gen 1:11–12, 1:24, 2:5–9 is tagged.
8. ***ĝar* = `שָׂם` only and always** (text 3 Decision 5), including the idioms *ki ĝar* "found" (35, 37: `הוּשַׂם`), *šu-še₃ ĝar* "subdue" (61, 106: `לַיָּד שָׂם`), *gu₂ ĝar* "submit" (102–103: `צוואר שָׂם`), *saĝ ĝar* "oppose" (18A); always vocalized. *ĝal₂* with an agent placing an object = `נָתַן` (17); intransitive = `קַיָּם` / nominal clause (23, 34, 90, 96). Text 3's `הֵבִיא לִכְלַל הֲוָיָה` (*ĝal₂* bringing into existence) is not used: in 96 *ĝal₂* has no agent (ETCSL's "has made people exist" is causative – noted).
9. ***al-tar* "construction work" = `הַבְּנִיָּה`** (45, 48, 54–55, 57–58, 60; 44 `בַּעַל הַבְּנִיָּה?`), *eš₂-gar₃* = `מִכְסָה` (9). **No noun or verb of the root עבד in the Hebrew lines**: the root is the Gen 2:5 / 2:15 / 3:23 / 4:2 comparison, and *al-tar*, seven times, would have created a dense false echo (the first draft had `עבודת הבניין`; changed in review (b)).
10. **Deviations by meaning from `renderings.md` §2** (Changelog there): *du₃* = `קָבַע` (9, the work quota) and `נָעַץ` (18, the hoe driven into the ground), `בָּנָה` elsewhere (36, 45, 98–99, 38 var.); *lugal* of a field = `בְּעָלִים` (101–103; `מֶלֶךְ` in 26, 59); *a₂* by sense (`שָׂכָר` 10, `זְרוֹעַ` 14, `כֹּחַ` 97); *ud* by sense (`אוֹר הַיּוֹם` 8, 47; `יוֹם` 36, 59; `סְעָרָה` 62).
11. **Naming:** *sa₄* (29, without *mu*) = `קָרָא בְּשֵׁם` (`קָרָא` reserved for naming, §2); tagged ↔ Gen 5:2, 2:19–20, Ex 31:2.
12. **Identical Sumerian, identical Hebrew** (checked by script, niqqud ignored): 4/5 (mirror), 11/32, 40/42, 48/58 (and 54), *{ĝiš}al-e kug-ga-am₃* in 49/69, *šu-še₃ al-ĝa₂-ĝa₂* in 61/106, 101–103, *al-du₃-e* (36, 45, 99), *al-ĝa₂-ĝa₂* (27, 61, 63, 99, 106), *nam … tar* (2, 9, 16, 53, 107), *za₃-mi₂ … dug₄* (11, 32, 108). In 69 this means `במעדר טהור הוא` as in 49, although ETCSL reads 69 "the hoe, sacred indeed".
13. **Untranslated by ETCSL → `…`:** 17 *ki-in-du* (ePSD2 "earth"; the reading *ki-in-dar* "crevice" of the literature not checked); 81 *ud nu₂-a*. Uncertain items marked `?` in the line: 7 `היתד?`, 14 `יתדו?`, 18A, 43 `הלביא?`, 44, 47 `האדיר?`, 63 `(ליד?)`, 71 `מקלות־התוף?`, 79 `רב־הספרים?`, 89 `ארון־הארגיביל?`.
14. **Names and letter traps:** Šara = `שַׁרָא` (always vocalized, never "Sarah"); Keš = `כֵּשׁ` (always vocalized); Utu always vocalized; Nergal = `נֵרְגַל` (= 2 Kgs 17:30); "the women's house **in** Tummal" = `בית הנשים אשר בתומל` (not `שבתומל`, which contains `שבת`); *dub-saĝ* "foremost" = `רִאשׁוֹן` (not `בָּרֹאשׁ`, letters ב־ר־א); 61 *nu-še-ga* = `אינה שומעת` (not `נשמעת`, letters נשמ, cf. `נִשְׁמַת חַיִּים`); *piriĝ* = `לָבִיא` (so that `אַרְיֵה` stays *ur-maḫ*); *irigal* = `הַשְּׁאוֹל` (lit. "the great city").

### Open decisions for the user
1. **Uzuea or Uzumua in 6 and 18** (Decision 6): follow ETCSL's composite (*uzu-e₃-a*, followed) or put the familiar Uzumua (the variant; KAR 4 24) into the lines.
2. ***ki* = `הָאָרֶץ`, never `אֲדָמָה`** (Decision 5): keep (followed; `ארצו את הארץ בוקעת`, 20), or use `הַקַּרְקַע` for *ki* in 20 (clearer Hebrew, but hides the [equivalent] wording of Gen 1:12/1:24/2:6 `מִן הָאָרֶץ`), or `הָאֲדָמָה` in 18–20 as "tilled ground" (allowed by the renderings, but it would put the Gen 2:7 word into the line).
3. ***al-tar* = `הַבְּנִיָּה`** (followed) or `חֲפִירָה` ("hoe-work", closer to *al* + *tar* "hoe-cutting"; ETCSL varies between "construction work" and "working with the hoe" (60)).
4. **29** *mu-ni-ib-sa₄-sa₄*: marû (`בשם הוא קורא`, followed) or ETCSL's past ("named"); and whether *saĝ zid saĝ kal-la* are the names given ("'True head', 'Precious head'") or the persons named (followed, with ETCSL).
5. **24** *ḫuĝ*: "pacify" (ORACC; ETCSL's translation "calmed"; followed) or "hire" (ETCSL's own lemma), which would link 24 with the wages of 10 and the workers of 25–31.
6. **69 = 49** (identical Hebrew for identical Sumerian, followed) or ETCSL's "he caused the hoe, sacred indeed, to be refined with fire".
7. **74** *irigal* = `הַשְּׁאוֹל` (followed) or the literal `הָעִיר הַגְּדוֹלָה`.
8. **Buy / borrow Farber 1997 (COS 1.157)**, and look for Farber 2001 (electronic edition) and Civil 1994: they would settle 7 (*bulug*), 14, 17 (*kindu* / *kindar*), 43, 71, 79 (*kindagal*), 81, 85 (*dur₁₀-al-lub* / *šen-al-lu₅*), 89, and the identification of the *al*-objects.
9. **Print stub and the combined `translation.rtl.md`** are the coordinator's (not created here).

### Progress log
- **Drafting** (2026-10-17): all 110 entries in order, from the ETCSL transliteration, the ETCSL lemmas (scratch extraction from the XML) and the ORACC lemma+morph of every word; ETCSL concordance searches (*ki dar*, *an ki-ta bad*, *saĝ šum₂*, *giri₁₇ šu ĝal₂*, *u₃-šub*, *uzu-mu₂-a*, *igi piriĝ*, *ki-in-du/dar*, *kindagal*) and ePSD2 lookups for the rare words; the Hebrew of each repeated phrase fixed at its first occurrence; ETCSL's translation compared after each line's parse; echo check per line, with the distribution of *e₃*, *mu₂*, *dar*, *ĝar*, *du₃*, *tud*, *nam tar*, *al-tar*, *saĝ* over the whole text. Drafted unvocalized and plene, niqqud only on names at their first occurrence and on ambiguous forms (`שָׂם`, `שְׁמוֹ`, `כַּר`, `תִּלִּים`, `הֶחֱיְתָה`, `נָעַץ`, `מָנָה` …). The transliterations were then compared by script with `transliteration.md` (identical).
- **Review (a) fidelity** (every `>` line against the transliteration and the morph, word by word): 9 `מכסת העבודה` → `המכסה` (*eš₂-gar₃* is one word; "work" was double); 59 `יומו (בו) עבר` → `היום (עמו) עבר` (no possessive in *ud zal-la*); 18A `באוזוּמוּאָה` → `בְּאוּזוּמוּאָה` and 19 `במַלְבֵּן` → `בַּמַּלְבֵּן` (first-occurrence vocalization, article); 47 `בְּכֵּשׁ` → `בְּכֵשׁ`; 55 Utu unvocalized → `אוּתוּ`; ETCSL imports recorded (3 "human seed", 20 "started to", 27 "human reproduction", 94 "everything", 96 causative "made exist"). Brackets: the composite has no restored words; the one damaged sign (95 var. *⸢šabra⸣*) is in the apparatus.
- **Review (b) consistency** (repeated phrases compared as strings by script, Decision 12; the Hebrew of every recurring word checked against the `>` lines of texts 2–3 and the renderings): *al-tar* `עבודת הבניין` (7 lines) → `הבנייה` (root עבד, Decision 9); 32 punctuation aligned with 11; confirmed against texts 2–3: *sukkal* `וזיר`, *igi bar* `הביט`, *ki-sikil* `עלמה`, *maš₂* `גדי`, *gud* `שור`, *ĝeštug₂ daĝal* `רחב(ת) הבינה`, *bur₁₂* `עקר`, *dab₅* `תפס`, *na-nam* `אכן`, *dug₃* (sound) `מתוק`, *ušumgal* `דרקון־ענק`, *tuš* `ישב`, *gub* `העמיד`. Found, not changed (flagged for the coordinator): *numun₂* is `אַסְפֶּסֶת?` in text 3 (43) and `חַלְפָה` here (ETCSL's translation "esparto" = Arabic *ḥalfa*); *za₃-mi₂* is `תהלה` in text 2 (281) and `תהילה` in text 3 and here.
- **Review (c) Hebrew** (read aloud; letter-sequence grep of the unvocalized lines): 37, 39 `שבתומל` → `אשר בתומל` (string `שבת`); 61 `נשמעת` → `שומעת` (string `נשמ`); 78 kept `ראשון` (not `בראש`); 1 transitive `הוֹפִיעַ` as in Enūma eliš; the terse name-lists 83–93 kept as nominal clauses; 20 and 3 kept with the doubled `הארץ` (Decision 5). Reserved words (§3) in the `>` lines: none; `שָׂם` only for *ĝar*; `טוֹבָה` once (95, *dug₃*); `צמח`/`יצא`/`בקע`/`קרא בשם`/`בנה` each its word's meaning, each tagged.
- **Checks:** `python3 -I verify.py 4` → OK (110 entries = ETCSL's line list; translation = annotated `>` lines). `tools/check_bible_quotes.py` on the annotated file, the translation, this file and `renderings.md` → 0 problems (after fixing 2 in the draft: Isa 7:25 `מַעְדֵּר` without the article's dagesh → `בַּמַּעְדֵּר יֵעָדֵרוּן`; 2 Sam 12:31 ketiv/qere `בַּמַּלְכֵּן (בַּמַּלְבֵּן)`).

### Parallels index (Song of the Hoe)

| Line | Original | Hebrew rendering | Bible | Tag | Comment |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 4–5 | *an … ki* (the pair) | השמיים … הארץ | `בְּרֵאשִׁית בָּרָא אֱלֹהִים אֵת הַשָּׁמַיִם וְאֵת הָאָרֶץ` (בראשית א:א) | [equivalent] | The first act concerns "heaven and earth" as a pair; the Sumerian story opens with their separation. |
| 1 | *niĝ₂-du₇-e pa … e₃* | את הדבר הראוי הוֹפִיעַ | `וַיְהִי אוֹר` (בראשית א:ג) | [divergence] | The opening act is "making the fitting thing appear" (*pa e₃* = *šūpû*), not a word. ETCSL "the world" for *niĝ₂-du₇* is interpretive. |
| 8 | *{ĝiš}al-e mu-un-ĝar ud al-e₃* | במעדר (זאת) שָׂם – אור היום יצא | `וַיִּקְרָא אֱלֹהִים לָאוֹר יוֹם` (בראשית א:ה) | [divergence] | Daylight comes out after an act with the hoe (*al-e₃*, a pun). |
| 4–5 | *an ki-ta bad*; *ki an-ta bad*; *saĝ šum₂* | להפריד את השמיים מן הארץ / את הארץ מן השמיים – אכן גם מיהר | `וַיַּבְדֵּל בֵּין הַמַּיִם אֲשֶׁר מִתַּחַת לָרָקִיעַ וּבֵין הַמַּיִם אֲשֶׁר מֵעַל לָרָקִיעַ` (בראשית א:ז) | [equivalent] | **Who:** Enlil, the lord of Nippur (not An, not by combat); **how:** "hastily", by "opening / removing far" (*bad*); **between what:** heaven and earth, no waters, no firmament; **what for:** so that the seed may come out of the earth and heads grow (3, 6). Rendered `הפריד`, never `הבדיל`. The same motif opens ETCSL 1.8.1.4 and 1.8.2.1. |
| 7 | *dur-an-ki-ka bulug nam-mi-in-la₂* | בדור־אנקי את היתד? אכן תלה | — (בראשית א:ו–ח) | [absent] | After the separation a "pin / axis" (*bulug*, ETCSL "axis of the world") is hung at Duranki, "the bond of heaven and earth"; Genesis has no link between the separated parts. |
| 3 | *numun kalam-ma ki-ta ed₂-de₃* | כדי שזרע הארץ מן הארץ ייצא | `וַתּוֹצֵא הָאָרֶץ דֶּשֶׁא עֵשֶׂב מַזְרִיעַ זֶרַע` (בראשית א:יב) | [equivalent] | *numun* = `זֶרַע`, *e₃* = `יָצָא`. The seed's coming out of the earth is the **purpose** of the separation (one act), where Genesis has two days (2 and 3). "Seed of the Land" may be plant seed or human seed (6, 20); ETCSL's "human seed" adds "human" (rank-3 import). |

Man rises by himself towards the god above, not taken (`לֻקַּח`) from the ground by the god. *ki* = `הָאָרֶץ`: the adam/adamah pun is Hebrew only. ETCSL's "started to" is not in the Sumerian. |

| 66 | *šita₂ {ĝiš}tukul ti mar-uru₅ {ĝiš}al* | מפץ, נשק, חץ, אשפה, מעדר | `וְכִתְּתוּ חַרְבוֹתָם לְאִתִּים` (ישעיהו ב:ד); `כֹּתּוּ אִתֵּיכֶם לַחֲרָבוֹת` (יואל ד:י) | [equivalent] | The hoe in a list of weapons; the hoe-weapon pair. |
| 69 | *izi nam-mi-in-la₂* | אש אכן העביר בו | `כָּל דָּבָר אֲשֶׁר יָבֹא בָאֵשׁ תַּעֲבִירוּ בָאֵשׁ וְטָהֵר` (במדבר לא:כג) | [equivalent] | Purifying a tool by fire. Our proposal. |
| 76 | *{d}nergal* | נֵרְגַל | `וְאַנְשֵׁי כוּת עָשׂוּ אֶת נֵרְגַל` (מלכים ב יז:ל) | [cognate] | The same divine name. |
| 99 | *e₂ zid* | בית נאמן | `וּבָנִיתִי לוֹ בַּיִת נֶאֱמָן` (שמואל א ב:לה) | [equivalent] | |
| 102 | *gu₂ … ĝar* | צוואר … שָׂם | `וְהַגּוֹי אֲשֶׁר יָבִיא אֶת צַוָּארוֹ בְּעֹל מֶלֶךְ בָּבֶל` (ירמיהו כז:יא) | [equivalent] | Neck as the sign of submission. |

### Proposals for the shared renderings
1. **The hoe and its tools** (all Sumerian texts; Grain and Sheep and KAR 4 will need them): *{ĝiš}al* = `מַעְדֵּר`; *u₃-šub* = `מַלְבֵּן` (never `תַּבְנִית`, reserved for *tamšīlu*); *{ĝiš}dusu* / *dub-sig* = `סַל` (confirmed); *pisaĝ* = `טֶנֶא`; *apin* = `מַחֲרֵשָׁה`; *gud-si-AŠ* = `כַּר`.
2. **Work nouns without the root עבד:** *al-tar* = `הַבְּנִיָּה`; *eš₂-gar₃* = `מִכְסָה`; *a₂* "wages" = `שָׂכָר`. Recommend for the corpus: Sumerian and Akkadian work nouns other than *kiĝ₂* (text 3 `עֲבוֹדָה`) get a non-עבד Hebrew noun, so that every עבד in a Hebrew line is a real counterpart of Gen 2:5/2:15/3:23/4:2 (cf. *dullu* = `עָמָל`).
3. **Emergence verbs:** *ki dar* = `בָּקַע (אֶת הָאָרֶץ)` [divergence ↔ Gen 2:7, 3:19], with the note that it is the Sumerian verb of sprouting (ETCSL 1.1.4 3 has people "like grass"); *mu₂* intransitive = `צָמַח`; *pa e₃* = `הוֹפִיעַ` (= *šūpû*).
4. ***sa₄* without *mu*** = `קָרָא בְּשֵׁם` (`קָרָא` reserved for naming); *mu sa₄* stays `קָרָא שֵׁם`.
5. ***ĝal₂*:** with an agent placing an object = `נָתַן`; intransitive = `קַיָּם` / nominal; `הֵבִיא לִכְלַל הֲוָיָה` only for bringing into existence (text 3, 32). Specify this in §2.
6. **Adjectives:** *zid* = `נֶאֱמָן` (= Akk. *kīnu*), *kal* = `יָקָר`, *kug* = `טָהוֹר` (confirmed); *ḫul* = `רַע`; *dug₃* of sound/name = `מָתוֹק`.
7. **Death:** *irigal* = `הַשְּׁאוֹל` (lit. "the great city"), *ad₆* = `גְּוִיָּה`, *tum₂* = `הֵבִיא`.
8. **Names (§1):** אוּזוּאֵאָה / אוּזוּמוּאָה (KAR 4 = text 7 will need Uzumua), דּוּר־אַנְקִי ("bond of heaven and earth" = Akk. *riksi šamê u erṣeti*), נוּנַמְנִיר, נִין־מֶנָּה, נִינְלִיל, תּוּמַּל, נִין־אִיסִינָה, שׁוּלְפָּאֵה, כֵּשׁ (always vocalized), אֵאַנָּה, זַבָּלָם, אֵחַמוּן, שַׁרָא (always vocalized), גִּיבִּיל, גֶּשְׁתִּין־אַנָּה, נֵרְגַל (= the biblical form), נִינְסוּמוּן.
9. **Harmonize:** *numun₂* = `חַלְפָה` (here) vs `אַסְפֶּסֶת?` (text 3, 43); *za₃-mi₂* plene `תהילה` (texts 3–4) vs `תהלה` (text 2, 281).
10. **Guidelines §6.7 (traps):** prefix + name can create a reserved string (`ש` + `בתומל` = `שבת…`): use `אשר ב…`; `בָּרֹאשׁ` contains ב־ר־א (use `רִאשׁוֹן`); Šara `שרא` ≠ `שרה`; Keš `כֵּשׁ` ≠ `קַשׁ`; a transliterated name that ends in *-e-a* can look like Ea (`אוּזוּאֵאָה`): write it unhyphenated and say so.

### Lessons for the guidelines
- **A poem built on puns:** translate by meaning, name every pun in the parsing (a fixed label, `**משחק מלים:**`), say in both introductions that the poem is built on them, and render name-lists as "class word + transliterated name", so that the sound the pun rests on stays visible. Use a natural Hebrew sound-play only where it is the meaning (`אַלּוֹן`).
- **A key Genesis root can enter through a frequent noun:** *al-tar* "construction work" (7×) drafted as `עבודת הבניין` would have put the root of `לַעֲבֹד אֶת הָאֲדָמָה` seven times into a hoe poem. Grep the `>` lines for the roots of the key comparison verses (עבד, אדמ, צמח, יצא, קרא, שׂים), not only for reserved words.
- **Check a motif with a concordance of its verb:** "humans break through the earth like plants" is not written in the Hoe (20); the ETCSL concordance of *ki dar* shows it is the Sumerian sprouting verb, and finds the explicit "people … like grass" in Enki's Journey 3 – a parallel that strengthens the reading without importing words into the line.
- **ETCSL's lemma and translation can disagree** (24 lemma *ḫuĝ* "hire", translation "calmed"; 36 lemma *ud* "storm", translation "by day"): check both against the context and the ORACC morph, and record the choice.
- **Rank-3 imports in ETCSL here:** "human seed" (3), "started to" (20), "human reproduction" (27), "everything" (94), causative "made people exist" (96). Each would sharpen a Genesis parallel beyond the Sumerian; they are recorded in the parallels index.
- **Distribution of one word across a whole text** found the poem's frame: *saĝ* "head" for the humans growing (6), set in the mould (19), named (29), brought out (30), buried (74), and the hoe "of the head" (96); and *pa* "shoot" from the opening (1, *pa e₃*) to the uprooting (105). Track the key noun as well as the key verb.

## Text 5: The Debate between Grain and Sheep

### Scope
- **Text:** *The Debate between Grain and Sheep* (*Ezina and Lahar*, older *Ašnan and Lahar*), Sumerian, Old Babylonian school text (19th–18th cent. BCE); ETCSL 5.3.2 (ORACC Q000761 is a catalogue entry only, with no text in the 2022 export). The whole composite: **193 entries** (ETCSL 1–193), all translated. ETCSL has no variant lines and no letter-numbered lines for this text; damaged and lost signs are marked as in the transliteration (101, 103, 106, 144–145, 147, 150, 152–154, 173, 176, 183–188, 191).
- **Manuscripts** (ETCSL header): about seventy OB tablets and fragments, mostly from Nippur (CBS, N, Ni, 3N-T, UM), with Ur (UET 6 33–35, 263, 266), Isin (IB 470+), BM 16921 (CT 42 39), the Bodleian, FLP 2628, HS 1458, 1534 and VAT 3467.
- **Files:** `annotated-5-Grain-Sheep.rtl.md` (Hebrew header; 20 sections following ETCSL's translation paragraphs; per line: ETCSL transliteration (copied by script from `transliteration.md`), Hebrew, parsing (ETCSL's TEI lemma + ePSD2 + ORACC `morph` of the same form elsewhere, where found), ETCSL + departures, parallels with tags, notes); `translation-5-Grain-Sheep.rtl.md` (introduction incl. a paragraph on the debate genre, the same 20 sections, 20 footnotes). Both files are generated from one line-by-line data file by a scratch builder (session scratchpad, not kept), so the clean lines equal the annotated `>` lines by construction; `python3 -I verify.py 5` → OK.
- **Content:** the cosmogonic prologue (1–42): when An begets the Anuna on "the mountain of heaven and earth", there is no grain, no weaving, no sheep or goat giving birth; the gods do not know the names Ezina-Kusu and Sheep; there is no barley, no garment, Uttu and Niĝirsi are not born, Šakkan has not gone out to the arid land; the humanity "of those days" does not know eating bread or wearing clothes, goes about "bare-limbed", eats grass with its mouth like sheep and drinks garden water (1–25); Sheep and Grain dwell (ETCSL: "are created") on the Holy Mound, the Anuna eat and drink of them and are not filled, and give them to mankind as sustenance (26–36); Enki proposes to Enlil to bring them down, and they do (37–42). Sheep in the fold and Grain in the furrow bring abundance to the Land; at a feast, after wine and beer, they quarrel (43–70). Four speeches (71–179): Grain (71–91), Sheep (92–115), Grain (116–142), Sheep (143–168), Grain (169–179). Enki's verdict to Enlil: they shall be sisters, but Grain the greater; the owners of silver, cattle and sheep shall sit at the gate of the owner of barley (180–191); colophon formula and praise of Enki (192–193).

### Sources consulted
- **Rank 1:** ETCSL 5.3.2 (`../../sources/ETCSL/5.3.2--Debate-Grain-and-Sheep/`: `transliteration.md`, `c.5.3.2.xml` with ETCSL's lemmas, README). The XML lemmas were extracted per line by a scratch script.
- **Rank 2:** the ePSD2 glossary (`gloss-sux.json` in `../../sources/ORACC/_bulk/epsd2-literary.zip`, read in place) for *se* (plural stem of *til₃*), *sissig*/*sig₇*, *sug₄*, *ĝešgen*, *musar*, *kiʾulutim*, *niĝir-si*, *temen*, *men*, *a₂-dam*, *erin*, *šudul*, *apin*, *zišagŋal*, *sudaŋ*, *kadra*, *lipiš*, *saŋursaŋ*, *zulumhi*, *mulmal za*, *gudug*, *pašeš*, *sugzag gu₇*, *bappir*, *titab*, *šakir*, *ĝišbun*, *nagada*, *engar*, *buniŋ*, *kinkin*, *šagtur*, *niŋdara*, *ban*, *namgur*, *buru*, *ulu*, *mir*, *ummud*, *esir*, *taka*, *adamin*. **ORACC `morph`:** since Q000761 has no text, every verbal form of 5.3.2 was looked up in all ORACC literary texts of the zip (index of 38,343 forms built by a scratch script); 99 of the 198 distinct verbal forms (many of them simple nonfinite forms such as *dug₃-ga*) were found with a `morph` (e.g. *mu-ni-ib-gu₇* `{mu.ni.b:~}` in How Grain came to Sumer, *i-im-na₈-na₈-ne* `{V.m:~;ene}`, *im-ma-da-ra-an-ed₃-de₃* `{Vmma.da.ta.n:~;e}`, *mu-na-ni-ib-gi₄-gi₄* `{mu.na.ni.b:~}`); the rest are parsed by us, and the parsing says so. **ETCSL bulk concordance** (`../../sources/ETCSL/_bulk/etcsl-unzipped/etcsl/transliterations/`) for *ĝiš-gen₆* (1.8.2.1 130; 2.4.4.1 5), *su-bi*, *sug₄*, *udu-gin₇ u₂ ka-ba … gu₇* (**1.7.6, How Grain came to Sumer, 1–3**: the same "like sheep they eat grass" line, with *ud re-a … nu-zu* and An bringing grain down), *ki-ulutim₂* (1.4.4 138, 168 "birthplace"; 2.2.6 11; 4.13.15; 4.22.1), *sig₇* (3), *a-na-zu mu-da-ĝal₂* (this text only), the debate colophon *a-da-min₃ dug₄-ga* (5.3.1 7, 194; 5.3.3 316; 5.3.5 188; **4.08.33 88, Dumuzid and Enkimdu, the shepherd–farmer dispute**).
- **Comparison:** texts 2–4 of this project (formulas *gu₃ de₂* / *gi₄-gi₄*, *ĝar* = `שָׂם`, *nam tar*, *sag₉*/*dug₃*, *ki-sikil*, *ab-sin₂*, *a-gar₃*, *barag*, *banšur*, *ki-nu₂*, *ĝišbun*, *za₃-mi₂*, *ki* = `הָאָרֶץ`), the corpus and flood renderings; the commentary on Gen 2:4–17 (`1020-…`: the garden as "the world of the hunter-gatherers"), 2:18–3:24 (`1030-…`) and 4:1–17 (`1040-…`).
- **Rank 3, after each line's own parse:** ETCSL's English translation (`translation.md`). **Note (honesty):** as in texts 2–4, ETCSL's translation was read once at the start, together with the transliteration (for the paragraph structure); each line was parsed from the lemmas before its ETCSL rendering was compared, and every departure is recorded under `תרגומים`.
- **Not available, not used:** Alster & Vanstiphout 1987 (*ASJ* 9, the edition), Vanstiphout 1997 (COS 1.180), Vanstiphout 1990, 1992; Bottéro 1991; Gragg 1973. No pirate copies were searched for.

### Decisions
1. **Aspect** (project Decisions 4): narrative marû → participle (2 `יולד`, 24–25 `אוכלים`, `שותים`, 31, 34, 42 `מורידים`, 43–46, 56–57, 65–66, 69–70, 98–100, 108, 112, 119, 125, 127, 129–131, 136, 159–160); ḫamṭu → past (3–11, 17–19, 21–23, 27–28, 31b/34b `ולא נמלאו`, 36, 54–55, 60, 64, 67–68, 75, 81, 109, 121, 157, 169); *u₃-* → `אחרי ש…` (39, 60, 67–68, 79, 85–86, 111, 117–118, 126, 148–151, 177–178); *ga-* → cohortative (40 `נורידן`); *ḫe₂-* → jussive (181–189, 191). **Where ORACC has no parsed instance of the form, the aspect is our analysis** and the parsing says so (2, 24, 28, 31, 54, 57, 95). 24 *mu-ni-ib-gu₇*: marû (habitual), because a human plural agent in ḫamṭu would need *-n-…-eš*, and ETCSL itself translates the identical line in 1.7.6 "used to eat".
2. **No `טֶרֶם`.** The negations of 3–25 are plain *nu-* (19 *la-ba-* = *nu-ba-*) with ḫamṭu or nonfinite forms; Sumerian has no "not yet" form here, and ETCSL does not write "not yet" either. Rendered `לא` throughout; the Gen 2:5 `טֶרֶם` row is [divergence] (as text 2 did for its 11–28).
3. **1 *ḫur-saĝ* = `הַר`, not "hill".** The brief (and ETCSL) speak of "the hill of heaven and earth"; the Sumerian of line 1 is *ḫur-saĝ* "mountain (range)", and *du₆* "mound, tell" (`תֵּל`) comes only from 27 on (*du₆ kug* = `הַתֵּל הַטָּהוֹר`).
4. **2 *ud … -a-ba* = `בַּיּוֹם אֲשֶׁר`** (literal "on the day when" = "when"), because Hebrew has the same idiom in the same position (`בְּיוֹם עֲשׂוֹת`, Gen 2:4b) – a real structural parallel, tagged [equivalent]. Text 3 (1–2) rendered *ud … -a-ba* `בימים אשר` in parallel with its *ud re-a* `בימים ההם`; here there is no *ud re-a* in the line, so the singular is kept (open decision 1).
5. **27, 39 *mu-un-se₁₂-eš* = `שָׁכְנוּ?`** ("dwelt", plural), following **ETCSL's own lemma** (*til₃* "to live", plural stem *se₁₂*), against ETCSL's translation "they created Sheep and Grain" / "have been created". The sign SIG₇ is read *sig₇* "to be green" in 3 (`הוריק?`) and *se₁₂* in 27/39; "created" may reflect a lexical equation SIG₇ = Akk. *banû* (our conjecture). Consequence: **the prologue has no creation verb for Sheep and Grain** (open decision 2). `שָׁכְנוּ` is vocalized so that it is not read as the name `שַׁכַּן`.
6. **23 *ĝiš-gen₆-na su-bi* = `באיברים חשופים?`.** ETCSL lemmatizes *su-bi* as *sug₄* "to be empty, bare"; ORACC elsewhere has *su-bi* = *su* "flesh" + *-bi*. The word is not the Sumerian for "naked", so `עֵירֹם`/`עָרוּם` (reserved, renderings §3) are not used; the Gen 2:25 / 3:7 / 3:10–11 parallels are tagged [equivalent] in the apparatus. *kalam* (no locative) is the subject: `הארץ … הלכה` (as text 4's `ארצו את הארץ בוקעת`).
7. **25 *a mu₂-sar-ra-ka* = `ממי הערוגות?`** – *mu₂-sar* "garden (bed)" (ETCSL lemma, ePSD2 *musar*), against ETCSL's translation "ditches"; `עֲרוּגָה` as text 2's *sar*. Not `גַּן` (reserved for *kiri₆*).
8. **Names and personification:** *ezina₂* = `דָּגָן` and *u₈* = `כִּבְשָׂה` (renderings §2), as **personal names: no article, feminine verbs** (the text calls them sisters, Grain a *ki-sikil*); the animal *u₈* (6, 8, 132) has the article where Hebrew needs it. *{d}ezina₂-{d}ku₃-su₃* = `דָּגָן־כּוּסוּ` (10, 89). New names: נִיגִּירְסִי (*niĝir-si*, ePSD2 "(bridegroom's) friend"), אִישְׁכּוּר (Iškur = Adad `אֲדַד`), הַסַּג־אוּר־סַג (*saĝ-ur-saĝ*), הַכֹּהֵן־הַגּוּדוּג, הַכֹּהֵן־הַפָּשֵׁשׁ; נִין־כַּסִי, אוּטּוּ, שַׁכַּן, אַן, הָאֲנוּנָּה, אֶנְקִי, אֶנְלִיל, אִינַנָּה, אוּתוּ as before (Uttu and Utu always vocalized).
9. **Debate formulas, identical wherever the Sumerian is** (checked by script; 6 groups identical): *X Y-ra gu₃ mu-(un-)na-de₂-e* = `X אל Y פונה:` (37, 71, 180); *2-kam-ma-še₃ X Y-ra mu-na-ni-ib-gi₄-gi₄* = `שנית – X לY משיבה:` (92, 116, 143; 170 without *2-kam-ma* `X לY משיבה:`); *a-na-zu mu-da-ĝal₂ a-na ma-ab-be₂-(en-)na-bi gi₄-ma-ni-ib* = `מה יש לך? מה שתאמרי לי – השיבי לי!` (91, 115, 142, 168; the spelling without *-en* in 115/142 treated as orthographic, with ETCSL); the colophon *X Y-bi a-da-min₃ dug₄-ga* = `ויכוח שנאמר בין X ו־Y:` (192).
10. **Fixed words followed** (renderings §2 and texts 2–4): *ninda* `לחם`, *gu₇* `אכל`, *naĝ* `שתה`, *tug₂* `בגד`, *mu₄* `לבש`, *u₂* `עשב`, *zu* `ידע`, *mu* `שם`, *tud* `ילד`, *dim₂* `עיצב`, *mu₂* `צמח`/`הצמיח`, *e₃* `יצא`/`הוציא`, *ed₃* `ירד`/`עלה`/`הוריד`, *du₃* `בנה`, *nam tar* `גזר גורל`, *ĝar* `שָׂם` only (45, 157, 175, 189, 191), *ki*/*kalam* `הארץ` (never `אדמה`), *edin* `ערבה`, *kug* `טהור`, *sag₉* `נאה`, *dug₃* `טוב` / `מתוק` (taste) / verb `היטיב`, *ud re-a* `בימים ההם` (20), *ud-ba* `בעת ההיא` (26, 37, 169, 180), *nam-lu₂-ulu₃* `האנושות`, *ki-sikil* `עלמה`, *men* `כתר` (text 4), *si sa₂* `סידר` (text 4), *saĝ šum₂* `מיהר` (text 4), *ki-bal* `הארץ המורדת`, *mar-uru₅* `אשפה`, *sa₂-dug₄* `המנחות הקבועות`, *tukul* `נשק`, *ĝeštug₂* `בינה`, *banšur* `שולחן`, *ki-nu₂* `משכב`, *ĝišbun* `משתה`, *barag* `דוכן`, *za₃-mi₂* `תהילה`, *a-a* `אב` (vocative `האב`), *ḫal* `פיזר`, *te* `ניגש`, *dab₅* `תפס`, *gub* `עמד`/`העמיד`, *kur₉* `נכנס`. *numun₂* does not occur in this text (the text-3/text-4 split is not touched).
11. **New renderings (echo control decisive):** *sipad* `רועה`, *na-gada* `נוקד` [cognate, Akk. *nāqidu*], *engar* `איכר` [cognate, Akk. *ikkaru* < Sum.], *šu-rin-na* `תנור` [cognate, Akk. *tinūru*], *udun* `כבשן`; *amaš* `דיר`, *e₂-ubur* `דיר־חליבה`; *apin* `מחרשה`, *šudul* `עול`, *erin₂* `צמד`; *ki-du₆* `גורן`; *ban* `סאה`; *kadra* `תשורה` (**not `מִנְחָה`**, which would pre-empt Gen 4:3; `מנחות` only for *sa₂-dug₄*, as text 4); *u₂-šim* `מספוא` (not `יֶרֶק`/`דֶּשֶׁא`); *zi-šag₄(-ĝal₂)* `מחיה` (keeps *zi* = חיים; not `נשמה`/`נפש`); *si* "fill" `מלא`/`נמלאו` (31, 34, 63, 179; not `שבע`); *du₁₄* `ריב`; *a-da-min₃* `ויכוח`; *bar-rim₄* `צייה` (**not text 2's `היבשה`**, whose sense there is "dry land vs. boat"; `יַּבָּשָׁה` would pre-empt Gen 1:9); *kur* `הרים` (15, 146; *ḫur-saĝ* `הר`); *ki-ulutim₂* `מקום הולדתם` (ETCSL 1.4.4 "birthplace"; not `יצירה`/`בריאה`); *muš* `נחש` (128, tagged ↔ Gen 3:1); *zulumḫi* `אדרת?`; *siki* `צמר`; *buru₅* `ציפור` (not `דְּרוֹר`, = *andurāru*); *išin* `גבעול` (not `קנה`, = *gi*); *saĝ* "slave" (146) = `'ראש'` (text 4 *saĝ* = `ראש`; not `עבד`).
12. **Deviations by meaning** (Changelog in `renderings.md`): *sig₉* "drive in (a peg)" = `תקע` (5; elsewhere *sig₉* = `הֵטִיל`, 156, as text 3); *sig₇* = `הוריק?` (3, causative "make green") and `רעננה` (50, "fresh, beautiful"); *zig₃* as adjective = `זקוף` (51; text 3 `קם`/`הקים`); *lipiš* = `קרב` (78, figurative "core"; text 3 *lipiš* as a body part = `מעיים`); *du₈* by sense: `הפיץ` (48, charm), `פתח` (88, stocks), `פרש` (158, dough); *tag* "touch" = `נעץ` (131, the tally-stick "touched" into the ground; `נגע` avoided, cf. Gen 3:3; text 2 *tag* = `מישש`); *ĝeštin* = `יין` (65, 67, the drink; text 2 `ענבים`, the fruit).
13. **Letter-sequence traps avoided:** `בשדמות` (contains `דמות`) → `בשדמה ובשדמה` (69, also mirroring the reduplication *a-gar₃-a-gar₃*); `מקום מושבן` instead of `שבתן` (63); no `בראש`/`בראשונה` (72 `הראשונה`, *dub-saĝ*, text 4); `שמן בְּשָׂמִים` vocalized (106; unvocalized it reads "oil in heaven"); `שָׂם`/`שָׁם` vocalized everywhere; `חֲלָבוֹ` "its milk" vocalized (32; unvocalized also `חֶלְבּוֹ` "its fat", the Gen 4:4 word); `בְּדִירָהּ`, `שָׂדָהּ`, `בִּגְדִי` vocalized for the possessive. The `>` lines contain none of the reserved words of renderings §3 and no `טרם`, `ברא`, `שבת`, `עבד`, `אדמ`, `קרא`, `נפש`, `עדן`, `ערום`; `נחש` (128, *muš*) and `טוב` (105, *dug₃*) are their words' meanings, tagged. (Note: the name `דגן` contains the string `גן`; harmless as a word, recorded for completeness.)

### Open decisions for the user
1. **2** *ud … -a-ba*: `בַּיּוֹם אֲשֶׁר` (followed; literal, and it shows the Gen 2:4b idiom) or `כַּאֲשֶׁר` (neutral). Text 3 has `בימים אשר` for the same construction next to *ud re-a*; a corpus rule would help (singular `ביום אשר` alone, plural only with *ud re-a*?).
2. **27, 39** *se₁₂*: `שָׁכְנוּ?` ("dwelt", ETCSL's lemma; followed) or ETCSL's translation "created" (`נוצרו`/`עוצבו` would be needed – never `נבראו`). The choice decides whether the prologue is a *creation* of grain and sheep (Gen 1:11–12, 24–25) or their *descent* from the gods' dwelling (Gen 3:23 inverted). Alster & Vanstiphout 1987 would settle it.
3. **23** `באיברים חשופים?` (followed; *sug₄* "bare") or the plain `ערומים` (natural Hebrew for the concept, but it puts the reserved Gen 2:25 / 3:7 word into the line where the Sumerian does not have the word for "naked").
4. **25** `ממי הערוגות?` (followed; lemma *mu₂-sar* "garden") or ETCSL's "ditches" (`ממי התעלות`).
5. **3** *sig₇* `הוריק?` (followed) or ETCSL's "created"; the same SIG₇ question as 27/39.
6. **Personified names without the article** (`כבשה`, `דגן`, feminine verbs; followed) or with it (`הכבשה`, `הדגן`), which reads more naturally but blurs name and animal.
7. **36, 55** *zi-šag₄ ĝal₂* = `מחיה נתנו` (followed) or a literal `חיים ולב נתנו`, which would make the Gen 2:7 contrast (breath vs. food) visible in the line.
8. **Buy / borrow Alster & Vanstiphout 1987** (ASJ 9, the edition with the manuscripts) and Vanstiphout 1997 (COS 1.180): they would settle 3/27/39 (SIG₇), 23 (*su-bi*), 25 (*mu₂-sar*), 82, 95 (*-NE*), 100, 102, 109, 114, 122, 141, 152–155, 172–174, 183.
9. **Print stub and the combined `translation.rtl.md`** are the coordinator's (not created here).

### Progress log
- **Drafting** (2026-10-17): all 193 entries in order, from the ETCSL transliteration and ETCSL's TEI lemmas (scratch extraction), with ePSD2 lookups and a morph lookup of every verbal form in the ORACC literary corpus; ETCSL concordance searches for the formulas and rare words (above); the Hebrew of each debate formula fixed at its first occurrence (37, 71, 91, 92); ETCSL's translation compared after each line's parse; echo check per line, and the distribution of *zu*, *gu₇*, *naĝ*, *tug₂*/*mu₄*, *ninda*, *u₂*, *ĝar*, *ed₃*, *e₃*, *tud*, *si*, *dug₃* over the whole text. **The first 42 lines were drafted with full niqqud against the working rule and were rewritten by hand, unvocalized and plene** (names at first occurrence and ambiguous forms only); 43–193 were drafted unvocalized from the start.
- **Review (a) fidelity** (every `>` line against the transliteration and the parsing, word by word; brackets by root): 10 `(ו)כבשה` → `וכבשה` (*-bi-da* "and" is in the Sumerian); 45 `את שדה` → `את שָׂדָהּ` (*-ani* "her"); 48 `בדירים` → `בדיר` (*amaš-a* singular; ETCSL's "sheepfolds"); 73 `לנוגה` → `של נוגה` (genitive *-ka*); 95 `מן המקום` → `(מן) המקום` (no ablative in the Sumerian); 104 `והנעליים` → `הנעליים` (no conjunction); 164 `גם את` → `ואת` (*u₃*); 166 `מאחריה` → `מאחריו` (*eĝer-bi*, referring to *me*); 173 `הוכתה` → `[הוכתה]` (the root *ra* is restored); brackets of 101, 103 (`⟨שלי⟩`), 106, 144–145, 183–186, 191 re-checked; ETCSL imports recorded: "hill" (1), "created" (3, 27, 39), "naked" (23), "ditches" (25), "glistens" (109), "your" (113, 141), "how many" (132).
- **Review (b) consistency** (repeated lines compared as strings by script, niqqud ignored: 30=33, 91=168, 115=142 (= 91), 92=143, 134=137, 135=138, 37/180 – all identical; 12–14, 21–22, 31/34, 47/49, 54/60, 65/67, 66/68 differ only where the Sumerian differs). Hebrew of every recurring word checked against renderings §1–4 and the `>` lines of texts 2–4: *banšur* `שולחן`, *ki-nu₂* `משכב`, *ĝišbun* `משתה`, *siki* `צמר`, *barag* `דוכן`, *men* `כתר`, *si sa₂* `סידר`, *ki-sikil* `עלמה`, *ab-sin₂* `תלם`, *a-gar₃* `שדמה` confirmed; the vowel-pointing of the *ĝar* forms completed (175 `אָשִׂים`, 189 `יָשִׂימוּ`, 191 `[יָשִׂימוּ]`); 69 `בשדמות` → `בשדמה ובשדמה` (string `דמות`).
- **Review (c) Hebrew** (read aloud): 57, 62 `כל` → `כֹּל` (archaic "everything"); 178 `שלך־שלך` → `– שלך עצמך –`; 106 `בשמים` → `בְּשָׂמִים` (ambiguity "in heaven"); 32 `חֲלָבוֹ` vocalized; 43, 47 `בְּדִירָהּ`, 107 `בִּגְדִי` vocalized; the long boast lines (90, 100, 141) kept close to the Sumerian order; 82, 152–155 left fragmentary with `…`/`[…]` rather than smoothed.
- **Checks:** `python3 -I verify.py 5` → OK (193 entries = ETCSL's line list; translation = annotated `>` lines). `tools/check_bible_quotes.py` on the annotated file, the translation, this file and `renderings.md` → 0 problems (after fixing 4 quotations in the draft: Gen 33:17, Isa 4:2, Gen 8:21 `הַנִּיחֹחַ`, Ex 9:31). Grep of the `>` lines for the reserved words and letter sequences: Decision 13.

| 111 | *tug₂ siki* | צמר | `בִּגְדֵי פִשְׁתִּים יִלְבָּשׁוּ; וְלֹא יַעֲלֶה עֲלֵיהֶם צֶמֶר` (יחזקאל מד:יז) | [divergence] | **Wool is the Sumerian priests' garment of purity; wool is forbidden to the priests in Ezekiel's temple** (linen, Lev 16:4). |
| 21, 24, 42 | *ninda*; *u₂*; *ed₃* | לחם; עשב; מורידים | `מַצְמִיחַ חָצִיר לַבְּהֵמָה וְעֵשֶׂב לַעֲבֹדַת הָאָדָם; לְהוֹצִיא לֶחֶם מִן הָאָרֶץ` (תהלים קד:יד); `וְלֶחֶם לְבַב אֱנוֹשׁ יִסְעָד` (תהלים קד:טו) | [equivalent] | Grass for beasts, bread for man; in the psalm bread comes "out of the earth", here down from the gods. |
| 187–188 | *{d}utu e₃-a-ra {d}utu šu₂-uš-e mu {d}ezina₂-kam ḫe₂-em-pad₃-pad₃-de₃* | מצאת אותו עד בוא אותו – שם דגן הוא אשר יינקב | `מִמִּזְרַח שֶׁמֶשׁ עַד מְבוֹאוֹ מְהֻלָּל שֵׁם יְהוָה` (תהלים קיג:ג); `כִּי מִמִּזְרַח שֶׁמֶשׁ וְעַד מְבוֹאוֹ גָּדוֹל שְׁמִי בַּגּוֹיִם` (מלאכי א:יא) | [equivalent] | **The same formula: from sunrise to sunset + the name.** ETCSL's "praised" for *pad₃*; we `יינקב`. |
| 59 | *e₂ ukur₃-ra saḫar ki us₂-sa* | בבית הדל, הדבק לעפר הארץ | `מְקִימִי מֵעָפָר דָּל` (תהלים קיג:ז) | [equivalent] | The poor in the dust, lifted by the gift. |
| 181 | *nin₉ ḫe₂-me-eš* | אחיות תהיינה | `הִנֵּה מַה טּוֹב וּמַה נָּעִים שֶׁבֶת אַחִים גַּם יָחַד` (תהלים קלג:א) | [equivalent] | Siblings together. |
| 66 | *kaš … dug₃-dug₃* | שכר … מטיבות (בו את ליבן) | `וַיֹּאכַל בֹּעַז וַיֵּשְׁתְּ וַיִּיטַב לִבּוֹ` (רות ג:ז) | [equivalent] | Drink "makes good" the heart. |
| 31, 34 | *gu₇ … nu-mu-un-de₃-si-si-eš* | אוכלים – ולא נמלאו | `וְגַם הַנֶּפֶשׁ לֹא תִמָּלֵא` (קהלת ו:ז); `אָכוֹל וְאֵין לְשָׂבְעָה` (חגי א:ו); `וַאֲכַלְתֶּם וְלֹא תִשְׂבָּעוּ` (ויקרא כו:כו) | [equivalent] | Eating without being filled – here of the gods, which is why they give the food away. |
| 24 | *udu-gin₇ … u₂ … gu₇* | כצאן … עשב אוכלים | `וְעִשְׂבָּא כְתוֹרִין יֵאכֻל` (דניאל ד:ל) | [equivalent] | The man who eats grass like cattle is outside humanity. |

### Proposals for the shared renderings
1. **Personified goods:** *ezina₂* = `דָּגָן`, *u₈* = `כִּבְשָׂה` as names (no article, feminine), *ezina₂-kusu* = `דָּגָן־כּוּסוּ`; the animal *u₈* with the article where Hebrew needs it.
2. **Herding and farming (corpus §3, they touch Gen 4:2):** *sipad* = `רוֹעֶה`; *na-gada* = `נוֹקֵד` [cognate loan]; *engar* = `אִכָּר` [cognate loan]; *nam-sipad* = `רוֹעוּת`; *amaš* = `דִּיר`; *e₂-ubur* = `דִּיר־חֲלִיבָה`; *apin* = `מַחֲרֵשָׁה`; *šudul* = `עֹל`; *erin₂* = `צֶמֶד`; *ab-sin₂* = `תֶּלֶם`; *ki-du₆* = `גֹּרֶן`; *ban* = `סְאָה`; *šu-rin-na* = `תַּנּוּר` [cognate loan], *udun* = `כִּבְשָׁן`; *ĝiš-šudum* = `מַקֵּל־הַסְּפִירָה`. Never `עֹבֵד`/`עֲבוֹדָה` for any of them.
3. **Gift words kept apart from Gen 4:3–4:** *kadra* = `תְּשׁוּרָה`; *niĝ₂-ba* = `מַתָּנָה` (text 2); `מִנְחָה` only for *sa₂-dug₄* (texts 4–5) or a real offering term.
4. **Naming and "before":** *mu … zu* = `יָדַע שֵׁם` (kept apart from *mu sa₄* = `קָרָא שֵׁם`); plain *nu-* = `לֹא`, never `טֶרֶם` (now in texts 2 and 5); *ud … -a-ba* = `בַּיּוֹם אֲשֶׁר` / `כַּאֲשֶׁר` to be fixed corpus-wide (open decision 1).
5. **Nakedness and clothing:** *sug₄* (of limbs) = `חָשׂוּף`; `עֵירֹם`/`עָרוּם` stay reserved for a Sumerian/Akkadian word that means "naked" (Akk. *erû*/*ūru*, Sum. *bar-dul₅*? – to be checked when one occurs).
6. **Food words:** *zi-šag₄(-ĝal₂)* = `מִחְיָה`; *si* (be filled) = `נִמְלָא`, not `שָׂבַע`; *u₂-šim* = `מִסְפּוֹא`; *dug₃* (verb, of drink and friendship) = `הֵיטִיב (לֵב)`; *niĝ₂ dug₃* (of food/drink) = `הַדָּבָר הַמָּתוֹק`; *i₃* = `שֶׁמֶן` (note "fat" at Gen 4:4 rows).
7. **Places:** *ḫur-saĝ* = `הַר`; *du₆ (kug)* = `(הַ)תֵּל (הַטָּהוֹר)`; *kur* (highland) = `הָרִים` / (foreign land) `אֶרֶץ` (text 2); *bar-rim₄* (arid land) = `צִיָּה` vs. (dry land, opp. water) `יַבָּשָׁה` (text 2); *an-edin* = `הָעֲרָבָה הָעֶלְיוֹנָה`; *ki-ulutim₂* = `מְקוֹם הוּלֶּדֶת`.
8. **Names (§1):** נִיגִּירְסִי, אִישְׁכּוּר (= אֲדַד), הַסַּג־אוּר־סַג, הַכֹּהֵן־הַגּוּדוּג, הַכֹּהֵן־הַפָּשֵׁשׁ.
9. **Guidelines §6.7 (traps):** plural `שדמות` contains `דמות` (use `שְׁדֵמוֹת` only vocalized, or a construction); `בשמים` "spices" vs "in heaven" – always vocalize `בְּשָׂמִים`; `חלבו` "his milk" vs "his fat" (Gen 4:4!) – vocalize; `שכנו` "dwelt" vs the name `שַׁכַּן` – vocalize; `דגן` contains `גן` (harmless).

### Lessons for the guidelines
- **ETCSL's lemma can contradict ETCSL's translation in exactly the key line.** In 27/39 ETCSL lemmatizes *se₁₂* "to live (pl.)" but translates "created" – and the "creation of grain and sheep" in the secondary literature rests on that translation. In 25 the lemma is "garden", the translation "ditches"; in 1 the word is "mountain", the brief and the literature say "hill". Read the lemma line before the translation, and list such contradictions in the parallels index.
- **When ORACC has no text, build a form index of the whole ORACC corpus** (form → cf, gw, morph, text, line) and look up every verbal form of the ETCSL text: here 99 of 198 distinct verbal forms have a parsed instance elsewhere (the "like sheep they eat grass" line itself in How Grain came to Sumer). Mark the rest as "our analysis". The throw-away scripts (session scratchpad): `lem.py` (ETCSL TEI → per-line form=lemma[label]), `buildidx.py` + `q.py`/`morph.py` (ORACC form index and per-line morph lookup), `conc.py` (ETCSL bulk concordance with the translation paragraph), `gl.py` (ePSD2 glossary regex lookup) – again a case for `tools/sumerian_workbench.py`.
- **Search the formula across genres before drafting a "unique" line:** the primeval humans "eating grass with their mouths like sheep" (24) is the first line of How Grain came to Sumer (1.7.6); the debate colophon finds Dumuzid and Enkimdu (4.08.33), the shepherd–farmer dispute whose verdict (for the shepherd) is the Genesis one.
- **Loanwords are the Sumerian [cognate]s:** Sumerian has no Semitic roots, but words that travelled through Akkadian into Hebrew (*engar* → `אִכָּר`, *na-gada* = `נֹקֵד`, *šu-rin-na* ~ `תַּנּוּר`) are real lexical links and should be rendered by their Hebrew descendants and tagged.
- **Personified commodities need a grammatical decision before drafting** (gender, article): Hebrew `דָּגָן` is masculine, the Sumerian Grain is a sister and a maiden; decide it once (here: name, feminine, no article) and say so in both introductions.
- **Draft unvocalized from the first line** – this session repeated text 3's mistake for 42 lines (a fully vocalized draft) and had to rewrite them; the rule should be the first line of the translator's brief, not one item among many.

## Text 6: The Founding of Eridu (CT 13 35–38)

### Scope
- **Text:** the bilingual Sumerian–Akkadian incantation *Ē elli bīt ilī* / *e₂ ku₃-ga e₂ diĝir-e-ne* ("The pure house, the house of the gods"), known as "The Creation of the World by Marduk" or "The Founding of Eridu": a cosmogony (obverse) that serves as the mythical introduction to a ritual of purifying a temple – by the reverse, Ezida, Nabû's temple at Borsippa (eBL genre: Magic: Varia: Building Rituals). **One tablet:** BM 93014 + 82-3-23,101 (Late Babylonian; copy CT 13 35–38; the join by Borger 1988). A transliteration search of eBL (2026-10-16) found no duplicate.
- **Lines: all 67 entries** – obv. 1–40 (the cosmogony, all translated) and rev. 1′–27′ (fragmentary; every legible word translated; rev. 1′–2′ and the broken Sumerian words of 3′, 6′, 11′ given as `…`). Labels as eBL: `obv. 1` … `obv. 40` (= King's), `rev. 1'` … `rev. 27'` (King rev. n ≈ eBL (n+9)′; eBL rev. 1′–9′ come from the joined fragment and are not in King).
- **Files:** `annotated-6-Founding-of-Eridu.rtl.md` (Hebrew header incl. the bilingual convention and the `{שומרית}` tags; 8 sections; per line our Akkadian normalization ‖ eBL's transliteration, then the Sumerian halves; parsing of every Akkadian word and brief Sumerian parsing; King 1902; parallels; notes); `translation-6-Founding-of-Eridu.rtl.md` (introduction, the same sections, 13 footnotes), generated from the annotated file by a scratch builder (session scratchpad), so that the numbered lines equal the `>` lines; `python3 -I verify.py 6` → OK.
- **Content:** a chain of negations – no pure house, no reed, no tree, no brick or brick-mould, no house, no city, no *nammaššû*, no Nippur/Ekur, Uruk/Eanna, Apsû/Eridu (1–9); "all the lands were sea", the spring in the sea only a channel (10–11); then Eridu and Esagil, Babylon and Esagil, the Anunnakkū and the holy city (12–16); Marduk binds a reed raft on the face of the waters, "builds" dust and pours it beside the raft, to settle the gods (17–19); he builds mankind; Aruru builds the seed of mankind with him; the animals of Šakkan in the steppe; Tigris and Euphrates, set in place and named well; reeds, marsh plants, forest; the green of the steppe; "the lands were marsh and reed-bed"; cattle and sheep; gardens and forests; the he-goat and wild ram stand for him (20–30); Marduk fills a terrace at the sea's edge, [Ea?] sets reed-bed and **dry land**; something is "brought into being" (31–33); and the negations of 2–7 are reversed in the same order (34–40). Reverse: fragments of hymnic epithets (1′–12′), Papsukkal and Ningirima purify the addressee with censer, torch and Apsû water by Marduk's spell; abundance and rites for ever; Ezida, beloved of Anu and Ištar; "pure as heaven, clean as earth"; the evil tongue out; rubric; catch-line *Ereqqu* 2 (13′–27′).

### Sources consulted
- **Rank 1:** eBL Fragmentarium BM 93014 (`../../sources/eBL-fragmentarium/atf/BM.93014.atf`, raw `json/BM.93014.json`; not lemmatised, "not collated"). The Akkadian is normalized by us; every line gives our normalization and eBL's transliteration side by side.
- **Rank 2:** the eBL dictionary API (= CDA), per word: *amu* "raft, usu. of reed", *rāṭu* "water-channel, pipe", *īnu*, *uššu* (III "dead reed"), *appāru* "reed-bed, marsh", *apu* "reed-bed; reeds", *qīšu* "forest", *kirû*, *littu*, *būru* II "calf", *mīru* "breeding bull", *laḫru* "ewe", *puḫādu*, *immeru*, *supūru* "sheepfold", *atūdu* "wild sheep; ram", *sappāru* "wild ram", *tamlû* ("filling"), *pāṭu*, *nābalu* "dry land, mainland" [PA.RI.IM], *nammaštû* "moving things, animals" [Á.DAM; NÍG.ZI.GÁL], *šiknu*, *ramû*, *mitḫāriš*, *ṣīriš*, *ṭūbu* (*ṭ. libbi*), *agubbû*, *gizillû*, *ebbu*, *nuḫšu*, *ereqqu* (*e. šamê* "Ursa Minor"), *eršu*, *ušumgallu*, *kiššatu*. Not found in eBL: *dittu* in a plant sense (rendered `גֹּמֶא?`). Corpus renderings, Enūma eliš renderings (*rakāsu* `רָכַס`, *šapāku* `שָׁפַךְ`, *ramû* `הִתְיַשֵּׁב`, *mitḫāriš* `יַחְדָּיו`, *ṣīru* `נַעֲלֶה`, *eršu* `פִּקֵּחַ`, *māliku* `מְיַעֵץ`, *nuḫšu* `עֲתֶרֶת`, *ana ṣâti* `לָעַד`, *šiptu* `קֶסֶם`, *tû* `לַחַשׁ`, *Gilima* `גִּילִימָה`, *Lugaldukuga*), texts 1–5 (Adapa *šuklulu* `שִׁכְלֵל`, *zēr amīlūti* `זֶרַע הָאֱנוֹשׁוּת`, *sukkallu* `וְזִיר`; Sumerian *dim₂* `עִצֵּב`, *du₃* `בָּנָה`, *ĝar* `שָׂם`, *kug*/*sikil* `טָהוֹר`/`זַךְ`, *u₃-šub* `מַלְבֵּן`, *a-gar₃*, *bar-rim₄*).
- **No rank 3 is available:** Lambert 2013 (*Babylonian Creation Myths*, 361–365), Ambos 2004 (*Mesopotamische Baurituale*, 199–212) and Heidel 1951 (*The Babylonian Genesis*, 61–63) are not owned; no pirate copies were searched for. **The Hebrew rests on our own reading of eBL's transliteration.**
- **Rank 5:** King 1902, vol. I 130–139 (`../../sources/King-1902--Seven-Tablets-of-Creation/vol-1_djvu.txt`, from l. 14600), read after each line; quoted in every entry. King's main outdated readings: 1 "in the holy place" (eBL has no *elli* in the Akkadian), 11 "a movement in the sea" (*rāṭu* "channel"), 17 "a reed" (*amu* "raft"), 19 *šubat* (not in eBL's Akkadian), 32 *na-ma-la* "marsh" (eBL *na-ba-la* = *nābalu* "dry land"), rev. 14′ "Nin-aha-kudu" (eBL Ningirim).
- **Comparison:** the commentary on Gen 1:2, 1:9–10 (`1010-…`: `וְתֵרָאֶה` as a "medial" Nifal, "a development that happened by itself"), 1:26–28, 2:4–17 (`1020-…`), 2:18–3:24, 4:1–17 (skimmed); Enūma eliš VI 1–66 (`../1-אנומה-אליש/translation-VI.rtl.md`); text 5 (the "not" chain, `שָׁכְנוּ?` / SIG₇).

### Decisions
1. **Bilingual convention** (project Decision 5, as specified by the coordinator's brief): the Akkadian is translated wherever it survives; the Sumerian is parsed in the apparatus and its differences noted there. A line whose Hebrew comes from the Sumerian begins with **`{שומרית}`** (a recension-style tag, guidelines §6.8). **Extension:** where only part of a line survives in Sumerian (obv. 37, 39, 40: the Akkadian half is restored or lost), the tag encloses only the words translated from the Sumerian, **`{שומרית: …}`**; also for the rubric *en₂* "incantation" at obv. 1, which is Sumerian only. Explained in both headers. (Decision 5 had excluded `{שומרית: …}` for *differences*; here it marks *provenance*, so the two uses do not collide.)
2. **Reverse with separate-line pairs** (12′/13′, 17′/18′, 22′/23′): each tablet line is one entry translated from its own language, so the Sumerian line gets `{שומרית}` and the next line the Akkadian; the content is therefore given twice, as on the tablet.
3. **No `טֶרֶם`.** Obv. 1–9 have *ul* + **stative** (*ul epuš*, *ul aṣi*, *ul bani*, *ul nadât*, *ul banât*, *ul šakin*, *ul epšet*): "was not made/built", not "not yet" (Akkadian would use *adīni*); King's "had not yet" is interpretive. Rendered with the Hebrew Nifal/Hophal and **vocalized** (`נַעֲשָׂה`, `נִבְנָה`, `הוּטְלָה`, `הוּשַׂם`) – unvocalized `נעשה` also reads `נַעֲשֶׂה` "let us make" (Gen 1:26). The Gen 2:5 row is [divergence]; Prov 8:26 `עַד-לֹא עָשָׂה` is the closer [equivalent]. Same policy as texts 2 and 5.
4. **Creation verbs:** *banû* = `בָּנָה` everywhere (17 preserved occurrences + 3 restored – reed, tree, brick-mould, city, temples, dust, mankind, the seed of mankind, animals, rivers, plants); *ibtani* parsed as G perfect (formally also Gt preterite; no reciprocal sense; LB narrative perfect); *epēšu* = `עָשָׂה` (10 + 3: house, city, cities, Apsû, gods); *šubšû* = `הֵבִיא לִכְלַל הֲוָיָה` once (33). **The bilingual maps the Sumerian verbs systematically:** Sum. *dim₂* → Akk. *banû*, Sum. *du₃* → Akk. *epēšu*, and twice Sum. *mu₂* "grow" → *banû* (18, 21); in the `{שומרית}` segments the Sumerian verbs keep their project renderings (*dim₂* `עִצֵּב`, *du₃* `בָּנָה`).
5. **Echo decisions:** *ina pān mê* = `עַל פְּנֵי הַמַּיִם` [cognate] (the preposition by meaning: on the surface); *nābalu* (Sum. *pa-rim₄*) = **`יַבָּשָׁה`** [equivalent], because the word means "dry land" as opposed to the sea (eBL; the Sumerian agrees), unlike text 5's *bar-rim₄* "arid land" (`צִיָּה`); *šikin napišti* (Sum. *niĝ₂-zi-ĝal₂* "that which has life") = `בַּעֲלֵי נֶפֶשׁ` (`נֶפֶשׁ חַיָּה` reserved; `נֶפֶשׁ` kept as the cognate of *napištu*); *eperu* = `עָפָר`; *urqītu* = `יֶרֶק`; *kirû* = `גַּן` (tagged); *ṭūb libbi* = `טוּב לֵב` [cognate]; *ṭābiš* = `הֵיטֵב` (root of `טוֹב`).
6. **New cognate renderings:** *rāṭu* = `רַהַט` (Gen 30:38; vocalized: modern "furniture"); *īnu* = `עַיִן` (eye/spring); *tamlû* + *malû* D = `מִלּוֹא מִלֵּא` (the Millo); *laḫru* = `רָחֵל`; *atūdu* = `עַתּוּד`; *rakāsu* = `רָכַס` (EE); *šapāku* = `שָׁפַךְ` (EE). Others: *amu* = `רַפְסוֹדָה` (2 Chr 2:15); *appāru* = `אֲגַם`; *apu* = `סוּף` (so that `קָנֶה` stays for *qanû*); *dittu* = `גֹּמֶא?`; *uššu* = `קָנֶה־יָבֵשׁ?`; *qīšu*/*qīštu* = `יַעַר`; *littu* `פָּרָה`, *būru* `עֵגֶל`, *mīru* `פַּר`, *puḫādu* `טָלֶה`, *immeru* `כֶּבֶשׂ`, *supūru* `דִּיר`, *sappāru* `אֵיל־הַבָּר`; *pāṭ tâmti* = `שְׂפַת הַיָּם`; *ṣīriš* = `בְּרוֹמְמוּת`; *mitḫāriš* = `יַחְדָּיו`; *ramû* = `הִתְיַשֵּׁב`.
7. **Ritual vocabulary (reverse):** *ellu* = `טָהוֹר`, *ebbu* = `זַךְ` (as Sum. *kug*/*sikil*, text 2); *elēlu* D = `טִהֵר`; *ubbubu* = `זִכֵּךְ`; *niqnakku* = `מִקְטֶרֶת`; *gizillû* = `לַפִּיד`; *agubbû* = `אַגַּן־הַמַּיִם`; *tû* = `לַחַשׁ`, *šiptu*/*en₂* = `קֶסֶם` (EE); *ka-inim-ma* = `נֻסַּח־הַקֶּסֶם`; *nuḫšu* = `עֲתֶרֶת` (EE; `שֶׁפַע` = *ḫegallu*); *ana ūmī ṣâti* = `לִימֵי עַד` (EE *ana ṣâti* `לָעַד`; never `לְעוֹלָם`); *kalālu* Št = `יִשְׁתַּכְלְלוּ`; *kiššatu* = `כָּל`; *sukkallu* = `וְזִיר`; *eršu* (Sum. *galanzu*) = `פִּקֵּחַ`; *māliku* = `מְיַעֵץ`; *ereqqi šamê* = `עֲגָלַת הַשָּׁמַיִם` (Ursa Minor).
8. **Cruxes kept visible:** obv. 15 (singular *īpuš* "he made the gods" vs. the Sumerian ergative plural "the gods built"): Hebrew keeps the Akkadian singular and the ambiguity, with `?`; obv. 32 eBL's restoration `[{d}IDIM tâm]ta` shown as `[אֵאָה? את הים?]`; obv. 18′ *ullu* = `טִהֲרָה?`; obv. 25 plant names with `?`.
9. **Names:** מַרְדּוּךְ (Sum. Gilima `גִּילִימָה` at 17, Asalluḫi at 31, 19′ – in the apparatus); אֲרוּרוּ (note: not `אָרוּר`); שַׁכַּן; הָאֲנוּנָּכִּי; לוּגַל־דּוּכּוּגָּה (the full Sumerian form; EE VII has `לוּגַל־דּוּכּוּ`); אֵסַגִּיל, אֵכּוּר, אֵאַנָּה, נִיפּוּר, אוּרוּךְ (= `אֶרֶךְ`), אֶרִידוּ, הָאַפְּסוּ, בָּבֶל; פַּפְּסוּכַּל; נִינְגִּירִימָה; אֵזִידָה; אָנוּ, אִשְׁתַּר (Sum. אַן, אִינַנָּה).
10. **Letter-sequence check** of the `>` lines: no `ברא`, `בראש`, `שבת`, `דמות`, `נשמ`, `עבד`, `אדמ`, `טרם`, `עדן`, `תהום`, `לעולם`; `נעשה` occurs only as vocalized `נַעֲשָׂה` (Decision 3); `קרא` only for *nabû* (16, 24). `שָׂם` (*šakānu*, *ĝar*) always vocalized; `בַּמָּקוֹם` vocalized (unvocalized also "instead of"); `שְׁמָן` vocalized ("oil").

### Open decisions for the user
1. **Buy or borrow Lambert 2013 (361–365) and Ambos 2004 (199–212)**: the only modern editions; they would settle obv. 11 (*rāṭu*), 15, 25 (*uššu*, *dittu*), 30 (*izzazzūšu*), 32 (the restoration and *nābalu*), 33, and the reverse 1′–12′.
2. **obv. 32 *nābalu* = `יַבָּשָׁה`** (followed: the meaning, and the Sumerian *pa-rim₄* agrees) or a word that does not pre-empt Gen 1:9–10 (`חָרָבָה`, `אֲדָמָה יְבֵשָׁה`). A corpus rule for "dry land" is needed (text 2 `הַיַּבָּשָׁה`, text 5 `צִיָּה`).
3. **obv. 15**: "he (Marduk) made the gods together" (Akkadian singular, King) or "the gods built (it) together" (Sumerian ergative; cf. Enūma eliš VI 57–62).
4. **obv. 22 `בַּעֲלֵי נֶפֶשׁ`** (followed) or `יְצוּרֵי חַיִּים` / `חַיִּים` – the latter avoid the Gen 1:24/1:30 `נֶפֶשׁ` but lose the cognate.
5. **obv. 11 `רַהַט`** (cognate, Gen 30:38; followed) or the neutral `צִנּוֹר`.
6. **The `{שומרית: …}` segment tag** (Decision 1) – confirm, or prefer a footnote for partly Sumerian lines.
7. **`קֶסֶם` for *en₂*/*šiptu*** follows project 1's open decision 4 (alternative `לַחַשׁ` for both, or `חֶבֶר`). **Settled 2026-10-21** (cross-corpus harmonization): *šiptu* = `קֶסֶם`, Akk. *tû* = `לַחַשׁ` (rev. 19′ keeps `בלחש`: its Akkadian is *tû*).
8. Print stub and the combined `translation.rtl.md` are the coordinator's (not created here).

### Progress log
- **Drafting** (2026-10-17): all 67 entries in order, from eBL's ATF; Akkadian normalized by us and parsed word by word with the eBL dictionary API (scratch script `q.py`/`q2.py`, session scratchpad); the Sumerian parsed briefly; King 1902 read after each line; the distribution of *banû* / *epēšu* / *šubšû* / *šakānu* / *nabû* counted over the whole obverse; the mirror structure 2–7 ↔ 34–40 checked line by line; echo check per line with the commentary on Gen 1:2, 1:9–10 and 2:4–7 open. Drafted unvocalized and plene, vocalizing names at first occurrence and the ambiguous statives.
- **Review (a) fidelity** (every `>` line against the normalization, the ATF and the Sumerian; brackets by root): Sumerian lines of 32, 34, 36–39 given with the ATF's brackets closed correctly; rev. 10′ `כל?` → `[כל?]` (root of *kiššatu* broken); obv. 39–40 checked that only the Sumerian half survives (tag segments); obv. 33 note corrected (the counts of *banû* and *epēšu*: 17 + 3 and 10 + 3, not "22"); King's outdated readings listed under each line (1, 11, 17, 19, 32, rev. 14′).
- **Review (b) consistency** (renderings §1–4, texts 1–5, Enūma eliš): *banû*/*epēšu*/*šakānu*/*nabû*/*ellu*/*ebbu*/*ina ašri*/`-ma` predicate `(היו)` checked as strings; names vocalized only at first occurrence; *zēr amēlūti* = text 1; *šuklulu* = text 1; *u₃-šub*, *a-gar₃* not needed; four Bible quotations fixed by `check_bible_quotes.py` (1 Kgs 9:15 shortened, Ps 23:6 `וְשַׁבְתִּי בְּבֵית-יְהוָה`, Jer 12:7 `יְדִדוּת`, a bare `לְעוֹלָם` before a reference rephrased).
- **Review (c) Hebrew** (read aloud): rev. 24′ `כשמים – יִטְהַר!, כארץ – יִזַּךְ!, …` → `כשמים יִטְהַר, כארץ יִזַּךְ, כלב השמים ⟨…⟩!`; obv. 19 kept in the Akkadian order (`את האלים – בטוּב לב – להושיב`); obv. 13, 16 `(בו)`, `(לה)` added for Hebrew syntax; `קנה־יבש?` hyphenated so that `קנה` alone stays *qanû*.
- **Checks:** `python3 -I verify.py 6` → OK (67 entries; translation = annotated `>` lines). `tools/check_bible_quotes.py` on both files → 0 problems. Reserved-word grep: Decision 10.
