### Proposals for the shared renderings
1. **Stative negation:** *ul* + stative = `לֹא` + Nifal/Hophal, vocalized (`לֹא נַעֲשָׂה`), never `טֶרֶם` (now texts 2, 5, 6); add to corpus §3 the trap `נעשה` = `נַעֲשָׂה` / `נַעֲשֶׂה`.
2. **"Dry land":** a corpus rule – *nābalu* / Sum. *pa-rim₄*, *bar-rim₄* "dry land as against water" = `יַבָּשָׁה` [equivalent, tagged]; *bar-rim₄* "arid land" = `צִיָּה` (text 5).
3. **Cognates for guidelines §6.7:** *rāṭu* = `רַהַט`; *tamlû* = `מִלּוֹא`; *laḫru* = `רָחֵל`; *atūdu* = `עַתּוּד`; *ṭūb libbi* = `טוּב לֵב`; *īnu* = `עַיִן` (spring); *karû* = `כְּרִי` (text 7).
4. ***šikin napišti*** / Sum. *niĝ₂-zi-ĝal₂* = `בַּעֲלֵי נֶפֶשׁ` (keeps `נֶפֶשׁ חַיָּה` reserved).
5. **Ritual pair** *ellu* / *ebbu* = `טָהוֹר` / `זַךְ` (parallel to Sum. *kug* / *sikil*); *elēlu* D = `טִהֵר`, *ubbubu* = `זִכֵּךְ`; *agubbû* = `אַגַּן־הַמַּיִם`; *gizillû* = `לַפִּיד`; *niqnakku* = `מִקְטֶרֶת`.
6. **Time:** *ana ūmī ṣâti* = `לִימֵי עַד`; *ana ūmī dārûti* = `לִימֵי נֶצַח` (text 7) – both never `לְעוֹלָם`.
7. **Names (§1):** לוּגַל־דּוּכּוּגָּה (full form; EE `לוּגַל־דּוּכּוּ`), גִּילִימָה, פַּפְּסוּכַּל, נִינְגִּירִימָה, אֵזִידָה.
8. **The `{שומרית}` / `{שומרית: …}` tags** for bilinguals → guidelines §6.8.

### Lessons for the guidelines
- **A bilingual shows the ancient translators' own verb equations:** Sum. *dim₂* → Akk. *banû*, *du₃* → *epēšu*, *mu₂* "grow" → *banû*, *tuku* "acquire" → *šubšû*. In a bilingual, count the creation verbs per language before building a parallel on one of them.
- **An unlemmatised eBL ATF can be a base text:** normalize it ourselves and print our normalization beside eBL's transliteration (`normalization ‖ ATF`), so that the reader can check every choice; parse every word with the eBL API; and say plainly in both headers that no rank-3 translation was available.
- **Statives in creation texts must be vocalized:** `נעשה`, `נבנה` read both as "was made/built" and "let us make/build" – exactly the Gen 1:26 contrast.
- **Old editions misread exactly the key words** (King: *rāṭu* "movement", *amu* "reed", *nābalu* "marsh"); list them line by line, since the comparative literature (Heidel, Gunkel) argued from them.
- **`check_bible_quotes.py`:** a backticked non-biblical word followed by a reference in parentheses is checked as a quotation (and fails); keep our renderings and references apart.

## Text 7: KAR 4

### Scope
- **Text:** KAR 4, the bilingual Sumerian–Akkadian account of the creation of man ("When heaven had been separated from earth"; older title "the creation of man from the blood of the Lamga gods"). Three manuscripts (SEAL): **A** VAT 9307 (Assur, Middle Assyrian; scribe Kidin-Sîn, apprentice, son of the king's scribe Sutê), the only complete one; **B** K 4175 + Sm 57 + 80-7-19,184 (+) 82-3-23,146 (Nineveh, Ashurbanipal's library); **C** A 17643 (Chicago; eBL writes A 17634), a few lines (25–29). In A and B each line is preceded by a column of the Syllable Alphabet (a school exercise; not translated); B's colophon calls the tablet "tablet 2 of *ME.ME PAP.PAP*", i.e. part of a scholastic compilation headed by the syllabary.
- **Lines: all 84 entries** – obv. 1–9, 9a (B only), 10–41; rev. 1–30; colophon A rev. 31–32; colophon B 1–10. Labels: SEAL's numbers with `obv.`/`rev.` prefixes (SEAL restarts at rev. 1); colophon B numbered by us in SEAL's order (eBL: K 4175 col. 4, 5–10). One entry per composite line; manuscript differences in the apparatus.
- **Files:** `annotated-7-KAR-4.rtl.md` (header; 9 sections; per line SEAL's Akkadian transliteration by manuscript, then the Sumerian, eBL's readings where they differ; parsing with our normalization; SEAL's translation; parallels; notes); `translation-7-KAR-4.rtl.md` (introduction, 19 footnotes), generated from the annotated file; `python3 -I verify.py 7` → OK.
- **Content:** after heaven was separated from earth, the gods and goddesses "dwelt" (?), the earth was "built", the designs of heaven and earth made firm, dykes and canals ordered, the banks of Tigris and Euphrates set (1–6); An, Enlil, Utu (Šamaš), Enki (Ea), the Anunna(kkū) sit on the lofty dais and deliberate (7–11); "the designs of heaven and earth … dyke and canal … Tigris and Euphrates – they are established: **what shall we do? what shall we build?**" (12–20); the gods answer Enlil: **"In Uzumua, the bond of heaven and earth, let us slaughter the Illa-Alla gods; from their blood let us build mankind; the quota of the gods shall be their quota"** – to establish the boundary for ever, to put hoe and basket in their hands, to mark out meadow by meadow, keep the dyke straight, make plants flourish, rain (21–41); to establish the boundary, heap grain, [three lines "broken" in the scribe's original], make the field of the Anunnakkū prosper, multiply abundance, perfect the gods' festivals, pour cold water (rev. 1–10); **Ullegarra and Annegarra – you shall name their names**; ox, sheep, animals, fish and birds, abundance to sprout; En-ul and Nin-ul honoured with pure mouth (11–16); Bēlet-ilī (Sum. Aruru), worthy of lordship, and the gods design great designs: craftsman and fool, sprouting from the earth like barley by themselves – unchangeable as the eternal stars; festivals day and night (17–25); An, Enlil, Ea and Ninmaḫ, the great gods; where mankind was built, Nisaba is established (26–30); colophons.

### Sources consulted
- **Rank 1:** SEAL 1600 (`../../sources/SEAL/KAR-4/node-1600--KAR-4.md`), checked line by line against eBL's transliterations of A (`../../sources/eBL-fragmentarium/atf/VAT.9307.atf`) and B (`K.4175.atf`).
- **Rank 2:** eBL dictionary API (*miṣru*, *īku*, *palgu*, *ugāru*, *karû*, *qarānu*, *naqû*, *kaṣû*, *isinnu*, *dešû*, *ummiānu*, *banû* II and IV, *nakāru*, *urru*, *šubtu*, *rabbû*, *ramānu*, *kilallān*, *izuzzu*, *ṭabāḫu*, *zakāru*, *uṣurtu*, *eṣēru*, *ešēru*, *rabû* II, *šuklulu*, *mūdû*, *pirištu*, *šiāmu*, *kânu*/*kunnu*, *bēlūtu*); corpus, Enūma eliš and flood renderings (*riksu* `מוֹסֵרָה`, *uṣurtu* `צוּרָה`, *eṣēru* `צָר`, *ṭabāḫu* `טָבַח`, *dešû* D `הִדְשִׁיא`, *nakāru* Dt `הוּמַר`, *isinnu* `חַג`, *ḫegallu* `שֶׁפַע`, *ummânu* `אֻמָּן`, *zunnu* `גֶּשֶׁם`, *apālu* `עָנָה`); texts 1–5 (*eš₂-gar₃* `מִכְסָה`, *al* `מַעְדֵּר`, *dusu* `סַל`, *e*/*pa₅* `סוֹלְלָה`/`תְּעָלָה`, *a-gar₃* `שְׁדֵמָה`, Uzumua, Duranki, Nisaba, Ninmaḫ, SIG₇ in text 5); Enūma eliš VI 1–38 and Atra-ḫasīs I (the slain god's blood).
- **Rank 3, after each line's own reading:** SEAL's English translation; every departure noted under `תרגומים` (e.g. 1 "already" not in the Sumerian; 26 "(C the first one)" for *saĝ* "head(s) = people"; 30 "cherished" for *kunnat*).
- **Not available:** Lambert 2013 (350–360, pl. 65–66), Pettinato 1971 (*Das altorientalische Menschenbild*, 74ff.), Heidel 1951, Landsberger 1933; no pirate copies were searched for.

### Decisions
1. **Bilingual convention** as text 6 (Decision 1): the Akkadian line is translated; `{שומרית}` for the Sumerian-only lines (obv. 1–6, 9a, 39–41; colophon B 1–2); restorations from the parallel half or the other manuscript are named in the apparatus (obv. 7–9: Akkadian names restored from the Sumerian; obv. 9 "great" preserved in B).
2. **Labels** `obv. N` / `rev. N` (SEAL restarts the numbering on the reverse), `obv. 9a` (B only), colophon A = SEAL `rev. 31–32`, colophon B = `colophon B 1–10` (our numbering, SEAL's order).
3. **obv. 16–17 (and 19–20): SEAL and B followed against eBL's transliteration of A.** SEAL reads A 16 *nītepuš*, 17 *nibni*; eBL reads A 16 *nīpuš*, 17 *nītepuš*; B has *nīpuš* / *nibni*. The Hebrew of 16 is the same either way (`מה נַעֲשֶׂה?`); 17 = `מה נִבְנֶה?` with SEAL and B (and with the Sumerian *dim₂*, which the bilingual tradition renders by *banû*). B's variants (19 *nušbalkit* "overturn", 20 *nibbi* "name") are in the apparatus only.
4. ***mīnâ i nīpuš* = `מה נַעֲשֶׂה?`** – *epēšu* = `עָשָׂה` (corpus) + the Akkadian cohortative: the meaning, not an allusion; tagged [equivalent] ↔ Gen 1:26 (no cognacy). Vocalized in 16 and 19 (identical Akkadian, identical Hebrew).
5. ***iškāru* (Sum. *eš₂-gar₃*) = `מִכְסָה`** ("work quota", text 4) – not `עָמָל`: the brief expected *dullu* = `עָמָל`, but *dullu* does not occur in KAR 4. Never the root עבד.
6. **The slain gods:** {d}NAGAR-{d}NAGAR = `אִילָּה־אַלָּה` (eBL's reading *Illa Alla*, hyphenated as one pair); SEAL *E₁₀?-Illa?*, Lambert "Alla", older "Lamga" in the apparatus (open decision 2).
7. ***kânu* D = `כּוֹנֵן`** (15, 29, 36, 38, rev. 1, 30 `מְכוֹנֶנֶת`); **one deviation by meaning:** rev. 16 *uktannū* with gods as object and "with pure mouth" = "honoured" → `הוֹקִירוּ?` (CAD *kunnu* "to treat with honour").
8. ***banû* II** (D *bunnû* "make beautiful", rev. 20) = `לְיַפּוֹת?` (as Adapa's *banû* "beautiful" = `יָפֶה`), kept apart from *banû* IV = `בָּנָה`; the note explains that the Akkadian renders Sum. SIG₇ "sprout" by *banû* – the equation that bears on text 5 (open decision 2 there) and on obv. 2 here.
9. **SIG₇ in obv. 2** = *se₁₂* "to dwell (pl.)", `שׁוֹכְנִים?` (marû → participle), with SEAL ("lived") and text 5's `שָׁכְנוּ?`.
10. **Names of the first humans:** `אוּלֶּגַרָּה` and `אַנֶּגַרָּה`, transliterated; the meanings ("placed for distant time", "whom An placed", both with *ĝar* "place") in the note. ***šuma zakāru*** Gtn = `תַּזְכִּיר ותזכיר` (corpus *šuma zakāru* = `הִזְכִּיר שֵׁם`; not `קָרָא`, which stays for *nabû*).
11. **Time:** *ana ūmī dārûti* = `לִימֵי נֶצַח` (*dārû* = `נֶצַח`, text 1), never `לְעוֹלָם` (reserved for the Gen 3:22 comparison); *urra u mūša* = `יוֹמָם וָלַיְלָה`.
12. **Scribal note *ḫepi*** (rev. 3–5) = `(הסופר כתב:) "שָׁבוּר"` – the parentheses mark the added words, the quotation marks the scribe's own word.
13. **Fixed words:** *uṣurtu* = `צוּרָה` (pl. `צוּרוֹת`), *eṣēru* D = `צָר` (`צָרוּ`; `לָצוּר (צורתן)` in 34, so that it is not read "besiege"); *riksu* = `מוֹסֵרָה` (EE; `קשר` = *kaṣāru*); *miṣru* = `גְּבוּל`; *īku* / *palgu* = `סוֹלְלָה` / `תְּעָלָה`; *ugāru* = `שְׁדֵמָה`; *eqlu* = `שָׂדֶה`; *allu* = `מַעְדֵּר`; *tupšikku* = `סַל`; *šakānu* / *ĝar* = `שָׂם` (vocalized); *ešēru* Št = `יִשֵּׁר`; *karû* = `כְּרִי` [cognate]; *qarānu* = `עָרַם` (restored); *rabû* D = `הִרְבָּה`; *dešû* D = `הִדְשִׁיא` [cognate]; *isinnu* = `חַג`; *šuklulu* = `שִׁכְלֵל`; *naqû* = `נִסֵּךְ`; *parakku* = `דּוּכָן`; *ṣīru* = `נַעֲלֶה`; *šulukat* = `רָאוּי`; *šubtu* = `מוֹשָׁב`; *būlu* = `חַיָּה`; *ummânu* = `אֻמָּן`; *nuʾʾû* = `בּוּר` (the Mishnaic word, Avot 2:5; Ps 92:7 has the near-synonym `בַּעַר` from another root); *bēlūtu* = `אֲדָנוּת`; *pirištu* = `סוֹד`; *niṣirtu* = `נְצוּרָה` [cognate]; *mūdû* = `הַיּוֹדֵעַ`; *kullumu* = `הֶרְאָה`; *apālu* = `עָנָה`; *izuzzu* = `עָמַד`; *wašābu* = `יָשַׁב`; *ina ramāni-* = `בְּעַצְמָם` / `מֵעַצְמוֹ`.
14. **Names:** in a bilingual line the Akkadian name stands in the Hebrew (renderings §1): אָנוּ, אֶנְלִיל, שַׁמַשׁ, אֵאָה, הָאֲנוּנָּכִּי, בֵּלֶת־אִילִי (Sum. אֲרוּרוּ), נִינְמַח, נִיסַבָּה; Sum.-only lines keep the Sumerian forms. אוּזוּמוּאָה (text 4's variant form), אֵן־אוּל, נִין־אוּל, קִידִין־סִין, סוּטֵי, אַשּׁוּר־בָּנִי־אַפְלִי.
15. **Colophon B 7** *enūma ilu a[wīlu]* rendered as Atra-ḫasīs I 1 (`כשהאלים (היו) [אדם]`), with `?` for the singular spelling; interpreted as the catch-line to the next tablet (our reading of the colophon's structure).
16. **Letter-sequence check** of the `>` lines: no `ברא`, `בראש`, `שבת`, `דמות`, `נשמ`, `עבד`, `אדמ`, `טרם`, `עדן`, `לעולם`, `קרא`; `נעשה` only as the vocalized cohortative `נַעֲשֶׂה` (16, 19, Decision 4); `נִבְנֶה`/`נִבְנְתָה`, `צָרוּ`, `שָׁם` ("there", 21) vocalized.

### Open decisions for the user
1. **Collate A obv. 16–17** (SEAL *nītepuš* / *nibni* vs eBL *nīpuš* / *nītepuš*; CDLI photo P282595). The Hebrew of 17 depends on it (`מה נִבְנֶה?` followed).
2. **The name of the slain gods:** `אִילָּה־אַלָּה` (eBL, followed), `הָאַלָּה` ("the Alla gods", Lambert; the brief's name) or the older `לַמְגָּה`.
3. **obv. 11 Sumerian** *šu gi₄-gi₄* "they repeated" (SEAL) vs eBL's emendation *ad!(ŠU) gi₄-gi₄* "they took counsel" – decides whether the text itself has a divine council's "deliberation" (Gen 1:26). The Hebrew follows the Akkadian (`ישבו, ובעצמם […]`) and is unaffected.
4. **rev. 16** `הוֹקִירוּ?` (followed, "honoured") or the fixed `כּוֹנְנוּ`.
5. **rev. 20** `לְיַפּוֹת?` (SEAL's *banû* II) or a rendering that shows the translators' equation SIG₇ = *banû* ("to be built/created"); to be decided together with text 5's open decision 2 and obv. 2 here (`שׁוֹכְנִים?` vs "sprouting").
6. **obv. 27 `מִכְסָה`** (followed) or `עָמָל` (the corpus word for the gods' toil, *dullu*), which would align KAR 4 with Atra-ḫasīs and Enūma eliš but hide the difference of words.
7. **Buy or borrow Lambert 2013 (350–360) and Pettinato 1971**: for 1–6, 9a, 10–11, 16–17, 25, 39–41, rev. 2, 11 (the names), 16, 19–21 and the colophon B catch-line.
8. Print stub and the combined `translation.rtl.md` are the coordinator's.

### Progress log
- **Drafting** (2026-10-17): all 84 entries, from SEAL's lines (A, then B, C), each checked against eBL's ATF of A and B; Akkadian normalized and parsed word by word (eBL API); the Sumerian parsed briefly; SEAL's translation read after each line; the distribution of *banû* (17, 20, 26, rev. 29), *epēšu* (16, 19), *kânu* (15, 29, 36, 38, rev. 1, 16, 30), *eṣēru*/*uṣurtu* (4, 12, 34, rev. 18, 24–25), *šakānu* (31, colophon B 6) and *ṭabāḫu* (25) counted; Enūma eliš VI 33 and Atra-ḫasīs I 204 compared for the blood lines (`בדמו בנה את האנושות`, `אל אחד יטבחו`).
- **Review (a) fidelity:** obv. 9a `הטהור` → `[הטהור]` (the root *ku₃* restored by SEAL); obv. 9 `[האלים] הגדולים` checked (B preserves "great"); brackets of 37, 38, rev. 1, 2, 7, 18–19 set by root; SEAL lemma slips recorded (14 *ù* lemmatized as *ūmu* "day"; rev. 17 *be-lu-te* as *bēltu* instead of *bēlūtu*); SEAL's translation departures noted (1, 26, 30).
- **Review (b) consistency:** identical Akkadian = identical Hebrew checked as strings: 16 = 19 and 17 = 20 (vocalization made identical: `מה נַעֲשֶׂה?`, `מה נִבְנֶה?`), 28 = 35, 29 = 36, 38 = rev. 1, rev. 8 = 23, obv. 32–33 ~ rev. 10, rev. 18 ~ 24–25; renderings against texts 1–6, EE and Atra-ḫasīs (26 = EE VI 33 in the cohortative); four Bible quotations fixed (1 Kgs 22:19 `עֹמֵד`, a Joshua quotation removed because the checker resolves `יהושע` as Hosea, the Ezra reference to Asnapar rephrased, a bare `לְעוֹלָם` rephrased).
- **Review (c) Hebrew:** 21 `(שם)` → `(שָׁם)` (else read as `שָׂם`, the *šakānu* word); 26 `בְּדָמָם` vocalized; 34 `לָצוּר (צורתן)`; 14 `את חדקל ואת פרת –` with the object marker for the Hebrew syntax of 14–15; the purpose chain 28–41 and rev. 1–10 kept as infinitive phrases, line by line, without smoothing.
- **Checks:** `python3 -I verify.py 7` → OK (84 entries; translation = annotated `>` lines). `tools/check_bible_quotes.py` on both files → 0 problems. Reserved-word grep: Decision 16.

| rev. 21 | *kakkab šamê dārûti* | כוכבי השמים הנצחיים | `כְּכוֹכְבֵי הַשָּׁמַיִם` (בראשית כב:יז) | [divergence] | Stars as an image of permanence here, of multitude there. |
| rev. 2 | *karû* | כרי | `וְרֹב דָּגָן וְתִירֹשׁ` (בראשית כז:כח) | [equivalent] | Grain heaps; *karû* = Mishnaic `כְּרִי` [cognate]. |

| rev. 19 | *ummânu ana ummâni nuʾʾû ana nuʾʾî* | אמן לאמן, בור לבור | `מִי שָׂם פֶּה לָאָדָם אוֹ מִי יָשׂוּם אִלֵּם אוֹ חֵרֵשׁ אוֹ פִקֵּחַ אוֹ עִוֵּר` (שמות ד:יא) | [equivalent] | The kinds of men (skilled and fool) are designed by the gods; cf. text 3. |

| obv. 29 | *miṣru* | גבול | `לֹא תַסִּיג גְּבוּל רֵעֲךָ אֲשֶׁר גָּבְלוּ רִאשֹׁנִים` (דברים יט:יד) | [equivalent] | Field boundaries "for ever". |
| rev. 31 | *pirištu … mūdû mūdâ likallim* | סוד: היודע את היודע יראה | `הַנִּסְתָּרֹת לַיהוָה אֱלֹהֵינוּ; וְהַנִּגְלֹת לָנוּ וּלְבָנֵינוּ` (דברים כט:כח) | [divergence] | The account of man's creation is a "secret" for the initiated; Genesis tells it to all. |
| obv. 21 | *ilū rabûtu šūt izzizū* | האלים הגדולים אשר עמדו (שם) | `רָאִיתִי אֶת יְהוָה יֹשֵׁב עַל כִּסְאוֹ וְכָל צְבָא הַשָּׁמַיִם עֹמֵד עָלָיו` (מלכים א כב:יט) | [equivalent] | The council standing before the throne. Our proposal. |
| obv. 3, 15 | Sum. *ki du₃*, *ki ĝar*; *ukinnū* | הארץ נבנתה; לשׂום את הארץ; כוננו | `יֹצֵר הָאָרֶץ וְעֹשָׂהּ הוּא כוֹנְנָהּ לֹא תֹהוּ בְרָאָהּ לָשֶׁבֶת יְצָרָהּ` (ישעיהו מה:יח) | [equivalent] | Earth "built", "placed", "made firm" – and for dwelling. |
| colophon B 5 | *niṣirtu* | נצורת | `וּנְצֻרוֹת וְלֹא יְדַעְתָּם` (ישעיהו מח:ו) | [cognate] | Hidden things. |
| rev. 21 | *ša lā uttakkaru kakkab šamê* | אשר לא יומר – כוכבי השמים | `נֹתֵן שֶׁמֶשׁ לְאוֹר יוֹמָם חֻקֹּת יָרֵחַ וְכוֹכָבִים לְאוֹר לָיְלָה` (ירמיהו לא:לד) | [equivalent] | The unchangeable order of the stars as guarantee of a human order (Jer 31:34–35). Our proposal. |
| obv. 4, 12; rev. 18 | *uṣurāt* | צורות | `צוּרַת הַבַּיִת וּתְכוּנָתוֹ … וְכָל צוּרֹתָו` (יחזקאל מג:יא) | [cognate] | "Forms" = the design of a building. |
| obv. 29 | *miṣra ana kunni* | גבול לכונן | `גְּבוּל-שַׂמְתָּ בַּל-יַעֲבֹרוּן` (תהלים קד:ט) | [equivalent] | God sets the boundary of the waters; here man keeps the boundaries for ever. |
| obv. 30 | *tupšikku* | סל | `כַּפָּיו מִדּוּד תַּעֲבֹרְנָה` (תהלים פא:ז) | [equivalent] | The basket of servitude. |
| rev. 9 | *mê kaṣûti ana naqê* | מים קרים לנסך | `וַיִּשְׁאֲבוּ מַיִם וַיִּשְׁפְּכוּ לִפְנֵי יְהוָה` (שמואל א ז:ו) | [equivalent] | Water libation. Weak. |

### Proposals for the shared renderings
1. **Corpus §2/§3:** *iškāru* / Sum. *eš₂-gar₃* = `מִכְסָה`, next to *dullu* = `עָמָל` and *šupšikku*/*tupšikku* = `סַל`; *allu* = `מַעְדֵּר` (Akkadian, as Sum. *al*).
2. ***epēšu* cohortative = `נַעֲשֶׂה`** (always vocalized), tagged ↔ Gen 1:26; and the stative `נַעֲשָׂה` vocalized (text 6).
3. ***banû* II** (be beautiful; D *bunnû*) = `יָפֶה` / `יִפָּה`, kept apart from *banû* IV = `בָּנָה`; record the scholarly equation SIG₇ = *banû* (KAR 4 rev. 20) in the corpus file, for text 5's open decision 2.
4. ***miṣru*** = `גְּבוּל`; ***ana ūmī dārûti*** = `לִימֵי נֶצַח`; ***urra u mūša*** = `יוֹמָם וָלַיְלָה`.
5. ***karû*** = `כְּרִי` [cognate] and ***niṣirtu*** = `נְצוּרָה` [cognate] for guidelines §6.7.
6. ***šuma zakāru*** Gtn = doubled verb `תַּזְכִּיר ותזכיר` (as Adapa's *ṣiāḫu* Gtn).
7. **Names (§1):** אִילָּה־אַלָּה (open decision 2), אוּלֶּגַרָּה, אַנֶּגַרָּה, אֵן־אוּל, נִין־אוּל; Uzumua `אוּזוּמוּאָה` confirmed for KAR 4.

### Lessons for the guidelines
- **Compare the two digital transliterations of the key verbs**: SEAL and eBL disagree on A obv. 16–17 – exactly the "what shall we make? what shall we build?" lines; a third witness (B) and the Sumerian decided provisionally. Record such disagreements as open decisions (collation by photo).
- **SEAL lemma slips** again (obv. 14 *ù* = *ūmu* "day"; rev. 17 *bēltu* for *bēlūti*; obv. 21 B *rabîš* for GAL{meš}): parse the form.
- **Colophons carry catch-lines that link texts:** KAR 4 B ends with *enūma ilu a[wīlu]*, the incipit of Atra-ḫasīs – a Nineveh compilation put the creation of man from slain gods' blood immediately before the Atra-ḫasīs epic. Read colophons as data for the corpus, not as appendices.
- **School copies:** a syllable-alphabet column beside a literary text (A, B) means the text was a school exercise; eBL then catalogues the tablet as a "list" – search eBL by transliteration, not only by genre.
- **`check_bible_quotes.py` bug:** the book key drops י and ו, so `יהושע` resolves to `הושע` (Hosea); a reference like `ד:י` after any backticked word is checked as Genesis. Tool fix needed (or quote Joshua elsewhere).

## Coordinator: status, harmonization and open decisions (2026-10-17)

### Status
All seven texts are drafted, each with three review passes by its translator, and reviewed by the coordinator (spot checks of the key lines, `verify.py`, `check_bible_quotes.py`):

| N | Text | Entries | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Adapa | 175 (MB 78, SB 97) | eBL `L/1/7` |
| 2 | Enki and Ninḫursaĝa | 305 | ETCSL 1.1.1 + Ur ms. P346086 (ORACC preserves only 1–23, 49A–V, 251–268 of it) |
| 3 | Enki and Ninmaḫ | 142 | ETCSL 1.1.2; the NA bilingual version is not in our sources |
| 4 | Song of the Hoe | 110 | ETCSL 5.5.4 |
| 5 | Grain and Sheep | 193 (whole debate) | ETCSL 5.3.2 (no ORACC text) |
| 6 | Founding of Eridu (BM 93014) | 67 (obv. 40, rev. 27′) | eBL fragmentarium; no modern edition available to us |
| 7 | KAR 4 | 84 (obv. 42, rev. 32, colophon 10) | SEAL 1600, checked against eBL VAT 9307 / K 4175 |

1,076 lines in all. `translation.rtl.md` is generated by `build_translation.py` (header `build_translation--head.rtl.md`); print stub `../../הדפסה/סיפורי-הבריאה-המסופוטמיים/2-טקסטי-בריאה-ועדן.rtl.md`. The seven parallels indexes are merged into `../parallels-index.md` (by `../1-אנומה-אליש/build_parallels_index.py`, extended for project 2; key findings for project 2 written there), and the proposals for the shared renderings into `../renderings.md` §5.

### Harmonization by the coordinator
- Spelling: `שמים` (texts 3–4 had `שמיים`), `תהילה` (text 2 had `תהלה`).
- Checked across all seven texts: no `אדמה`, `טרם`, `עולם` or root עבד in any Hebrew line (the cross-text rules of `../renderings.md` §5).
- Known small inconsistency left for the user: Sum. *numun₂* is `אַסְפֶּסֶת?` in text 3 (43) and `חַלְפָה` in text 4.
- `tools/check_bible_quotes.py` fixed: the book key dropped the matres, so `יהושע` resolved as `הושע` (found by the text 6–7 translator).
- **2026-10-21, cross-corpus harmonization** (the lead's decisions on STATUS.md §6; corpus `renderings.md` §8): *mušen* = `צִפּוֹר` – Enki and Ninḫursaĝa 18 `עוף השמים … אינו אוכל` → `ציפור השמים … אינה אוכלת` (note: Ps 8:9 `צִפּוֹר שָׁמַיִם`), the Song of the Hoe 83 `עוף האל הוא` → `ציפור האל היא`, KAR 4 rev. 13 `ועופות` → `וציפורים` (*iṣṣūru* = MUŠEN, [cognate]); the Hoe 12 got the first-occurrence [equivalent] note on `סַפִּיר` (Exod 24:10, Ezek 1:26, Job 28:6); Inana `אִינַנָּה` also in the apparatus and the Hoe heading 49–51 (was `אינאנה`); Eridu rev. 19′ note on *tu₆* / *tû*; Eridu open decision 7 settled. Already in line: `סַפִּיר`, `פּוּךְ` (E&Nḫ 230), `וְזִיר`, `הַ־מֶה`, `כְּרִי`.

### Open decisions for the user (the most important; details in each text's section)
1. **Sources to buy or borrow:** Izre'el 2001 (Adapa), Attinger 1984 (Enki and Ninḫursaĝa), Ceccarelli 2016 / Klein 1997 (Enki and Ninmaḫ, incl. the bilingual), Farber 1997 (Hoe), Alster & Vanstiphout 1987 (Grain and Sheep), Lambert 2013 (Founding of Eridu and KAR 4; also Enūma eliš), Ambos 2004, Pettinato 1971. Lambert 2013 alone would cover texts 6–7 and check project 1.
2. **Enki and Ninḫursaĝa 220:** "swore an oath" (followed: *nam-erim₂ kud*) or the traditional "cursed".
3. **Grain and Sheep 27, 39:** grain and sheep "dwelt" with the gods (ETCSL's lemma, followed) or were "created" (ETCSL's translation); KAR 4 rev. 20 (SIG₇ = *banû*) bears on it.
4. **Enki and Ninmaḫ:** add the biblical disability word in parentheses (`(= עִוֵּר)`) or keep the Sumerian description only (followed); *tiru* `אִישׁ־חָצֵר` or `סָרִיס`.
5. **Adapa MB 75′:** "you shall not live!" as a statement (followed) or Foster's question; MB 15′ and 58′ readings.
6. **Song of the Hoe:** Uzuea (edition, followed) or the familiar Uzumua in the lines; *ki* `הארץ` (followed) or `קרקע`.
7. **KAR 4:** the slain gods' name (Illa-Alla, followed; Alla; Lamga); obv. 11 "repeated" (SEAL) or "took counsel" (eBL emendation: decides whether the text has a deliberating council); obv. 16–17 to be collated on the photo.
8. **Corpus-wide:** *ṭeḫû* (`נִגַּשׁ` here, `קָרַב` in Enūma eliš); *šipru* (`פֹּעַל` / `מְלָאכָה`, project 1); the `{שומרית}` tag for Sumerian-only lines of bilinguals.

### Not done / skipped
- The Sumerian Adapa (Meturan, Nippur) and the NA bilingual Enki and Ninmaḫ: not in eBL/ETCSL/ORACC as we have them.
- The Ur manuscript of Enki and Ninḫursaĝa could be compared only where ORACC has it (not for the water, the plants or the oath).
